       EN: The Lord of hosts has sworn, saying: Surely, as I have thought, so it will be, and as I have planned in My mind,

14:25  LA: sic eveniet : ut conteram Assyrium in terra mea, et in montibus meis conculcem eum ; et auferetur ab eis jugum ejus, et onus illius ab humero eorum tolletur.
       EN: so it will happen: I will crush the Assyrian in My land and trample him on My mountains. His yoke will be removed from them, and his burden lifted from their shoulders.

14:26  LA: Hoc consilium quod cogitavi super omnem terram ; et hæc est manus extenta super universas gentes.
       EN: This is the plan I have devised concerning the whole earth, and this is the hand outstretched over all the nations.

14:27  LA: Dominus enim exercituum decrevit ; et quis poterit infirmare ? et manus ejus extenta ; et quis avertet eam ?]
       EN: For the Lord of hosts has decreed it; who can annul it? His hand is outstretched; who will turn it back?

14:28  LA: In anno quo mortuus est rex Achaz, factum est onus istud :
       EN: In the year when King Ahaz died, this burden came:

14:29  LA: [Ne lætaris, Philisthæa omnis tu, quoniam comminuta est virga percussoris tui ; de radice enim colubri egredietur regulus, et semen ejus absorbens volucrem.
       EN: Do not rejoice, all you Philistia, because the rod of the one who struck you has been broken. For from the serpent's root a basilisk will come forth, and its offspring will swallow a bird.

14:30  LA: Et pascentur primogeniti pauperum, et pauperes fiducialiter requiescent ; et interire faciam in fame radicem tuam, et reliquias tuas interficiam.
       EN: The firstborn of the poor will graze, and the poor will rest securely. I will cause your root to perish by hunger, and I will kill your remnant.

14:31  LA: Ulula, porta ; clama civitas ; prostrata est Philisthæa omnis ; ab aquilone enim fumus veniet, et non est qui effugiet agmen ejus.
       EN: Wail, gate! Cry out, city! All Philistia has been laid low. For smoke will come from the north, and no one will escape its host.

14:32  LA: Et quid respondebitur nuntiis gentis ? Quia Dominus fundavit Sion, et in ipso sperabunt pauperes populi ejus.]
       EN: What answer will be given to the nation's messengers? That the Lord has founded Zion, and the poor of His people will hope in Him.

ISAIAH 15

15:1  LA: Onus Moab. [Quia nocte vastata est Ar Moab, conticuit ; quia nocte vastatus est murus Moab, conticuit.
      EN: The burden concerning Moab. For in the night Ar of Moab has been devastated and silenced; in the night the wall of Moab has been devastated and silenced.

15:2  LA: Ascendit domus, et Dibon ad excelsa, in planctum super Nabo ; et super Medaba, Moab ululavit ; in cunctis capitibus ejus calvitium, et omnis barba radetur.
      EN: The household and Dibon have gone up to the high places to mourn over Nebo. Over Medeba Moab has wailed; every head is bald, and every beard will be shaved.

15:3  LA: In triviis ejus accincti sunt sacco ; super tecta ejus et in plateis ejus omnis ululatus descendit in fletum.
      EN: In its crossroads they are clothed with sackcloth; on its rooftops and in its streets every wail sinks into weeping.

15:4  LA: Clamabit Hesebon et Eleale, usque Jasa audita est vox eorum ; super hoc expediti Moab ululabunt, anima ejus ululabit sibi.
      EN: Heshbon and Elealeh will cry out; their voice has been heard as far as Jahaz. Therefore Moab's armed men will wail; its soul will wail within it.

15:5  LA: Cor meum ad Moab clamabit ; vectes ejus usque ad Segor, vitulam conternantem ; per ascensum enim Luith flens ascendet, et in via Oronaim clamorem contritionis levabunt.
      EN: My heart will cry out for Moab. Its bars reach as far as Zoar, a three-year-old heifer. For he will go up the ascent of Luhith weeping, and on the road to Horonaim they will raise a cry of devastation.

15:6  LA: Aquæ enim Nemrim desertæ erunt, quia aruit herba, defecit germen, viror omnis interiit.
      EN: For the waters of Nimrim will be desolate, because the grass has dried up, the growth has failed, and all greenery has perished.

15:7  LA: Secundum magnitudinem operis, et visitatio eorum : ad torrentem Salicum ducent eos.
      EN: In proportion to the greatness of their deeds will be their visitation; they will lead them to the torrent of Willows.

15:8  LA: Quoniam circuivit clamor terminum Moab ; usque ad Gallim ululatus ejus, et usque ad puteum Elim clamor ejus.
      EN: For the cry has gone around Moab's border, its wailing as far as Gallim, its cry as far as the well of Elim.

15:9  LA: Quia aquæ Dibon repletæ sunt sanguine ; ponam enim super Dibon additamenta ; his qui fugerint de Moab leonem, et reliquiis terræ.]
      EN: For Dibon's waters are filled with blood. I will bring still more upon Dibon: a lion upon those who escape from Moab and upon the remnant of the land.

ISAIAH 16

16:1  LA: Emitte agnum, Domine, dominatorem terræ, de petra deserti ad montem filiæ Sion.
      EN: Send forth the lamb, Lord, the ruler of the land, from the rock of the wilderness to the mountain of daughter Zion.

16:2  LA: Et erit : sicut avis fugiens, et pulli de nido avolantes, sic erunt filiæ Moab in transcensu Arnon.
      EN: And this will happen: like a fleeing bird and fledglings flying from the nest, so the daughters of Moab will be at the crossing of the Arnon.

16:3  LA: Ini consilium, coge concilium ; pone quasi noctem umbram tuam in meridie ; absconde fugientes, et vagos ne prodas.
      EN: Form a plan; summon a council. Make your shadow like night at midday. Hide the fugitives, and do not betray the wanderers.

16:4  LA: Habitabunt apud te profugi mei ; Moab, esto latibulum eorum a facie vastatoris : finitus est enim pulvis, consummatus est miser, defecit qui conculcabat terram.
      EN: My fugitives will dwell with you. Moab, be their hiding place from the devastator; for the dust has come to an end, the wretched one is finished, and the one who trampled the land has ceased.

16:5  LA: Et præparabitur in misericordia solium, et sedebit super illud in veritate in tabernaculo David, judicans et quærens judicium, et velociter reddens quod justum est.
      EN: A throne will be established in mercy, and one will sit upon it in truth, in David's tent, judging and seeking judgment and swiftly rendering what is just.

16:6  LA: Audivimus superbiam Moab : superbus est valde ; superbia ejus, et arrogantia ejus, et indignatio ejus plus quam fortitudo ejus.
      EN: We have heard of Moab's pride—he is very proud. His pride, arrogance, and indignation exceed his strength.

16:7  LA: Idcirco ululabit Moab ad Moab ; universus ululabit : his qui lætantur super muros cocti lateris, loquimini plagas suas.
      EN: Therefore Moab will wail to Moab; everyone will wail. To those who rejoice over walls of baked brick, speak of their wounds.

16:8  LA: Quoniam suburbana Hesebon deserta sunt, et vineam Sabama domini gentium exciderunt : flagella ejus usque ad Jazer pervenerunt, erraverunt in deserto ; propagines ejus relictæ sunt, transierunt mare.
      EN: For the outskirts of Heshbon are deserted, and the lords of the nations have cut down the vine of Sibmah. Its shoots reached as far as Jazer and wandered into the wilderness; its branches were left behind, they crossed the sea.

16:9  LA: Super hoc plorabo in fletu Jazer vineam Sabama ; inebriabo de lacrima mea, Hesebon et Eleale, quoniam super vindemiam tuam et super messem tuam vox calcantium irruit.
      EN: Therefore with Jazer's weeping I will weep for the vine of Sibmah. Heshbon and Elealeh, I will drench you with my tears, because the cry of those who trample has fallen upon your vintage and your harvest.

16:10  LA: Et auferetur lætitia et exsultatio de Carmelo, et in vineis non exsultabit neque jubilabit. Vinum in torculari non calcabit qui calcare consueverat ; vocem calcantium abstuli.
       EN: Joy and exultation will be taken away from Carmel, and no one will exult or shout in the vineyards. The one who used to tread wine in the press will no longer tread it; I have taken away the cry of those who tread.

16:11  LA: Super hoc venter meus ad Moab quasi cithara sonabit, et viscera mea ad murum cocti lateris.
       EN: Therefore my belly will resound like a harp for Moab, and my inward parts for the wall of baked brick.

16:12  LA: Et erit : cum apparuerit quod laboravit Moab super excelsis suis, ingredietur ad sancta sua ut obsecret, et non valebit.]
       EN: When it becomes clear that Moab has wearied himself upon his high places, he will enter his sanctuaries to plead, but he will not prevail.

16:13  LA: Hoc verbum quod locutus est Dominus ad Moab ex tunc.
       EN: This is the word that the Lord spoke concerning Moab formerly.

16:14  LA: Et nunc locutus est Dominus, dicens : In tribus annis, quasi anni mercenarii, auferetur gloria Moab super omni populo multo, et relinquetur parvus et modicus, nequaquam multus.
       EN: And now the Lord has spoken, saying: Within three years, like the years of a hired worker, Moab's glory will be taken away, despite all its great population; what remains will be small and slight, by no means numerous.

ISAIAH 17

17:1  LA: Onus Damasci. [Ecce Damascus desinet esse civitas, et erit sicut acervus lapidum in ruina.
      EN: The burden concerning Damascus. Behold, Damascus will cease to be a city and become like a heap of stones in a ruin.

17:2  LA: Derelictæ civitates Aroër gregibus erunt, et requiescent ibi, et non erit qui exterreat.
      EN: The abandoned cities of Aroer will belong to flocks; they will rest there, with no one to frighten them.

17:3  LA: Et cessabit adjutorium ab Ephraim, et regnum a Damasco ; et reliquiæ Syriæ sicut gloria filiorum Israël erunt, dicit Dominus exercituum.
      EN: Help will cease from Ephraim and kingship from Damascus; the remnant of Syria will be like the glory of the children of Israel, says the Lord of hosts.

17:4  LA: Et erit in die illa : attenuabitur gloria Jacob, et pinguedo carnis ejus marcescet.
      EN: On that day Jacob's glory will dwindle, and the fatness of his flesh will waste away.

17:5  LA: Et erit sicut congregans in messe quod restiterit, et brachium ejus spicas leget ; et erit sicut quærens spicas in valle Raphaim.
      EN: It will be like someone gathering what is left at harvest, whose arm gleans the ears of grain; it will be like someone searching for ears in the valley of Rephaim.

17:6  LA: Et relinquetur in eo sicut racemus et sicut excussio oleæ duarum vel trium olivarum in summitate rami, sive quatuor aut quinque in cacuminibus ejus fructus ejus, dicit Dominus Deus Israël.
      EN: What remains in it will be like a cluster and like the beating of an olive tree: two or three olives at the top of a branch, or four or five on its upper limbs as its fruit, says the Lord, the God of Israel.

17:7  LA: In die illa inclinabitur homo ad factorem suum, et oculi ejus ad Sanctum Israël respicient ;
      EN: On that day a person will turn toward his Maker, and his eyes will look to the Holy One of Israel.

17:8  LA: et non inclinabitur ad altaria quæ fecerunt manus ejus ; et quæ operati sunt digiti ejus non respiciet lucos et delubra.
      EN: He will not turn toward the altars his hands have made or look to what his fingers have fashioned—the sacred groves and shrines.

17:9  LA: In die illa erunt civitates fortitudinis ejus derelictæ sicut aratra, et segetes quæ derelictæ sunt a facie filiorum Israël ; et eris deserta.
      EN: On that day his strong cities will be abandoned like the plows and crops abandoned before the children of Israel, and you will be desolate.

17:10  LA: Quia oblitus es Dei salvatoris tui, et fortis adjutoris tui non es recordata : propterea plantabis plantationem fidelem, et germen alienum seminabis ;
       EN: For you have forgotten God your savior and have not remembered your mighty helper. Therefore you will plant a trustworthy planting and sow a foreign shoot;

17:11  LA: in die plantationis tuæ labrusca, et mane semen tuum florebit ; ablata est messis in die hæreditatis, et dolebit graviter.
       EN: on the day of your planting there will be a wild vine, and in the morning your seed will blossom. The harvest has been taken away on the day of inheritance, and there will be grievous pain.

17:12  LA: Væ multitudini populorum multorum, ut multitudo maris sonantis ; et tumultus turbarum, sicut sonitus aquarum multarum.
       EN: Woe to the multitude of many peoples, like the multitude of the roaring sea, and the tumult of crowds, like the sound of many waters!

17:13  LA: Sonabunt populi sicut sonitus aquarum inundantium, et increpabit eum, et fugiet procul ; et rapietur sicut pulvis montium a facie venti, et sicut turbo coram tempestate.
       EN: Peoples will roar like the sound of flooding waters; He will rebuke it, and it will flee far away. It will be swept off like dust on the mountains before the wind, like a whirlwind before a storm.

17:14  LA: In tempore vespere, et ecce turbatio ; in matutino, et non subsistet. Hæc est pars eorum qui vastaverunt nos, et sors diripientium nos.]
       EN: At evening, behold, turmoil; by morning it will be no more. This is the portion of those who ravaged us, the lot of those who plunder us.

ISAIAH 18

18:1  LA: Væ terræ cymbalo alarum, quæ est trans flumina Æthiopiæ,
      EN: Woe to the land of clanging wings, which lies beyond the rivers of Ethiopia,

18:2  LA: qui mittit in mare legatos, et in vasis papyri super aquas. Ite, angeli veloces, ad gentem convulsam et dilaceratam ; ad populum terribilem, post quem non est alius ; ad gentem exspectantem et conculcatam, cujus diripuerunt flumina terram ejus.
      EN: which sends envoys on the sea and in papyrus vessels over the waters. Go, swift messengers, to a nation torn and mangled, to a fearsome people beyond which there is no other, to a nation waiting and trampled, whose land the rivers have plundered.

18:3  LA: Omnes habitatores orbis, qui moramini in terra, cum elevatum fuerit signum in montibus, videbitis, et clangorem tubæ audietis.
      EN: All inhabitants of the world who dwell on the earth, when a signal is raised on the mountains, you will see it, and you will hear the trumpet's blast.

18:4  LA: Quia hæc dicit Dominus ad me : Quiescam et considerabo in loco meo, sicut meridiana lux clara est, et sicut nubes roris in die messis.
      EN: For thus the Lord says to me: I will rest and watch in My place, like the clear light of midday and like a cloud of dew on a harvest day.

18:5  LA: Ante messem enim totus effloruit, et immatura perfectio germinabit ; et præcidentur ramusculi ejus falcibus, et quæ derelicta fuerint abscindentur et excutientur.
      EN: For before the harvest it has all blossomed, and unripe fullness will bud. Its little branches will be cut off with pruning hooks, and what remains will be cut away and shaken off.

18:6  LA: Et relinquentur simul avibus montium et bestiis terræ ; et æstate perpetua erunt super eum volucres, et omnes bestiæ terræ super illum hiemabunt.
      EN: They will be left together to the birds of the mountains and the beasts of the earth. The birds will spend the whole summer upon it, and all the beasts of the earth will winter upon it.

18:7  LA: In tempore illo deferetur munus Domino exercituum a populo divulso et dilacerato, a populo terribili, post quem non fuit alius ; a gente exspectante, exspectante et conculcata, cujus diripuerunt flumina terram ejus ; ad locum nominis Domini exercituum, montem Sion.]
      EN: At that time a gift will be brought to the Lord of hosts from a people torn and mangled, from a fearsome people beyond which there was no other, from a nation waiting, waiting and trampled, whose land the rivers have plundered, to the place of the name of the Lord of hosts, Mount Zion.

ISAIAH 19

19:1  LA: Onus Ægypti. [Ecce Dominus ascendet super nubem levem, et ingredietur Ægyptum, et commovebuntur simulacra Ægypti a facie ejus, et cor Ægypti tabescet in medio ejus,
      EN: The burden concerning Egypt. Behold, the Lord will mount a light cloud and enter Egypt. Egypt's images will tremble before Him, and Egypt's heart will melt within it.

19:2  LA: et concurrere faciam Ægyptios adversus Ægyptios ; et pugnabit vir contra fratrem suum, et vir contra amicum suum, civitas adversus civitatem, regnum adversus regnum.
      EN: I will make Egyptians clash with Egyptians; a man will fight his brother and a man his friend, city against city, kingdom against kingdom.

19:3  LA: Et dirumpetur spiritus Ægypti in visceribus ejus, et consilium ejus præcipitabo ; et interrogabunt simulacra sua, et divinos suos, et pythones, et ariolos.
      EN: Egypt's spirit will be broken within it, and I will overthrow its counsel. They will consult their images and diviners, their mediums and soothsayers.

19:4  LA: Et tradam Ægyptum in manu dominorum crudelium, et rex fortis dominabitur eorum, ait Dominus Deus exercituum.
      EN: I will deliver Egypt into the hand of cruel masters, and a mighty king will rule over them, says the Lord, the God of hosts.

19:5  LA: Et arescet aqua de mari, et fluvius desolabitur atque siccabitur.
      EN: Water will dry up from the sea, and the river will be laid waste and become dry.

19:6  LA: Et deficient flumina, attenuabuntur et siccabuntur rivi aggerum, calamus et juncus marcescet.
      EN: The rivers will fail; the streams along the embankments will dwindle and dry up, and reed and rush will wither.

19:7  LA: Nudabitur alveus rivi a fonte suo, et omnis sementis irrigua siccabitur, arescet, et non erit.
      EN: The riverbed will be laid bare from its source, and every irrigated crop will dry up, wither, and cease to be.

19:8  LA: Et mœrebunt piscatores, et lugebunt omnes mittentes in flumen hamum ; et expandentes rete super faciem aquarum emarcescent.
      EN: The fishermen will mourn, and all who cast a hook into the river will lament; those who spread a net over the waters' surface will waste away.

19:9  LA: Confundentur qui operabantur linum, pectentes et texentes subtilia.
      EN: Those who worked flax, combing and weaving fine fabrics, will be put to shame.

19:10  LA: Et erunt irrigua ejus flaccentia : omnes qui faciebant lacunas ad capiendos pisces.
       EN: Its watered places will fail, and all who made pools for catching fish.

19:11  LA: Stulti principes Taneos, sapientes consiliarii Pharaonis dederunt consilium insipiens. Quomodo dicetis Pharaoni : Filius sapientium ego, filius regum antiquorum ?
       EN: The rulers of Tanis are fools; Pharaoh's wise counselors have given foolish advice. How will you say to Pharaoh: I am a son of wise men, a son of ancient kings?

19:12  LA: Ubi nunc sunt sapientes tui ? annuntient tibi, et indicent quid cogitaverit Dominus exercituum super Ægyptum.
       EN: Where are your wise men now? Let them announce to you and make known what the Lord of hosts has planned concerning Egypt.

19:13  LA: Stulti facti sunt principes Taneos, emarcuerunt principes Mempheos ; deceperunt Ægyptum, angulum populorum ejus.
       EN: The rulers of Tanis have become fools; the rulers of Memphis have wasted away. They have deceived Egypt, the cornerstone of its peoples.

19:14  LA: Dominus miscuit in medio ejus spiritum vertiginis ; et errare fecerunt Ægyptum in omni opere suo, sicut errat ebrius et vomens.
       EN: The Lord has mingled a spirit of dizziness in its midst. They have made Egypt wander in all its work, as a drunken man wanders and vomits.

19:15  LA: Et non erit Ægypto opus quod faciat caput et caudam, incurvantem et refrenantem.
       EN: Egypt will have no work that produces head and tail, one who bends down and one who restrains.

19:16  LA: In die illa erit Ægyptus quasi mulieres ; et stupebunt, et timebunt a facie commotionis manus Domini exercituum, quam ipse movebit super eam.
       EN: On that day Egypt will be like women; they will be astonished and afraid before the shaking hand of the Lord of hosts, which He will shake over it.

19:17  LA: Et erit terra Juda Ægypto in pavorem ; omnis qui illius fuerit recordatus pavebit a facie consilii Domini exercituum, quod ipse cogitavit super eam.
       EN: The land of Judah will become a terror to Egypt. Everyone who remembers it will dread the plan that the Lord of hosts Himself has devised against it.

19:18  LA: In die illa erunt quinque civitates in terra Ægypti loquentes lingua Chanaan, et jurantes per Dominum exercituum : Civitas solis vocabitur una.
       EN: On that day there will be five cities in the land of Egypt speaking the language of Canaan and swearing by the Lord of hosts; one will be called the City of the Sun.

19:19  LA: In die illa erit altare Domini in medio terræ Ægypti, et titulus Domini juxta terminum ejus.
       EN: On that day there will be an altar to the Lord in the midst of the land of Egypt, and a pillar to the Lord beside its border.

19:20  LA: Erit in signum et in testimonium Domino exercituum in terra Ægypti ; clamabunt enim ad Dominum a facie tribulationis, et mittet eis salvatorem et propugnatorem qui liberet eos.
       EN: It will be a sign and a testimony to the Lord of hosts in the land of Egypt. For they will cry out to the Lord in distress, and He will send them a savior and defender to deliver them.

19:21  LA: Et cognoscetur Dominus ab Ægypto, et cognoscent Ægyptii Dominum in die illa ; et colent eum in hostiis et in muneribus ; et vota vovebunt Domino, et solvent.
       EN: The Lord will be known by Egypt, and the Egyptians will know the Lord on that day. They will worship Him with sacrifices and gifts; they will make vows to the Lord and fulfill them.

19:22  LA: Et percutiet Dominus Ægyptum plaga, et sanabit eam ; et revertentur ad Dominum, et placabitur eis, et sanabit eos.
       EN: The Lord will strike Egypt with a wound and heal it. They will return to the Lord, and He will be reconciled to them and heal them.

19:23  LA: In die illa erit via de Ægypto in Assyrios ; et intrabit Assyrius Ægyptum, et Ægyptius in Assyrios, et servient Ægyptii Assur.
       EN: On that day there will be a road from Egypt to the Assyrians. The Assyrian will enter Egypt and the Egyptian the land of the Assyrians, and the Egyptians will serve Assyria.

19:24  LA: In die illa erit Israël tertius Ægyptio et Assyrio ; benedictio in medio terræ
       EN: On that day Israel will be a third alongside the Egyptian and the Assyrian, a blessing in the midst of the land,

19:25  LA: cui benedixit Dominus exercituum, dicens : Benedictus populus meus Ægypti, et opus manuum mearum Assyrio ; hæreditas autem mea Israël.]
       EN: which the Lord of hosts has blessed, saying: Blessed be My people of Egypt, and the work of My hands among the Assyrians; but Israel is My inheritance.

ISAIAH 20

20:1  LA: In anno quo ingressus est Thathan in Azotum, cum misisset eum Sargon, rex Assyriorum, et pugnasset contra Azotum, et cepisset eam :
      EN: In the year when Tartan entered Ashdod, after Sargon king of the Assyrians had sent him, and he fought against Ashdod and captured it,

20:2  LA: in tempore illo locutus est Dominus in manu Isaiæ, filii Amos, dicens : Vade, et solve saccum de lumbis tuis, et calceamenta tua tolle de pedibus tuis. Et fecit sic, vadens nudus et discalceatus.
      EN: at that time the Lord spoke through Isaiah son of Amoz, saying: Go, loosen the sackcloth from your waist and remove your shoes from your feet. He did so, walking naked and barefoot.

20:3  LA: Et dixit Dominus : [Sicut ambulavit servus meus Isaias nudus et discalceatus, trium annorum signum et portentum erit super Ægyptum et super Æthiopiam ;
      EN: The Lord said: Just as My servant Isaiah has walked naked and barefoot, this will be a sign and a portent of three years concerning Egypt and Ethiopia.

20:4  LA: sic minabit rex Assyriorum captivitatem Ægypti, et transmigrationem Æthiopiæ, juvenum et senum, nudam et discalceatam, discoopertis natibus, ad ignominiam Ægypti.
      EN: So the king of the Assyrians will drive away Egypt's captives and Ethiopia's exiles, young and old, naked and barefoot, with their buttocks uncovered, to Egypt's shame.

20:5  LA: Et timebunt, et confundentur ab Æthiopia spe sua, et ab Ægypto gloria sua.
      EN: They will be afraid and ashamed of Ethiopia their hope and Egypt their glory.

20:6  LA: Et dicet habitator insulæ hujus in die illa : Ecce hæc erat spes nostra, ad quos confugimus in auxilium, ut liberarent nos a facie regis Assyriorum : et quomodo effugere poterimus nos ?]
      EN: On that day the inhabitant of this island will say: Behold, this was our hope, those to whom we fled for help to deliver us from the king of the Assyrians! How shall we ourselves escape?

ISAIAH 21

21:1  LA: Onus deserti maris. [Sicut turbines ab africo veniunt, de deserto venit, de terra horribili.
      EN: The burden concerning the wilderness of the sea. As whirlwinds come from the southwest, it comes from the wilderness, from a dreadful land.

21:2  LA: Visio dura nuntiata est mihi : qui incredulus est infideliter agit ; et qui depopulator est vastat. Ascende, Ælam ; obside, Mede ; omnem gemitum ejus cessare feci.
      EN: A harsh vision has been announced to me: the faithless acts faithlessly, and the plunderer devastates. Go up, Elam; lay siege, Mede. I have put an end to all its groaning.

21:3  LA: Propterea repleti sunt lumbi mei dolore ; angustia possedit me sicut angustia parturientis ; corrui cum audirem, conturbatus sum cum viderem.
      EN: Therefore my loins are filled with pain; anguish has seized me like the anguish of a woman in labor. I collapsed when I heard; I was troubled when I saw.

21:4  LA: Emarcuit cor meum ; tenebræ stupefecerunt me : Babylon dilecta mea posita est mihi in miraculum.
      EN: My heart has withered; darkness has stunned me. My beloved Babylon has become an astonishment to me.

21:5  LA: Pone mensam, contemplare in specula comedentes et bibentes : surgite, principes, arripite clypeum.
      EN: Set the table; watch from the lookout those who eat and drink. Rise, rulers; seize the shield.

21:6  LA: Hæc enim dixit mihi Dominus : Vade, et pone speculatorem, et quodcumque viderit annuntiet.
      EN: For thus the Lord said to me: Go and station a watchman, and let him report whatever he sees.

21:7  LA: Et vidit currum duorum equitum, ascensorem asini, et ascensorem cameli ; et contemplatus est diligenter multo intuitu.
      EN: He saw a chariot with two horsemen, a donkey rider, and a camel rider, and he watched carefully with close attention.

21:8  LA: Et clamavit leo : Super speculam Domini ego sum, stans jugiter per diem ; et super custodiam meam ego sum, stans totis noctibus.
      EN: And a lion cried out: I am upon the Lord's watchtower, standing continually by day; I am at my watch, standing through all the nights.

21:9  LA: Ecce iste venit ascensor vir bigæ equitum ; et respondit, et dixit : Cecidit, cecidit Babylon, et omnia sculptilia deorum ejus contrita sunt in terram.
      EN: Behold, here comes a man riding in a two-horse chariot. He answered and said: Fallen, fallen is Babylon, and all the carved images of its gods have been crushed to the ground.

21:10  LA: Tritura mea et filii areæ meæ, quæ audivi a Domino exercituum, Deo Israël, annuntiavi vobis.]
       EN: My threshing, and children of my threshing floor, what I have heard from the Lord of hosts, the God of Israel, I have announced to you.

21:11  LA: Onus Duma. [Ad me clamat ex Seir : Custos, quid de nocte ? custos, quid de nocte ?
       EN: The burden concerning Dumah. He calls to me from Seir: Watchman, what of the night? Watchman, what of the night?

21:12  LA: Dixit custos : Venit mane et nox ; si quæritis, quærite ; convertimini, venite.]
       EN: The watchman said: Morning comes, and night. If you inquire, inquire; turn back, come.

21:13  LA: Onus in Arabia. [In saltu ad vesperam dormietis, in semitis Dedanim.
       EN: The burden concerning Arabia. In the forest at evening you will sleep, on the paths of the Dedanites.

21:14  LA: Occurrentes sitienti ferte aquam, qui habitatis terram austri ; cum panibus occurrite fugienti.
       EN: Bring water to meet the thirsty, you who dwell in the southern land; meet the fugitive with bread.

21:15  LA: A facie enim gladiorum fugerunt, a facie gladii imminentis, a facie arcus extenti, a facie gravis prælii.
       EN: For they have fled from swords, from the threatening sword, from the drawn bow, from the weight of battle.

21:16  LA: Quoniam hæc dicit Dominus ad me : Adhuc in uno anno, quasi in anno mercenarii, et auferetur omnis gloria Cedar.
       EN: For thus the Lord says to me: Within one more year, like the year of a hired worker, all Kedar's glory will be taken away.

21:17  LA: Et reliquiæ numeri sagittariorum fortium de filiis Cedar imminuentur ; Dominus enim Deus Israël locutus est.]
       EN: The remaining number of mighty archers among the children of Kedar will dwindle, for the Lord, the God of Israel, has spoken.

ISAIAH 22

22:1  LA: Onus vallis Visionis. [Quidnam quoque tibi est, quia ascendisti et tu omnis in tecta ?
      EN: The burden concerning the Valley of Vision. What is the matter with you, that you too have all gone up onto the rooftops?

22:2  LA: Clamoris plena, urbs frequens, civitas exsultans ; interfecti tui, non interfecti gladio, nec mortui in bello.
      EN: You are full of shouting, a crowded town, an exultant city. Your slain were not slain by the sword or killed in war.

22:3  LA: Cuncti principes tui fugerunt simul dureque ligati sunt ; omnes qui inventi sunt vincti sunt pariter ; procul fugerunt.
      EN: All your rulers fled together and were bound fast; all who were found were bound together, though they had fled far away.

22:4  LA: Propterea dixi : Recedite a me : amare flebo ; nolite incumbere ut consolemini me super vastitate filiæ populi mei ;
      EN: Therefore I said: Turn away from me; I will weep bitterly. Do not press to comfort me over the devastation of the daughter of my people.

22:5  LA: dies enim interfectionis, et conculcationis, et fletuum, Domino Deo exercituum, in valle Visionis, scrutans murum, et magnificus super montem.
      EN: For the Lord, the God of hosts, has a day of slaughter and trampling and weeping in the Valley of Vision, searching out the wall and looming great over the mountain.

22:6  LA: Et Ælam sumpsit pharetram, currum hominis equitis, et parietem nudavit clypeus.
      EN: Elam took up the quiver, the chariot of a mounted man, and the shield laid bare the wall.

22:7  LA: Et erunt electæ valles tuæ plenæ quadrigarum, et equites ponent sedes suas in porta.
      EN: Your choicest valleys will be filled with chariots, and horsemen will establish their positions at the gate.

22:8  LA: Et revelabitur operimentum Judæ, et videbis in die illa armamentarium domus saltus.
      EN: Judah's covering will be exposed, and on that day you will see the armory of the House of the Forest.

22:9  LA: Et scissuras civitatis David videbitis, quia multiplicatæ sunt ; et congregastis aquas piscinæ inferioris,
      EN: You will see that the breaches in the city of David are many, and you have collected the waters of the lower pool.

22:10  LA: et domos Jerusalem numerastis, et destruxistis domos ad muniendum murum.
       EN: You have counted Jerusalem's houses and demolished houses to fortify the wall.

22:11  LA: Et lacum fecistis inter duos muros ad aquam piscinæ veteris ; et non suspexistis ad eum qui fecerat eam, et operatorem ejus de longe non vidistis.
       EN: You made a reservoir between the two walls for the water of the old pool, but you did not look up to the One who made it or see the One who fashioned it long ago.

22:12  LA: Et vocabit Dominus Deus exercituum in die illa ad fletum, et ad planctum, ad calvitium, et ad cingulum sacci ;
       EN: On that day the Lord, the God of hosts, will call for weeping and lamentation, for baldness and a girding of sackcloth;

22:13  LA: et ecce gaudium et lætitia, occidere vitulos et jugulare arietes, comedere carnes, et bibere vinum : comedamus et bibamus, cras enim moriemur.
       EN: but behold, joy and gladness, slaughtering calves and killing rams, eating meat and drinking wine: Let us eat and drink, for tomorrow we will die.

22:14  LA: Et revelata est in auribus meis vox Domini exercituum : Si dimittetur iniquitas hæc vobis donec moriamini, dicit Dominus Deus exercituum.]
       EN: The voice of the Lord of hosts was revealed in my ears: Surely this iniquity will not be forgiven you until you die, says the Lord, the God of hosts.

22:15  LA: [Hæc dicit Dominus Deus exercituum : Vade, ingredere ad eum qui habitat in tabernaculo, ad Sobnam, præpositum templi, et dices ad eum :
       EN: Thus says the Lord, the God of hosts: Go, enter the dwelling of the man who lives in the tent, Shebna, the superintendent of the temple, and say to him:

22:16  LA: Quid tu hic, aut quasi quis hic ? quia excidisti tibi hic sepulchrum, excidisti in excelso memoriale diligenter, in petra tabernaculum tibi.
       EN: What have you here, or as whom are you here, that you have hewn yourself a tomb here, carefully hewn a memorial on high, a dwelling for yourself in the rock?

22:17  LA: Ecce Dominus asportari te faciet, sicut asportatur gallus gallinaceus ; et quasi amictum, sic sublevabit te.
       EN: Behold, the Lord will have you carried away as a rooster is carried away; He will lift you up like a garment.

22:18  LA: Coronas coronabit te tribulatione ; quasi pilam mittet te in terram latam et spatiosam ; ibi morieris, et ibi erit currus gloriæ tuæ, ignominia domus domini tui.
       EN: He will crown you with a crown of affliction; He will throw you like a ball into a broad and spacious land. There you will die, and there will be your glorious chariot, you disgrace to your master's house.

22:19  LA: Et expellam te de statione tua, et de ministerio tuo deponam te.
       EN: I will drive you from your station and remove you from your office.

22:20  LA: Et erit in die illa : vocabo servum meum Eliacim, filium Helciæ,
       EN: On that day I will summon My servant Eliakim son of Hilkiah.

22:21  LA: et induam illum tunica tua, et cingulo tuo confortabo eum, et potestatem tuam dabo in manu ejus ; et erit quasi pater habitantibus Jerusalem et domui Juda.
       EN: I will clothe him in your tunic and strengthen him with your girdle, and I will put your authority into his hand. He will be like a father to the inhabitants of Jerusalem and to the house of Judah.

22:22  LA: Et dabo clavem domus David super humerum ejus ; et aperiet, et non erit qui claudat ; et claudet, et non erit qui aperiat.
       EN: I will place the key of the house of David upon his shoulder. He will open, and no one will shut; he will shut, and no one will open.

22:23  LA: Et figam illum paxillum in loco fideli, et erit in solium gloriæ domui patris ejus.
       EN: I will fasten him like a peg in a secure place, and he will become a throne of glory to his father's house.

22:24  LA: Et suspendent super eum omnem gloriam domus patris ejus ; vasorum diversa genera, omne vas parvulum, a vasis craterarum usque ad omne vas musicorum.
       EN: They will hang upon him all the glory of his father's house: vessels of various kinds, every small vessel, from the vessels used as bowls to every instrument of musicians.

22:25  LA: In die illa, dicit Dominus exercituum, auferetur paxillus qui fixus fuerat in loco fideli, et frangetur, et cadet, et peribit quod pependerat in eo, quia Dominus locutus est.]
       EN: On that day, says the Lord of hosts, the peg that had been fastened in a secure place will be removed; it will break and fall, and what hung upon it will perish, for the Lord has spoken.

ISAIAH 23

23:1  LA: Onus Tyri. [Ululate, naves maris, quia vastata est domus unde venire consueverant : de terra Cethim revelatum est eis.
      EN: The burden concerning Tyre. Wail, ships of the sea, for the house from which they used to come has been devastated; from the land of Kittim it has been revealed to them.

23:2  LA: Tacete, qui habitatis in insula ; negotiatores Sidonis, transfretantes mare, repleverunt te.
      EN: Be silent, you who dwell on the island. Sidon's merchants, crossing the sea, have filled you.

23:3  LA: In aquis multis semen Nili ; messis fluminis fruges ejus : et facta est negotiatio gentium.
      EN: On the many waters is the seed of the Nile; the river's harvest is its produce, and it has become the marketplace of nations.

23:4  LA: Erubesce, Sidon ; ait enim mare, fortitudo maris, dicens : Non parturivi, et non peperi, et non enutrivi juvenes, nec ad incrementum perduxi virgines.
      EN: Be ashamed, Sidon, for the sea, the stronghold of the sea, has spoken: I have not been in labor or given birth; I have not reared young men or brought virgins to maturity.

23:5  LA: Cum auditum fuerit in Ægypto, dolebunt cum audierint de Tiro.
      EN: When it is heard in Egypt, they will grieve as they hear of Tyre.

23:6  LA: Transite maria, ululate, qui habitatis in insula !
      EN: Cross the seas; wail, you who dwell on the island!

23:7  LA: Numquid non vestra hæc est, quæ gloriabatur a diebus pristinis in antiquitate sua ? Ducent eam pedes sui longe ad peregrinandum.
      EN: Is this not your city, which gloried in its antiquity from the earliest days? Her feet will carry her far away to sojourn.

23:8  LA: Quis cogitavit hoc super Tyrum quondam coronatam, cujus negotiatores principes, institores ejus inclyti terræ ?
      EN: Who has planned this against Tyre, once crowned, whose merchants were rulers and whose traders were the renowned of the earth?

23:9  LA: Dominus exercituum cogitavit hoc, ut detraheret superbiam omnis gloriæ, et ad ignominiam deduceret universos inclytos terræ.
      EN: The Lord of hosts has planned it, to bring down the pride of all glory and bring all the renowned of the earth to disgrace.

23:10  LA: Transi terram tuam quasi flumen, filia maris ! non est cingulum ultra tibi.
       EN: Pass through your land like a river, daughter of the sea! You have no girdle any longer.

23:11  LA: Manum suam extendit super mare ; conturbavit regna. Dominus mandavit adversus Chanaan, ut contereret fortes ejus ;
       EN: He has stretched His hand over the sea and shaken kingdoms. The Lord has commanded against Canaan, to crush its mighty ones.

23:12  LA: et dixit : Non adjicies ultra ut glorieris, calumniam sustinens virgo filia Sidonis : in Cethim consurgens transfreta : ibi quoque non erit requies tibi.
       EN: He said: You will glory no longer, oppressed virgin daughter of Sidon. Rise and cross over to Kittim; there too you will have no rest.

23:13  LA: Ecce terra Chaldæorum, talis populus non fuit : Assur fundavit eam ; in captivitatem traduxerunt robustos ejus, suffoderunt domos ejus, posuerunt eam in ruinam.
       EN: Behold, the land of the Chaldeans: there was no such people. Assyria founded it. They led its mighty ones into captivity, undermined its houses, and made it a ruin.

23:14  LA: Ululate, naves maris, quia devastata est fortitudo vestra.]
       EN: Wail, ships of the sea, for your stronghold has been devastated.

23:15  LA: Et erit in die illa : in oblivione eris, o Tyre ! septuaginta annis, sicut dies regis unius ; post septuaginta autem annos erit Tyro quasi canticum meretricis :
       EN: On that day you will be forgotten, O Tyre, for seventy years, like the days of one king. After seventy years, Tyre will be like the song of a prostitute:

23:16  LA: [Sume citharam, circui civitatem, meretrix oblivioni tradita : bene cane, frequenta canticum, ut memoria tui sit.
       EN: Take a harp and go around the city, forgotten prostitute. Play well, repeat your song, so that you may be remembered.

23:17  LA: Et erit post septuaginta annos : visitabit Dominus Tyrum, et reducet eam ad mercedes suas, et rursum fornicabitur cum universis regnis terræ super faciem terræ ;
       EN: After seventy years the Lord will visit Tyre and restore her to her earnings; she will again prostitute herself with all the kingdoms of the world upon the face of the earth.

23:18  LA: et erunt negotiationes ejus et mercedes ejus sanctificatæ Domino : non condentur neque reponentur, quia his qui habitaverint coram Domino erit negotiatio ejus, ut manducent in saturitatem, et vestiantur usque ad vetustatem.]
       EN: Her trade and her earnings will be consecrated to the Lord. They will not be stored or laid away, for her trade will belong to those who dwell before the Lord, so that they may eat their fill and be clothed to old age.

ISAIAH 24

24:1  LA: Ecce Dominus dissipabit terram : et nudabit eam, et affliget faciem ejus, et disperget habitatores ejus.
      EN: Behold, the Lord will scatter the earth and strip it bare; He will afflict its face and disperse its inhabitants.

24:2  LA: Et erit sicut populus, sic sacerdos ; et sicut servus, sic dominus ejus ; sicut ancilla, sic domina ejus ; sicut emens, sic ille qui vendit ; sicut fœnerator, sic is qui mutuum accipit ; sicut qui repetit, sic qui debet.
      EN: People and priest will be alike, slave and master alike, maid and mistress alike, buyer and seller alike, lender and borrower alike, creditor and debtor alike.

24:3  LA: Dissipatione dissipabitur terra, et direptione prædabitur ; Dominus enim locutus est verbum hoc.
      EN: The earth will be utterly scattered and thoroughly plundered, for the Lord has spoken this word.

24:4  LA: Luxit, et defluxit terra, et infirmata est ; defluxit orbis, infirmata est altitudo populi terræ.
      EN: The earth has mourned, faded, and weakened; the world has faded, and the high estate of the earth's people has grown weak.

24:5  LA: Et terra infecta est ab habitatoribus suis, quia transgressi sunt leges, mutaverunt jus, dissipaverunt fœdus sempiternum.
      EN: The earth has been polluted by its inhabitants, because they have transgressed laws, altered justice, and broken the everlasting covenant.

24:6  LA: Propter hoc maledictio vorabit terram, et peccabunt habitatores ejus ; ideoque insanient cultores ejus, et relinquentur homines pauci.
      EN: Therefore a curse will devour the earth, and its inhabitants will sin. Therefore its cultivators will go mad, and few people will be left.

24:7  LA: Luxit vindemia, infirmata est vitis, ingemuerunt omnes qui lætabantur corde ;
      EN: The vintage has mourned; the vine has grown weak; all who were glad of heart have groaned.

24:8  LA: cessavit gaudium tympanorum, quievit sonitus lætantium, conticuit dulcedo citharæ.
      EN: The joy of tambourines has ceased, the sound of rejoicing has come to rest, and the sweetness of the harp has fallen silent.

24:9  LA: Cum cantico non bibent vinum ; amara erit potio bibentibus illam.
      EN: They will not drink wine with a song; the drink will be bitter to those who drink it.

24:10  LA: Attrita est civitas vanitatis, clausa est omnis domus, nullo introëunte.
       EN: The city of vanity has been crushed; every house is shut, with no one entering.

24:11  LA: Clamor erit super vino in plateis, deserta est omnia lætitia, translatum est gaudium terræ.
       EN: There will be a cry over wine in the streets; all gladness has been abandoned, and the earth's joy has been taken away.

24:12  LA: Relicta est in urbe solitudo, et calamitas opprimet portas.
       EN: Desolation is left in the city, and calamity will crush the gates.

24:13  LA: Quia hæc erunt in medio terræ in medio populorum, quomodo si paucæ olivæ quæ remanserunt excutiantur ex olea et racemi, cum fuerit finita vindemia.]
       EN: For it will be so in the midst of the land, among the peoples, as when the few olives remaining on an olive tree are shaken off, or the clusters left when the vintage has ended.

24:14  LA: [Hi levabunt vocem suam, atque laudabunt : cum glorificatus fuerit Dominus, hinnient de mari.
       EN: These will lift their voice and give praise; when the Lord is glorified, they will cry out from the sea.

24:15  LA: Propter hoc in doctrinis glorificate Dominum ; in insulis maris nomen Domini Dei Israël.
       EN: Therefore glorify the Lord in teachings, the name of the Lord, the God of Israel, in the islands of the sea.

24:16  LA: A finibus terræ laudes audivimus, gloriam Justi. Et dixi : Secretum meum mihi, secretum meum mihi. Væ mihi ! prævaricantes prævaricati sunt, et prævaricatione transgressorum prævaricati sunt.
       EN: From the ends of the earth we have heard praises, the glory of the righteous one. And I said: My secret is my own, my secret is my own. Woe to me! The treacherous have acted treacherously, and with the treachery of transgressors they have acted treacherously.

24:17  LA: Formido, et fovea, et laqueus super te, qui habitator es terræ.
       EN: Terror and pit and snare are upon you, inhabitant of the earth.

24:18  LA: Et erit : qui fugerit a voce formidinis cadet in foveam ; et qui se explicaverit de fovea tenebitur laqueo ; quia cataractæ de excelsis apertæ sunt et concutientur fundamenta terræ.
       EN: Whoever flees from the sound of terror will fall into the pit, and whoever climbs out of the pit will be caught in the snare. For the floodgates on high have been opened, and the foundations of the earth will be shaken.

24:19  LA: Confractione confringetur terra, contritione conteretur terra, commotione commovebitur terra ;
       EN: The earth will be utterly broken, the earth utterly crushed, the earth utterly shaken.

24:20  LA: agitatione agitabitur terra sicut ebrius, et auferetur quasi tabernaculum unius noctis ; et gravabit eam iniquitas sua, et corruet, et non adjiciet ut resurgat.
       EN: The earth will stagger violently like a drunkard and be swept away like a tent pitched for one night. Its iniquity will weigh it down; it will fall and will not rise again.

24:21  LA: Et erit : in die illa visitabit Dominus super militiam cæli in excelso, et super reges terræ qui sunt super terram ;
       EN: On that day the Lord will punish the host of heaven on high and the kings of the earth upon the earth.

24:22  LA: et congregabuntur in congregatione unius fascis in lacum, et claudentur ibi in carcere, et post multos dies visitabuntur.
       EN: They will be gathered together in one bundle into the pit and shut there in prison; after many days they will be visited.

24:23  LA: Et erubescet luna, et confundetur sol, cum regnaverit Dominus exercituum in monte Sion et in Jerusalem et in conspectu senum suorum fuerit glorificatus.]
       EN: The moon will blush, and the sun will be put to shame, when the Lord of hosts reigns on Mount Zion and in Jerusalem and is glorified in the sight of His elders.

ISAIAH 25

25:1  LA: Domine , Deus meus es tu ; exaltabo te, et confitebor nomini tuo : quoniam fecisti mirabilia, cogitationes antiquas fideles. Amen.
      EN: Lord, You are my God; I will exalt You and give thanks to Your name, for You have done wonders, ancient and faithful plans. Amen.

25:2  LA: Quia posuisti civitatem in tumulum, urbem fortem in ruinam, domum alienorum : ut non sit civitas, et in sempiternum non ædificetur.
      EN: For You have made the city a mound, the fortified town a ruin, the foreigners' house no longer a city, never to be rebuilt.

25:3  LA: Super hoc laudabit te populus fortis ; civitas gentium robustarum timebit te :
      EN: Therefore a strong people will praise You, and the city of mighty nations will fear You,

25:4  LA: quia factus es fortitudo pauperi, fortitudo egeno in tribulatione sua, spes a turbine, umbraculum ab æstu ; spiritus enim robustorum quasi turbo impellens parietem.
      EN: because You have become strength for the poor, strength for the needy in their distress, a refuge from the whirlwind, shade from the heat; for the breath of the mighty is like a whirlwind beating against a wall.

25:5  LA: Sicut æstus in siti, tumultum alienorum humiliabis ; et quasi calore sub nube torrente, propaginem fortium marcescere facies.
      EN: Like heat in a parched place, You will bring down the tumult of foreigners; as by heat burning beneath a cloud, You will make the offspring of the mighty wither.

25:6  LA: Et faciet Dominus exercituum omnibus populis in monte hoc convivium pinguium, convivium vindemiæ, pinguium medullatorum, vindemiæ defæcatæ.
      EN: On this mountain the Lord of hosts will make for all peoples a banquet of rich foods, a banquet of wine from the vintage, rich foods full of marrow, wine cleared of its lees.

25:7  LA: Et præcipitabit in monte isto faciem vinculi colligati super omnes populos, et telam quam orditus est super omnes nationes.
      EN: On this mountain He will overthrow the covering of the bond laid over all peoples, and the web that He has woven over all nations.

25:8  LA: Præcipitabit mortem in sempiternum ; et auferet Dominus Deus lacrimam ab omni facie, et opprobrium populi sui auferet de universa terra : quia Dominus locutus est.]
      EN: He will overthrow death forever. The Lord God will remove the tear from every face and remove His people's disgrace from the whole earth, for the Lord has spoken.

25:9  LA: [Et dicet in die illa : Ecce Deus noster iste ; exspectavimus eum, et salvabit nos ; iste Dominus, sustinuimus eum : exsultabimus, et lætabimur in salutari ejus.
      EN: On that day it will be said: Behold, this is our God; we have waited for Him, and He will save us. This is the Lord; we have waited for Him. We will exult and rejoice in His salvation.

25:10  LA: Quia requiescet manus Domini in monte isto ; et triturabitur Moab sub eo, sicuti teruntur paleæ in plaustro.
       EN: For the Lord's hand will rest on this mountain, and Moab will be threshed beneath Him, as straw is crushed by a cart.

25:11  LA: Et extendet manus suas sub eo sicut extendit natans ad natandum ; et humiliabit gloriam ejus cum allisione manuum ejus.
       EN: Moab will stretch out his hands beneath him as a swimmer stretches out his hands to swim, and He will humble his glory with the striking of his hands.

25:12  LA: Et munimenta sublimium murorum tuorum concident, et humiliabuntur, et detrahentur in terram usque ad pulverem.]
       EN: The fortifications of your lofty walls will fall and be brought low, dragged down to the earth, to the dust itself.

ISAIAH 26

26:1  LA: In die illa cantabitur canticum istud in terra Juda : Urbs fortitudinis nostræ Sion ; salvator ponetur in ea murus et antemurale.
      EN: On that day this song will be sung in the land of Judah: Zion is the city of our strength; a savior will be set within her as a wall and an outer wall.

26:2  LA: Aperite portas, et ingrediatur gens justa, custodiens veritatem.
      EN: Open the gates, and let the righteous nation enter, the nation that keeps truth.

26:3  LA: Vetus error abiit : servabis pacem ; pacem, quia in te speravimus.
      EN: The old error has passed away. You will preserve peace—peace, because we have hoped in You.

26:4  LA: Sperastis in Domino in sæculis æternis ; in Domino Deo forti in perpetuum.
      EN: You have hoped in the Lord for everlasting ages, in the Lord God, mighty forever.

26:5  LA: Quia incurvabit habitantes in excelso ; civitatem sublimem humiliabit : humiliabit eam usque ad terram, detrahet eam usque ad pulverem.
      EN: For He will bring down those dwelling on high; He will humble the lofty city, humble it to the ground, drag it down to the dust.

26:6  LA: Conculcabit eam pes, pedes pauperis, gressus egenorum.
      EN: A foot will trample it, the feet of the poor, the steps of the needy.

26:7  LA: Semita justi recta est, rectus callis justi ad ambulandum.
      EN: The path of the righteous is straight, straight is the righteous one's way to walk.

26:8  LA: Et in semita judiciorum tuorum, Domine, sustinuimus te : nomen tuum et memoriale tuum in desiderio animæ.
      EN: In the path of Your judgments, Lord, we have waited for You; Your name and Your remembrance are the soul's desire.

26:9  LA: Anima mea desideravit te in nocte, sed et spiritu meo in præcordiis meis de mane vigilabo ad te. Cum feceris judicia tua in terra, justitiam discent habitatores orbis.
      EN: My soul has desired You in the night; indeed, with my spirit within me I will keep watch for You in the morning. When You carry out Your judgments on the earth, the inhabitants of the world will learn righteousness.

26:10  LA: Misereamur impio, et non discet justitiam ; in terra sanctorum iniqua gessit, et non videbit gloriam Domini.
       EN: Let us show mercy to the wicked, and he will not learn righteousness; in the land of the holy he has acted unjustly, and he will not see the Lord's glory.

26:11  LA: Domine, exaltetur manus tua, et non videant ; videant, et confundantur zelantes populi ; et ignis hostes tuos devoret.
       EN: Lord, let Your hand be exalted, though they do not see. Let the jealous peoples see and be put to shame, and let fire devour Your enemies.

26:12  LA: Domine, dabis pacem nobis : omnia enim opera nostra operatus es nobis.
       EN: Lord, You will give us peace, for You have accomplished all our works for us.

26:13  LA: Domine Deus noster, possederunt nos domini absque te ; tantum in te recordemur nominis tui.
       EN: Lord our God, masters other than You have possessed us; only in You may we remember Your name.

26:14  LA: Morientes non vivant, gigantes non resurgant : propterea visitasti et contrivisti eos, et perdidisti omnem memoriam eorum.
       EN: Let the dying not live; let the giants not rise again. Therefore You have visited and crushed them and destroyed all memory of them.

26:15  LA: Indulsisti genti, Domine, indulsisti genti, numquid glorificatus es ? elongasti omnes terminos terræ.
       EN: You have shown favor to the nation, Lord; You have shown favor to the nation. Have You been glorified? You have extended all the boundaries of the land.

26:16  LA: Domine, in angustia requisierunt te, in tribulatione murmuris doctrina tua eis.
       EN: Lord, in anguish they have sought You; amid the distress of murmuring, Your instruction was theirs.

26:17  LA: Sicut quæ concipit, cum appropinquaverit ad partum, dolens clamat in doloribus suis, sic facti sumus a facie tua, Domine.
       EN: As a pregnant woman nearing childbirth cries out in the pain of her labor, so we have become before You, Lord.

26:18  LA: Concepimus, et quasi parturivimus, et peperimus spiritum. Salutes non fecimus in terra ; ideo non ceciderunt habitatores terræ.
       EN: We conceived and were as though in labor, and we gave birth to wind. We have brought no deliverance on earth; therefore the inhabitants of the earth have not fallen.

26:19  LA: Vivent mortui tui, interfecti mei resurgent. Expergiscimini, et laudate, qui habitatis in pulvere, quia ros lucis ros tuus, et terram gigantum detrahes in ruinam.
       EN: Your dead will live; my slain will rise again. Awake and give praise, you who dwell in the dust, for Your dew is a dew of light, and You will bring the land of the giants down to ruin.

26:20  LA: Vade, populus meus, intra in cubicula tua ; claude ostia tua super te, abscondere modicum ad momentum, donec pertranseat indignatio.
       EN: Go, My people, enter your chambers; shut your doors behind you. Hide for a little while, for a moment, until the indignation passes by.

26:21  LA: Ecce enim Dominus egredietur de loco suo, ut visitet iniquitatem habitatoris terræ contra eum ; et revelabit terra sanguinem suum, et non operiet ultra interfectos suos.]
       EN: For behold, the Lord will go out from His place to punish the inhabitant of the earth for his iniquity. The earth will reveal its blood and no longer cover its slain.

ISAIAH 27

27:1  LA: In die illa visitabit Dominus in gladio suo duro, et grandi, et forti, super Leviathan, serpentem vectem, et super Leviathan, serpentem tortuosum, et occidet cetum qui in mari est.
      EN: On that day the Lord, with His hard and great and mighty sword, will punish Leviathan the bar-like serpent and Leviathan the twisting serpent, and He will kill the sea creature that is in the sea.

27:2  LA: In die illa vinea meri cantabit ei.
      EN: On that day the vineyard of pure wine will sing to Him.

27:3  LA: Ego Dominus qui servo eam ; repente propinabo ei. Ne forte visitetur contra eam, nocte et die servo eam.
      EN: I am the Lord who guards it; I will promptly give it drink. Lest an attack be made against it, I guard it night and day.

27:4  LA: Indignatio non est mihi. Quis dabit me spinam et veprem in prælio ? gradiar super eam, succendam eam pariter.
      EN: I have no indignation. Who will make Me a thorn and brier in battle? I will march upon it and burn it all together.

27:5  LA: An potius tenebit fortitudinem meam ? faciet pacem mihi, pacem faciet mihi.
      EN: Or will it rather take hold of My strength? It will make peace with Me; it will make peace with Me.

27:6  LA: Qui ingrediuntur impetu ad Jacob, florebit et germinabit Israël, et implebunt faciem orbis semine.
      EN: As for those who enter Jacob with a rush, Israel will blossom and bud, and they will fill the face of the world with seed.

27:7  LA: Numquid juxta plagam percutientis se percussit eum ? aut sicut occidit interfectos ejus, sic occisus est ?
      EN: Has He struck him according to the blow of the one who struck him? Or has he been killed as He killed those slain by him?

27:8  LA: In mensura contra mensuram, cum abjecta fuerit, judicabis eam ; meditatus est in spiritu suo duro per diem æstus.
      EN: Measure against measure, when she has been cast away, You will judge her; He has pondered with His harsh spirit on the day of heat.

27:9  LA: Idcirco super hoc dimittetur iniquitas domui Jacob ; et iste omnis fructus : ut auferatur peccatum ejus, cum posuerit omnes lapides altaris sicut lapides cineris allisos : non stabunt luci et delubra.
      EN: Therefore through this the iniquity of the house of Jacob will be forgiven, and this is all the fruit: that its sin be removed, when it makes all the altar's stones like stones crushed to ash. Sacred groves and shrines will not stand.

27:10  LA: Civitas enim munita desolata erit ; speciosa relinquetur, et dimittetur quasi desertum ; ibi pascetur vitulus, et ibi accubabit, et consumet summitates ejus.
       EN: For the fortified city will be desolate; the beautiful city will be left and abandoned like a wilderness. The calf will graze there and lie down there and consume its upper branches.

27:11  LA: In siccitate messes illius conterentur. Mulieres venientes, et docentes eam ; non est enim populus sapiens : propterea non miserebitur ejus qui fecit eum, et qui formavit eum non parcet ei.
       EN: In the drought its harvests will be crushed. Women will come and teach it, for it is not a wise people; therefore the One who made it will not have mercy on it, and the One who formed it will not spare it.

27:12  LA: Et erit : in die illa percutiet Dominus ab alveo fluminis usque ad torrentem Ægypti ; et vos congregabimini unus et unus, filii Israël.
       EN: On that day the Lord will strike from the channel of the river to the torrent of Egypt, and you will be gathered one by one, children of Israel.

27:13  LA: Et erit : in die illa clangetur in tuba magna ; et venient qui perditi fuerant de terra Assyriorum, et qui ejecti erant in terra Ægypti, et adorabunt Dominum in monte sancto in Jerusalem.]
       EN: On that day a great trumpet will be sounded, and those who had been lost from the land of the Assyrians and those driven away into the land of Egypt will come and worship the Lord on the holy mountain in Jerusalem.

ISAIAH 28

28:1  LA: Væ coronæ superbiæ, ebriis Ephraim, et flori decidenti, gloriæ exsultationis ejus, qui erant in vertice vallis pinguissimæ, errantes a vino.
      EN: Woe to the crown of pride, to Ephraim's drunkards, and to the fading flower, the glory of its exultation, to those who were on the crest of the richest valley, wandering because of wine.

28:2  LA: Ecce validus et fortis Dominus sicut impetus grandinis ; turbo confringens, sicut impetus aquarum multarum inundantium et emissarum super terram spatiosam.
      EN: Behold, the Lord is strong and mighty, like the force of hail, a shattering whirlwind, like the force of many flooding waters let loose over a spacious land.

28:3  LA: Pedibus conculcabitur corona superbiæ ebriorum Ephraim.
      EN: The crown of pride of Ephraim's drunkards will be trampled underfoot.

28:4  LA: Et erit flos decidens gloriæ exsultationis ejus, qui est super verticem vallis pinguium, quasi temporaneum ante maturitatem autumni, quod, cum aspexerit videns, statim ut manu tenuerit, devorabit illud.
      EN: The fading flower of the glory of its exultation, on the crest of the rich valley, will be like an early fruit before autumn's ripening: when someone sees it, he devours it as soon as he takes it in his hand.

28:5  LA: In die illa erit Dominus exercituum corona gloriæ, et sertum exsultationis residuo populi sui ;
      EN: On that day the Lord of hosts will be a crown of glory and a garland of exultation for the remnant of His people,

28:6  LA: et spiritus judicii sedenti super judicium, et fortitudo revertentibus de bello ad portam.]
      EN: a spirit of judgment to the one seated in judgment, and strength to those returning from battle to the gate.

28:7  LA: [Verum hi quoque præ vino nescierunt, et præ ebrietate erraverunt ; sacerdos et propheta nescierunt præ ebrietate ; absorpti sunt a vino, erraverunt in ebrietate, nescierunt videntem, ignoraverunt judicium.
      EN: But these too have lost understanding through wine and wandered through drunkenness; priest and prophet have lost understanding through drunkenness. They are swallowed up by wine, they have wandered in drunkenness, they have failed to recognize the seer, they have been ignorant of judgment.

28:8  LA: Omnes enim mensæ repletæ sunt vomitu sordiumque, ita ut non esset ultra locus.
      EN: For all the tables are filled with vomit and filth, so that there is no room left.

28:9  LA: Quem docebit scientiam ? et quem intelligere faciet auditum ? Ablactatos a lacte, avulsos ab uberibus.
      EN: Whom will He teach knowledge, and whom will He make understand what is heard? Those weaned from milk, taken away from the breasts.

28:10  LA: Quia manda, remanda ; manda, remanda ; exspecta, reexspecta ; exspecta, reexspecta ; modicum ibi, modicum ibi.
       EN: For command, command again; command, command again; wait, wait again; wait, wait again; a little there, a little there.

28:11  LA: In loquela enim labii, et lingua altera loquetur ad populum istum.
       EN: For with the speech of the lip and with another tongue He will speak to this people,

28:12  LA: Cui dixit : Hæc est requies mea, reficite lassum ; et hoc est meum refrigerium : et noluerunt audire.
       EN: to whom He said: This is My rest; refresh the weary. This is My refreshment. But they refused to listen.

28:13  LA: Et erit eis verbum Domini : Manda, remanda ; manda, remanda ; exspecta, reexspecta ; exspecta, reexspecta ; modicum ibi, modicum ibi ; ut vadant, et cadant retrorsum, et conterantur, et illaqueentur, et capiantur.
       EN: The word of the Lord will be to them: Command, command again; command, command again; wait, wait again; wait, wait again; a little there, a little there—so that they go and fall backward and are crushed and snared and captured.

28:14  LA: Propter hoc audite verbum Domini, viri illusores, qui dominamini super populum meum, qui est in Jerusalem.
       EN: Therefore hear the word of the Lord, scoffing men who rule over My people in Jerusalem.

28:15  LA: Dixistis enim : Percussimus fœdus cum morte, et cum inferno fecimus pactum : flagellum inundans cum transierit, non veniet super nos quia posuimus mendacium spem nostram, et mendacio protecti sumus.
       EN: For you have said: We have struck a covenant with death and made an agreement with the underworld. When the overwhelming scourge passes through, it will not come upon us, because we have made falsehood our hope and are protected by falsehood.

28:16  LA: Idcirco hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Ecce ego mittam in fundamentis Sion lapidem, lapidem probatum, angularem, pretiosum, in fundamento fundatum ; qui crediderit, non festinet.
       EN: Therefore thus says the Lord God: Behold, I will set in Zion's foundations a stone, a tested stone, a precious cornerstone, established in the foundation. Let the one who believes not make haste.

28:17  LA: Et ponam in pondere judicium, et justitiam in mensura ; et subvertet grando spem mendacii, et protectionem aquæ inundabunt.
       EN: I will set judgment by weight and righteousness by measure. Hail will overthrow the hope of falsehood, and waters will flood its shelter.

28:18  LA: Et delebitur fœdus vestrum cum morte, et pactum vestrum cum inferno non stabit : flagellum inundans cum transierit, eritis ei in conculcationem.
       EN: Your covenant with death will be erased, and your agreement with the underworld will not stand. When the overwhelming scourge passes through, you will become something for it to trample.

28:19  LA: Quandocumque pertransierit, tollet vos, quoniam in mane diluculo pertransibit in die et in nocte ; et tantummodo sola vexatio intellectum dabit auditui.
       EN: Whenever it passes through, it will carry you away, for morning by morning it will pass through, by day and by night; and only affliction itself will give understanding to what is heard.

28:20  LA: Coangustatum est enim stratum, ita ut alter decidat ; et pallium breve utrumque operire non potest.
       EN: For the bed is too narrow, so that one falls off, and the short cloak cannot cover both.

28:21  LA: Sicut enim in monte divisionum stabit Dominus ; sicut in valle quæ est in Gabaon irascetur, ut faciat opus suum, alienum opus ejus : ut operetur opus suum, peregrinum est opus ejus ab eo.
       EN: For the Lord will stand as on the mountain of Divisions; He will be angry as in the valley at Gibeon, to do His work—His strange work—to accomplish His work, a work foreign to Him.

28:22  LA: Et nunc nolite illudere, ne forte constringantur vincula vestra ; consummationem enim et abbreviationem audivi a Domino Deo exercituum, super universam terram.]
       EN: Now, do not scoff, lest your bonds be tightened, for I have heard from the Lord, the God of hosts, of destruction and a cutting short upon the whole earth.

28:23  LA: [Auribus percipite, et audite vocem meam : attendite, et audite eloquium meum.
       EN: Listen closely and hear my voice; pay attention and hear my speech.

28:24  LA: Numquid tota die arabit arans ut serat ? proscindet et sarriet humum suam ?
       EN: Does the plowman plow all day in order to sow? Does he keep breaking up and hoeing his ground?

28:25  LA: Nonne cum adæquaverit faciem ejus, seret gith et cyminum sparget ? et ponet triticum per ordinem, et hordeum, et milium, et viciam in finibus suis ?
       EN: When he has leveled its surface, does he not sow black cumin and scatter cumin, and set wheat in rows, barley and millet, and vetch along its borders?

28:26  LA: Et erudiet illum in judicio ; Deus suus docebit illum.
       EN: He will instruct him in judgment; his God will teach him.

28:27  LA: Non enim in serris triturabitur gith, nec rota plaustri super cyminum circuibit ; sed in virga excutietur gith, et cyminum in baculo.
       EN: For black cumin is not threshed with saws, nor does a cartwheel pass over cumin; black cumin is beaten out with a rod, and cumin with a staff.

28:28  LA: Panis autem comminuetur ; verum non in perpetuum triturans triturabit illum, neque vexabit eum rota plaustri, neque ungulis suis comminuet eum.
       EN: Grain for bread will be crushed, yet the thresher will not thresh it forever; neither will he batter it with a cartwheel nor crush it with his animals' hooves.

28:29  LA: Et hoc a Domino Deo exercituum exivit, ut mirabile faceret consilium, et magnificaret justitiam.]
       EN: This too has come from the Lord, the God of hosts, to make His counsel wondrous and His righteousness great.

ISAIAH 29

29:1  LA: Væ Ariel, Ariel civitas, quam expugnavit David ! additus est annus ad annum : solemnitates evolutæ sunt.
      EN: Woe to Ariel, Ariel, the city David conquered! Year has been added to year; the feasts have run their course.

29:2  LA: Et circumvallabo Ariel, et erit tristis et mœrens, et erit mihi quasi Ariel.
      EN: I will besiege Ariel; it will be sad and mourning, and it will be to Me like Ariel.

29:3  LA: Et circumdabo quasi sphæram in circuitu tuo, et jaciam contra te aggerem, et munimenta ponam in obsidionem tuam.
      EN: I will encircle you like a sphere, throw up a rampart against you, and set fortifications to besiege you.

29:4  LA: Humiliaberis, de terra loqueris, et de humo audietur eloquium tuum ; et erit quasi pythonis de terra vox tua, et de humo eloquium tuum mussitabit.
      EN: You will be humbled; you will speak from the earth, and your speech will be heard from the ground. Your voice will be like a medium's from the earth, and your speech will mutter from the ground.

29:5  LA: Et erit sicut pulvis tenuis multitudo ventilantium te, et sicut favilla pertransiens multitudo eorum qui contra te prævaluerunt ;
      EN: The multitude of those who winnow you will be like fine dust, and the multitude of those who prevailed against you like passing ash;

29:6  LA: eritque repente confestim. A Domino exercituum visitabitur in tonitruo, et commotione terræ, et voce magna turbinis et tempestatis, et flammæ ignis devorantis.
      EN: it will happen suddenly, in an instant. There will be a visitation from the Lord of hosts with thunder and earthquake and a mighty sound of whirlwind and storm and a flame of devouring fire.

29:7  LA: Et erit sicut somnium visionis nocturnæ multitudo omnium gentium quæ dimicaverunt contra Ariel, et omnes qui militaverunt, et obsederunt, et prævaluerunt adversus eam.
      EN: Like a dream, a vision of the night, will be the multitude of all the nations that fought against Ariel, and all who campaigned and besieged it and prevailed against it.

29:8  LA: Et sicut somniat esuriens, et comedit, cum autem fuerit expergefactus, vacua est anima ejus ; et sicut somniat sitiens et bibit, et postquam fuerit expergefactus, lassus adhuc sitit, et anima ejus vacua est : sic erit multitudo omnium gentium quæ dimicaverunt contra montem Sion.
      EN: As a hungry man dreams and eats, but when he awakens his appetite is empty; and as a thirsty man dreams and drinks, but when he awakens he is still weary and thirsty, his appetite empty—so will be the multitude of all the nations that fought against Mount Zion.

29:9  LA: Obstupescite et admiramini ; fluctuate et vacillate ; inebriamini, et non a vino ; movemini, et non ab ebrietate.
      EN: Be stunned and marvel; reel and stagger. Be drunk, but not with wine; sway, but not from strong drink.

29:10  LA: Quoniam miscuit vobis Dominus spiritum soporis ; claudet oculos vestros : prophetas et principes vestros, qui vident visiones, operiet.
       EN: For the Lord has mingled for you a spirit of deep sleep. He will close your eyes; He will cover your prophets and rulers who see visions.

29:11  LA: Et erit vobis visio omnium sicut verba libri signati, quem cum dederint scienti litteras, dicent : Lege istum : et respondebit : Non possum, signatus est enim.
       EN: The whole vision will be to you like the words of a sealed book. When they give it to someone who can read, saying, Read this, he will answer: I cannot, for it is sealed.

29:12  LA: Et dabitur liber nescienti litteras, diceturque ei : Lege ; et respondebit : Nescio litteras.
       EN: The book will be given to someone who cannot read, and he will be told, Read. He will answer: I cannot read.

29:13  LA: Et dixit Dominus : Eo quod appropinquat populus iste ore suo, et labiis suis glorificat me, cor autem ejus longe est a me, et timuerunt me mandato hominum et doctrinis,
       EN: The Lord said: Because this people draws near with its mouth and glorifies Me with its lips, but its heart is far from Me, and their fear of Me comes from human command and teachings,

29:14  LA: ideo ecce ego addam ut admirationem faciam populo huic miraculo grandi et stupendo ; peribit enim sapientia a sapientibus ejus, et intellectus prudentium ejus abscondetur.
       EN: therefore, behold, I will again astonish this people with a great and astounding wonder. For wisdom will perish from its wise, and the understanding of its prudent will be hidden.

29:15  LA: Væ qui profundi estis corde, ut a Domino abscondatis consilium ; quorum sunt in tenebris opera, et dicunt : Quis videt nos ? et quis novit nos ?
       EN: Woe to you who go deep in heart to hide your plan from the Lord, whose deeds are in darkness, who say: Who sees us? Who knows us?

29:16  LA: Perversa est hæc vestra cogitatio ; quasi si lutum contra figulum cogitet, et dicat opus factori suo : Non fecisti me ; et figmentum dicat fictori suo : Non intelligis.
       EN: This thought of yours is perverse, as though clay should devise a plan against the potter, and the work say to its maker, You did not make me, and the thing formed say to its former, You do not understand.

29:17  LA: Nonne adhuc in modico et in brevi convertetur Libanus in carmel, et carmel in saltum reputabitur ?
       EN: Is it not just a little while, a short time, until Lebanon becomes a fruitful field, and a fruitful field is regarded as a forest?

29:18  LA: Et audient in die illa surdi verba libri, et de tenebris et caligine oculi cæcorum videbunt.
       EN: On that day the deaf will hear the words of the book, and out of darkness and gloom the eyes of the blind will see.

29:19  LA: Et addent mites in Domino lætitiam, et pauperes homines in Sancto Israël exsultabunt ;
       EN: The meek will increase their joy in the Lord, and poor people will exult in the Holy One of Israel,

29:20  LA: quoniam defecit qui prævalebat, consummatus est illusor, et succisi sunt omnes qui vigilabant super iniquitatem,
       EN: for the one who prevailed has ceased, the scoffer is finished, and all who watched for iniquity have been cut down,

29:21  LA: qui peccare faciebant homines in verbo, et arguentem in porta supplantabant, et declinaverunt frustra a justo.
       EN: those who made people sin by a word, tripped up the one who reproved at the gate, and turned away from the righteous for nothing.

29:22  LA: Propter hoc, hæc dicit Dominus ad domum Jacob, qui redemit Abraham : Non modo confundetur Jacob, nec modo vultus ejus erubescet ;
       EN: Therefore thus says the Lord, who redeemed Abraham, to the house of Jacob: Jacob will not now be put to shame, nor will his face now blush;

29:23  LA: sed cum viderit filios suos, opera manuum mearum in medio sui sanctificantes nomen meum, et sanctificabunt Sanctum Jacob, et Deum Israël prædicabunt ;
       EN: but when he sees his children, the works of My hands, in his midst sanctifying My name, they too will sanctify the Holy One of Jacob and proclaim the God of Israel.

29:24  LA: et scient errantes spiritu intellectum, et mussitatores discent legem.]
       EN: Those who wander in spirit will know understanding, and those who murmur will learn the law.

ISAIAH 30

30:1  LA: Væ filii desertores, dicit Dominus, ut faceretis consilium, et non ex me, et ordiremini telam, et non per spiritum meum, ut adderetis peccatum super peccatum ;
      EN: Woe to you, deserting children, says the Lord, who form a plan, but not from Me, and weave a web, but not through My spirit, adding sin upon sin;

30:2  LA: qui ambulatis ut descendatis in Ægyptum, et os meum non interrogastis, sperantes auxilium in fortitudine Pharaonis, et habentes fiduciam in umbra Ægypti !
      EN: who set out to go down to Egypt without consulting My mouth, hoping for help in Pharaoh's strength and trusting in Egypt's shadow!

30:3  LA: Et erit vobis fortitudo Pharaonis in confusionem, et fiducia umbræ Ægypti in ignominiam.
      EN: Pharaoh's strength will become your shame, and trust in Egypt's shadow your disgrace.

30:4  LA: Erant enim in Tani principes tui, et nuntii tui usque ad Hanes pervenerunt.
      EN: For your rulers were in Tanis, and your messengers reached Hanes.

30:5  LA: Omnes confusi sunt super populo qui eis prodesse non potuit : non fuerunt in auxilium et in aliquam utilitatem, sed in confusionem et in opprobrium.]
      EN: They have all been put to shame by a people that could not profit them; they were no help and of no use, but a source of shame and reproach.

30:6  LA: Onus jumentorum austri. [In terra tribulationis et angustiæ leæna, et leo ex eis, vipera et regulus volans ; portantes super humeros jumentorum divitias suas, et super gibbum camelorum thesauros suos, ad populum qui eis prodesse non poterit.
      EN: The burden concerning the beasts of the south. In a land of distress and anguish, from which come lioness and lion, viper and flying basilisk, they carry their wealth upon the shoulders of beasts of burden and their treasures upon the camels' humps, to a people that cannot profit them.

30:7  LA: Ægyptus enim frustra et vane auxiliabitur. Ideo clamavi super hoc : Superbia tantum est, quiesce.
      EN: For Egypt will help fruitlessly and in vain. Therefore I cried concerning this: It is only pride; be still.

30:8  LA: Nunc ergo ingressus, scribe ei super buxum, et in libro diligenter exara illud, et erit in die novissimo in testimonium usque in æternum.
      EN: Now, therefore, go in and write it for them on boxwood, and carefully inscribe it in a book, so that on the last day it may be an everlasting testimony.

30:9  LA: Populus enim ad iracundiam provocans est : et filii mendaces, filii nolentes audire legem Dei ;
      EN: For it is a people provoking to anger, lying children, children unwilling to hear God's law,

30:10  LA: qui dicunt videntibus : Nolite videre, et aspicientibus : Nolite aspicere nobis ea quæ recta sunt ; loquimini nobis placentia : videte nobis errores.
       EN: who say to the seers, Do not see, and to those who look, Do not look upon what is right for us. Speak pleasant things to us; see errors for us.

30:11  LA: Auferte a me viam ; declinate a me semitam ; cesset a facie nostra Sanctus Israël.
       EN: Take the way away from me; turn the path away from me. Let the Holy One of Israel disappear from before us.

30:12  LA: Propterea hæc dicit Sanctus Israël : Pro eo quod reprobastis verbum hoc, et sperastis in calumnia et in tumultu, et innixi estis super eo ;
       EN: Therefore thus says the Holy One of Israel: Because you have rejected this word and hoped in oppression and tumult and leaned upon it,

30:13  LA: propterea erit vobis iniquitas hæc sicut interruptio cadens, et requisita in muro excelso, quoniam subito, dum non speratur, veniet contritio ejus.
       EN: this iniquity will be to you like a falling breach discovered in a high wall, whose collapse comes suddenly, when it is not expected.

30:14  LA: Et comminuetur sicut conteritur lagena figuli contritione pervalida, et non invenietur de fragmentis ejus testa in qua portetur igniculus de incendio, aut hauriatur parum aquæ de fovea.
       EN: It will be shattered as a potter's jar is broken with a most violent breaking, so that among its fragments no shard is found with which to carry a little ember from the fire or draw a little water from a pit.

30:15  LA: Quia hæc dicit Dominus Deus, Sanctus Israël : Si revertamini et quiescatis, salvi eritis ; in silentio et in spe erit fortitudo vestra. Et noluistis,
       EN: For thus says the Lord God, the Holy One of Israel: If you return and rest, you will be saved; in silence and hope will be your strength. But you were unwilling,
