30:16  LA: et dixistis : Nequaquam, sed ad equos fugiemus : ideo fugietis ; et : Super veloces ascendemus : ideo velociores erunt qui persequentur vos.
       EN: and you said: No, we will flee to horses. Therefore you will flee. And: We will mount swift steeds. Therefore those who pursue you will be swifter.

30:17  LA: Mille homines a facie terroris unius ; et a facie terroris quinque fugietis, donec relinquamini quasi malus navis in vertice montis, et quasi signum super collem.
       EN: A thousand men at the terror of one; at the terror of five you will flee, until you are left like a ship's mast on a mountain summit and like a signal on a hill.

30:18  LA: Propterea exspectat Dominus ut misereatur vestri ; et ideo exaltabitur parcens vobis, quia Deus judicii Dominus : beati omnes qui exspectant eum !
       EN: Therefore the Lord waits to have mercy on you, and therefore He will be exalted in sparing you, for the Lord is a God of judgment. Blessed are all who wait for Him!

30:19  LA: Populus enim Sion habitabit in Jerusalem : plorans nequaquam plorabis : miserans miserebitur tui, ad vocem clamoris tui : statim ut audierit, respondebit tibi.
       EN: For the people of Zion will dwell in Jerusalem. You will surely weep no more. He will show you abundant mercy at the sound of your cry; as soon as He hears, He will answer you.

30:20  LA: Et dabit vobis Dominus panem arctum, et aquam brevem ; et non faciet avolare a te ultra doctorem tuum ; et erunt oculi tui videntes præceptorem tuum.
       EN: The Lord will give you scant bread and little water. He will no longer make your teacher fly away from you, and your eyes will see your instructor.

30:21  LA: Et aures tuæ audient verbum post tergum monentis : Hæc est via ; ambulate in ea, et non declinetis neque ad dexteram, neque ad sinistram.
       EN: Your ears will hear the word of one warning behind you: This is the way; walk in it, and turn neither to the right nor to the left.

30:22  LA: Et contaminabis laminas sculptilium argenti tui, et vestimentum conflatilis auri tui, et disperges ea sicut immunditiam menstruatæ. Egredere, dices ei.
       EN: You will defile the silver plates of your carved images and the covering of your cast golden image, and scatter them like the uncleanness of a menstruating woman. Out! you will say to it.

30:23  LA: Et dabitur pluvia semini tuo, ubicumque seminaveris in terra, et panis frugum terræ erit uberrimus et pinguis ; pascetur in possessione tua in die illo agnus spatiose,
       EN: Rain will be given to your seed wherever you sow in the land, and bread from the land's produce will be abundant and rich. On that day the lamb will graze at large on your property,

30:24  LA: et tauri tui, et pulli asinorum, qui operantur terram, commistum migma comedent sicut in area ventilatum est.
       EN: and your bulls and young donkeys that work the ground will eat mixed fodder, just as it has been winnowed on the threshing floor.

30:25  LA: Et erunt super omnem montem excelsum, et super omnem collem elevatum, rivi currentium aquarum, in die interfectionis multorum, cum ceciderint turres :
       EN: On every high mountain and every lofty hill there will be streams of flowing water, on the day of the slaughter of many, when the towers fall.

30:26  LA: et erit lux lunæ sicut lux solis, et lux solis erit septempliciter sicut lux septem dierum, in die qua alligaverit Dominus vulnus populi sui, et percussuram plagæ ejus sanaverit.
       EN: The moon's light will be like the sun's light, and the sun's light sevenfold, like the light of seven days, on the day when the Lord binds His people's wound and heals the blow of their injury.

30:27  LA: Ecce nomen Domini venit de longinquo, ardens furor ejus, et gravis ad portandum ; labia ejus repleta sunt indignatione, et lingua ejus quasi ignis devorans.
       EN: Behold, the name of the Lord comes from afar, His fury burning and heavy to bear. His lips are filled with indignation, and His tongue is like a devouring fire.

30:28  LA: Spiritus ejus velut torrens inundans usque ad medium colli, ad perdendas gentes in nihilum, et frenum erroris quod erat in maxillis populorum.
       EN: His breath is like an overflowing torrent reaching to the middle of the neck, to bring the nations to nothing and destroy the bridle of error that was in the peoples' jaws.

30:29  LA: Canticum erit vobis sicut nox sanctificatæ solemnitatis, et lætitia cordis sicut qui pergit cum tibia, ut intret in montem Domini ad Fortem Israël.
       EN: You will have a song as on the night of a consecrated festival, and gladness of heart like one walking with a flute to enter the mountain of the Lord, to the Mighty One of Israel.

30:30  LA: Et auditam faciet Dominus gloriam vocis suæ, et terrorem brachii sui ostendet in comminatione furoris, et flamma ignis devorantis : allidet in turbine, et in lapide grandinis.
       EN: The Lord will make the glory of His voice heard and display the terror of His arm with threatening fury and a flame of devouring fire. He will strike with whirlwind and hailstone.

30:31  LA: A voce enim Domini pavebit Assur virga percussus.
       EN: For at the Lord's voice Assyria, struck with the rod, will be terrified.

30:32  LA: Et erit transitus virgæ fundatus, quam requiescere faciet Dominus super eum in tympanis et citharis ; et in bellis præcipuis expugnabit eos.
       EN: The passage of the rod that the Lord makes rest upon him will be firmly set, with tambourines and harps, and in decisive battles He will defeat them.

30:33  LA: Præparata est enim ab heri Topheth, a rege præparata, profunda, et dilatata. Nutrimenta ejus, ignis et ligna multa ; flatus Domini sicut torrens sulphuris succendens eam.]
       EN: For Topheth has been prepared since yesterday, prepared by the king, deep and wide. Its fuel is fire and much wood; the Lord's breath is like a torrent of sulfur setting it ablaze.

ISAIAH 31

31:1  LA: Væ qui descendunt in Ægyptum ad auxilium, in equis sperantes, et habentes fiduciam super quadrigis, quia multæ sunt ; et super equitibus, quia prævalidi nimis ; et non sunt confisi super Sanctum Israël, et Dominum non requisierunt !
      EN: Woe to those who go down to Egypt for help, hoping in horses and trusting in chariots because they are many, and in horsemen because they are very powerful, but who have not trusted the Holy One of Israel or sought the Lord!

31:2  LA: Ipse autem sapiens adduxit malum, et verba sua non abstulit ; et consurget contra domum pessimorum, et contra auxilium operantium iniquitatem.
      EN: Yet He, being wise, has brought calamity and has not withdrawn His words. He will rise against the house of the most wicked and against the help of those who work iniquity.

31:3  LA: Ægyptus homo, et non deus ; et equi eorum caro, et non spiritus ; et Dominus inclinabit manum suam, et corruet auxiliator, et cadet cui præstatur auxilium, simulque omnes consumentur.
      EN: Egypt is human, not God, and their horses are flesh, not spirit. The Lord will lower His hand; the helper will collapse, and the one being helped will fall, and they will all be consumed together.

31:4  LA: Quia hæc dicit Dominus ad me : Quomodo si rugiat leo et catulus leonis super prædam suam ; et cum occurrerit ei multitudo pastorum, a voce eorum non formidabit, et a multitudine eorum non pavebit : sic descendet Dominus exercituum ut prælietur super montem Sion et super collem ejus.
      EN: For thus the Lord says to me: As a lion and a young lion roar over their prey, and when a crowd of shepherds confronts them they do not dread their voice or fear their multitude, so the Lord of hosts will come down to fight over Mount Zion and its hill.

31:5  LA: Sicut aves volantes, sic proteget Dominus exercituum Jerusalem, protegens et liberans, transiens et salvans.
      EN: Like flying birds, so the Lord of hosts will protect Jerusalem, protecting and delivering, passing over and saving.

31:6  LA: Convertimini, sicut in profundum recesseratis, filii Israël.
      EN: Return, children of Israel, as deeply as you had withdrawn.

31:7  LA: In die enim illa abjiciet vir idola argenti sui, et idola auri sui, quæ fecerunt vobis manus vestræ in peccatum.
      EN: For on that day each man will cast away his silver idols and his golden idols, which your own hands made for you as a sin.

31:8  LA: Et cadet Assur in gladio non viri, et gladius non hominis vorabit eum : et fugiet non a facie gladii, et juvenes ejus vectigales erunt.
      EN: Assyria will fall by a sword that is not a man's, and a sword that is not human will devour him. He will flee, though not from the face of a sword, and his young men will be subject to tribute.

31:9  LA: Et fortitudo ejus a terrore transibit, et pavebunt fugientes principes ejus, dixit Dominus : cujus ignis est in Sion et caminus ejus in Jerusalem.]
      EN: His strength will pass away in terror, and his fleeing rulers will be afraid, says the Lord, whose fire is in Zion and whose furnace is in Jerusalem.

ISAIAH 32

32:1  LA: Ecce in justitia regnabit rex, et principes in judicio præerunt.
      EN: Behold, a king will reign in righteousness, and rulers will govern with judgment.

32:2  LA: Et erit vir sicut qui absconditur a vento, et celat se a tempestate ; sicut rivi aquarum in siti, et umbra petræ prominentis in terra deserta.
      EN: A man will be like one sheltered from the wind, hiding from the storm, like streams of water in a parched place and the shadow of a projecting rock in a deserted land.

32:3  LA: Non caligabunt oculi videntium, et aures audientium diligenter auscultabunt.
      EN: The eyes of those who see will not grow dim, and the ears of those who hear will listen attentively.

32:4  LA: Et cor stultorum intelliget scientiam, et lingua balborum velociter loquetur et plane.
      EN: The heart of fools will grasp knowledge, and the tongue of stammerers will speak swiftly and clearly.

32:5  LA: Non vocabitur ultra is qui insipiens est, princeps, neque fraudulentus appellabitur major ;
      EN: The senseless man will no longer be called a ruler, nor will the deceitful be called great.

32:6  LA: stultus enim fatua loquetur, et cor ejus faciet iniquitatem, ut perficiat simulationem, et loquatur ad Dominum fraudulenter, et vacuam faciat animam esurientis, et potum sitienti auferat.
      EN: For the fool will speak foolish things, and his heart will work iniquity, practicing hypocrisy and speaking deceitfully to the Lord, leaving the hungry person's appetite empty and taking drink away from the thirsty.

32:7  LA: Fraudulenti vasa pessima sunt ; ipse enim cogitationes concinnavit ad perdendos mites in sermone mendacii, cum loqueretur pauper judicium.
      EN: The deceitful man's instruments are most evil, for he has devised schemes to destroy the meek with lying speech when the poor plead for justice.

32:8  LA: Princeps vero ea quæ digna sunt principe cogitabit, et ipse super duces stabit.]
      EN: But the ruler will devise things worthy of a ruler, and he himself will stand above leaders.

32:9  LA: [Mulieres opulentæ, surgite, et audite vocem meam ; filiæ confidentes, percipite auribus eloquium meum.
      EN: Wealthy women, rise and hear my voice; confident daughters, listen to my speech.

32:10  LA: Post dies enim et annum, vos conturbabimini confidentes ; consummata est enim vindemia, collectio ultra non veniet.
       EN: For after a year and some days you confident ones will be troubled, because the vintage has ended and the gathering will come no more.

32:11  LA: Obstupescite, opulentæ ; conturbamini, confidentes : exuite vos et confundimini ; accingite lumbos vestros.
       EN: Be stunned, wealthy women; be troubled, confident ones. Strip yourselves and be ashamed; gird your loins.

32:12  LA: Super ubera plangite, super regione desiderabili, super vinea fertili.
       EN: Beat your breasts over the desirable country, over the fruitful vineyard.

32:13  LA: Super humum populi mei spinæ et vepres ascendent : quanto magis super omnes domos gaudii civitatis exultantis !
       EN: Thorns and briers will grow over the soil of My people—how much more over all the houses of joy in the exultant city!

32:14  LA: Domus enim dimissa est, multitudo urbis relicta est, tenebræ et palpatio factæ sunt super speluncas usque in æternum ; gaudium onagrorum, pascua gregum.
       EN: For the house has been abandoned and the city's multitude forsaken. Darkness and groping have come over its caves forever, a delight for wild donkeys, a pasture for flocks,

32:15  LA: Donec effundatur super nos spiritus de excelso, et erit desertum in carmel, et carmel in saltum reputabitur.
       EN: until the spirit is poured upon us from on high. The wilderness will become a fruitful field, and a fruitful field will be regarded as a forest.

32:16  LA: Et habitabit in solitudine judicium, et justitia in carmel sedebit.
       EN: Judgment will dwell in the wilderness, and righteousness will abide in the fruitful field.

32:17  LA: Et erit opus justitiæ pax, et cultus justitiæ silentium, et securitas usque in sempiternum.
       EN: The work of righteousness will be peace, and the practice of righteousness quietness and security forever.

32:18  LA: Et sedebit populus meus in pulchritudine pacis, et in tabernaculis fiduciæ, et in requie opulenta.
       EN: My people will dwell in the beauty of peace, in secure tents and in abundant rest.

32:19  LA: Grando autem in descensione saltus, et humilitate humiliabitur civitas.
       EN: But hail will fall when the forest comes down, and the city will be utterly humbled.

32:20  LA: Beati qui seminatis super omnes aquas, immittentes pedem bovis et asini.]
       EN: Blessed are you who sow beside all waters, letting the foot of ox and donkey roam.

ISAIAH 33

33:1  LA: Væ qui prædaris ! nonne et ipse prædaberis ? et qui spernis, nonne et ipse sperneris ? Cum consummaveris deprædationem, deprædaberis ; cum fatigatus desieris contemnere, contemneris.
      EN: Woe to you who plunder! Will you not yourself be plundered? And you who despise, will you not yourself be despised? When you have finished plundering, you will be plundered; when you have grown weary and ceased despising, you will be despised.

33:2  LA: Domine, miserere nostri, te enim exspectavimus ; esto brachium nostrum in mane, et salus nostra in tempore tribulationis.
      EN: Lord, have mercy on us, for we have waited for You. Be our arm in the morning and our salvation in time of distress.

33:3  LA: A voce angeli fugerunt populi, et ab exaltatione tua dispersæ sunt gentes.
      EN: At the voice of the angel peoples have fled, and at Your rising nations have been scattered.

33:4  LA: Et congregabuntur spolia vestra sicut colligitur bruchus, velut cum fossæ plenæ fuerint de eo.
      EN: Your spoils will be gathered as the locust is gathered, as when trenches have been filled with it.

33:5  LA: Magnificatus est Dominus, quoniam habitavit in excelso ; implevit Sion judicio et justitia.
      EN: The Lord is magnified, for He has dwelt on high; He has filled Zion with judgment and righteousness.

33:6  LA: Et erit fides in temporibus tuis : divitiæ salutis sapientia et scientia ; timor Domini ipse est thesaurus ejus.
      EN: There will be faithfulness in your times; wisdom and knowledge will be riches of salvation. The fear of the Lord is its treasure.

33:7  LA: Ecce videntes clamabunt foris ; angeli pacis amare flebunt.
      EN: Behold, those who see will cry out outside; messengers of peace will weep bitterly.

33:8  LA: Dissipatæ sunt viæ, cessavit transiens per semitam : irritum factum est pactum, projecit civitates, non reputavit homines.
      EN: The roads are desolate; the traveler on the path has ceased. The covenant has been annulled; he has cast aside cities and taken no account of people.

33:9  LA: Luxit et elanguit terra ; confusus est Libanus, et obsorduit : et factus est Saron sicut desertum, et concussa est Basan, et Carmelus.
      EN: The land has mourned and languished; Lebanon is put to shame and befouled. Sharon has become like a wilderness, and Bashan and Carmel have been shaken.

33:10  LA: Nunc consurgam, dicit Dominus ; nunc exaltabor, nunc sublevabor.
       EN: Now I will arise, says the Lord; now I will be exalted, now I will be lifted up.

33:11  LA: Concipietis ardorem, parietis stipulam ; spiritus vester ut ignis vorabit vos.
       EN: You will conceive heat and give birth to stubble; your own breath will devour you like fire.

33:12  LA: Et erunt populi quasi de incendio cinis ; spinæ congregatæ igni comburentur.
       EN: The peoples will be like ash from a burning; gathered thorns will be burned with fire.

33:13  LA: Audite, qui longe estis, quæ fecerim ; et cognoscite, vicini, fortitudinem meam.
       EN: Hear what I have done, you who are far away; acknowledge My strength, you who are near.

33:14  LA: Conterriti sunt in Sion peccatores ; possedit tremor hypocritas. Quis poterit habitare de vobis cum igne devorante ? quis habitabit ex vobis cum ardoribus sempiternis ?
       EN: Sinners in Zion are terrified; trembling has seized the hypocrites. Who among you can dwell with devouring fire? Who among you can dwell with everlasting flames?

33:15  LA: Qui ambulat in justitiis et loquitur veritatem, qui projicit avaritiam ex calumnia, et excutit manus suas ab omni munere, qui obturat aures suas ne audiat sanguinem, et claudit oculos suos ne videat malum.
       EN: The one who walks in righteous deeds and speaks truth, who rejects gain from oppression and shakes every gift from his hands, who stops his ears from hearing of bloodshed and closes his eyes to avoid seeing evil.

33:16  LA: Iste in excelsis habitabit ; munimenta saxorum sublimitas ejus : panis ei datus est, aquæ ejus fideles sunt.
       EN: He will dwell on the heights; fortresses of rock will be his high refuge. Bread has been given to him, and his waters are sure.

33:17  LA: Regem in decore suo videbunt oculi ejus, cernent terram de longe.
       EN: His eyes will see the king in his beauty; they will discern the land from afar.

33:18  LA: Cor tuum meditabitur timorem : ubi est litteratus ? ubi legis verba ponderans ? ubi doctor parvulorum ?
       EN: Your heart will ponder terror: Where is the learned man? Where is the one who weighs the words of the law? Where is the teacher of little children?

33:19  LA: Populum impudentem non videbis, populum alti sermonis, ita ut non possis intelligere disertitudinem linguæ ejus, in quo nulla est sapientia.
       EN: You will not see the shameless people, the people of lofty speech, whose eloquent tongue you cannot understand, in whom there is no wisdom.

33:20  LA: Respice, Sion, civitatem solemnitatis nostræ : oculi tui videbunt Jerusalem, habitationem opulentam, tabernaculum quod nequaquam transferri poterit ; nec auferentur clavi ejus in sempiternum, et omnes funiculi ejus non rumpentur :
       EN: Look, Zion, upon the city of our solemn festival. Your eyes will see Jerusalem, a rich dwelling, a tent that can never be moved. Its pegs will never be taken away, and none of its ropes will break,

33:21  LA: quia solummodo ibi magnificus est Dominus noster : locus fluviorum rivi latissimi et patentes : non transibit per eum navis remigum, neque trieris magna transgredietur eum.
       EN: for there alone our Lord is magnificent: a place of rivers, of very broad and open streams. No ship of oarsmen will pass through it, nor will a great galley cross it.

33:22  LA: Dominus enim judex noster, Dominus legifer noster, Dominus rex noster, ipse salvabit nos.
       EN: For the Lord is our judge, the Lord our lawgiver, the Lord our king; He Himself will save us.

33:23  LA: Laxati sunt funiculi tui, et non prævalebunt ; sic erit malus tuus ut dilatare signum non queas. Tunc dividentur spolia prædarum multarum ; claudi diripient rapinam.
       EN: Your ropes have slackened and will not hold firm; your mast will be in such a state that you cannot spread the ensign. Then the spoils of much plunder will be divided; the lame will seize the booty.

33:24  LA: Nec dicet vicinus : Elangui ; populus qui habitat in ea, auferetur ab eo iniquitas.]
       EN: No neighbor will say, I have grown weak. The people dwelling in her will have their iniquity taken away.

ISAIAH 34

34:1  LA: Accedite , gentes, et audite ; et populi, attendite : audiat terra, et plenitudo ejus ; orbis, et omne germen ejus.
      EN: Draw near, nations, and hear; peoples, pay attention. Let the earth hear, and all that fills it, the world and everything it brings forth.

34:2  LA: Quia indignatio Domini super omnes gentes, et furor super universam militiam eorum : interfecit eos, et dedit eos in occisionem.
      EN: For the Lord's indignation is against all nations, and His fury against all their armies. He has slain them and given them over to slaughter.

34:3  LA: Interfecti eorum projicientur, et de cadaveribus eorum ascendet fœtor ; tabescent montes a sanguine eorum.
      EN: Their slain will be cast out, and a stench will rise from their corpses; the mountains will melt with their blood.

34:4  LA: Et tabescet omnis militia cælorum, et complicabuntur sicut liber cæli : et omnis militia eorum defluet, sicut defluit folium de vinea et de ficu.
      EN: All the host of heaven will waste away, and the heavens will be rolled up like a scroll. All their host will fall, as a leaf falls from the vine and the fig tree.

34:5  LA: Quoniam inebriatus est in cælo gladius meus ; ecce super Idumæam descendet, et super populum interfectionis meæ, ad judicium.
      EN: For My sword has drunk its fill in heaven. Look, it will descend upon Edom, upon the people I have appointed for slaughter, for judgment.

34:6  LA: Gladius Domini repletus est sanguine, incrassatus est adipe, de sanguine agnorum et hircorum, de sanguine medullatorum arietum : victima enim Domini in Bosra, et interfectio magna in terra Edom.
      EN: The Lord's sword is filled with blood, fattened with fat, with the blood of lambs and goats, with the blood of well-fed rams. For the Lord has a sacrifice in Bozrah and a great slaughter in the land of Edom.

34:7  LA: Et descendent unicornes cum eis, et tauri cum potentibus ; inebriabitur terra eorum sanguine, et humus eorum adipe pinguium.
      EN: Unicorns will fall with them, and bulls with the mighty ones. Their land will drink its fill of blood, and their soil of the fat of the well-fed.

34:8  LA: Quia dies ultionis Domini, annus retributionum judicii Sion.
      EN: For it is the Lord's day of vengeance, the year of recompense for Zion's cause.

34:9  LA: Et convertentur torrentes ejus in picem, et humus ejus in sulphur ; et erit terra ejus in picem ardentem.
      EN: Its streams will be turned into pitch and its soil into sulfur; its land will become burning pitch.

34:10  LA: Nocte et die non extinguetur, in sempiternum ascendet fumus ejus, a generatione in generationem desolabitur, in sæcula sæculorum non erit transiens per eam.
       EN: Night and day it will not be extinguished; its smoke will rise forever. From generation to generation it will lie desolate; forever and ever no one will pass through it.

34:11  LA: Et possidebunt illam onocrotalus et ericius ; ibis et corvus habitabunt in ea : et extendetur super eam mensura, ut redigatur ad nihilum, et perpendiculum in desolationem.
       EN: Pelican and hedgehog will possess it; ibis and raven will dwell there. A measuring line will be stretched over it to reduce it to nothing, and a plummet for desolation.

34:12  LA: Nobiles ejus non erunt ibi ; regem potius invocabunt, et omnes principes ejus erunt in nihilum.
       EN: Its nobles will not be there; instead they will call upon a king, and all its princes will come to nothing.

34:13  LA: Et orientur in domibus ejus spinæ et urticæ, et paliurus in munitionibus ejus ; et erit cubile draconum, et pascua struthionum.
       EN: Thorns and nettles will grow in its houses, and brambles in its fortifications. It will be a lair of dragons and a pasture for ostriches.

34:14  LA: Et occurrent dæmonia onocentauris, et pilosus clamabit alter ad alterum ; ibi cubavit lamia, et invenit sibi requiem.
       EN: Demons will meet donkey-centaurs, and one shaggy creature will call to another. There the lamia has lain down and found rest for herself.

34:15  LA: Ibi habuit foveam ericius, et enutrivit catulos, et circumfodit, et fovit in umbra ejus ; illuc congregati sunt milvi, alter ad alterum.
       EN: There the hedgehog made its burrow, reared its young, dug around, and cherished them in its shade. There the kites gathered, each with its mate.

34:16  LA: Requirite diligenter in libro Domini, et legite : Unum ex eis non defuit, alter alterum non quæsivit ; quia quod ex ore meo procedit, ille mandavit, et spiritus ejus ipse congregavit ea.
       EN: Search carefully in the book of the Lord and read: Not one of them was missing; none had to seek its mate. For He commanded what comes from My mouth, and His Spirit Himself gathered them.

34:17  LA: Et ipse misit eis sortem, et manus ejus divisit eam illis in mensuram : usque in æternum possidebunt eam ; in generationem et generationem habitabunt in ea.]
       EN: He Himself cast the lot for them, and His hand divided it among them by measure. They will possess it forever; from generation to generation they will dwell there.

ISAIAH 35

35:1  LA: Lætabitur deserta et invia, et exsultabit solitudo, et florebit quasi lilium.
      EN: The deserted and trackless land will rejoice; the wilderness will exult and blossom like a lily.

35:2  LA: Germinans germinabit, et exsultabit lætabunda et laudans : gloria Libani data est ei, decor Carmeli et Saron ; ipsi videbunt gloriam Domini, et decorem Dei nostri.
      EN: It will blossom abundantly and exult with gladness and praise. The glory of Lebanon has been given to it, the beauty of Carmel and Sharon. They will see the glory of the Lord and the beauty of our God.

35:3  LA: Confortate manus dissolutas, et genua debilia roborate.
      EN: Strengthen the feeble hands and make firm the weak knees.

35:4  LA: Dicite pusillanimis : Confortamini, et nolite timere : ecce Deus vester ultionem adducet retributionis ; Deus ipse veniet, et salvabit vos.
      EN: Say to the fainthearted, “Take courage and do not fear. Look, your God will bring the vengeance of recompense. God Himself will come and save you.”

35:5  LA: Tunc aperientur oculi cæcorum, et aures surdorum patebunt ;
      EN: Then the eyes of the blind will be opened, and the ears of the deaf will be unstopped.

35:6  LA: tunc saliet sicut cervus claudus, et aperta erit lingua mutorum : quia scissæ sunt in deserto aquæ, et torrentes in solitudine ;
      EN: Then the lame will leap like a deer, and the tongue of the mute will be loosened, for waters have burst forth in the desert and streams in the wilderness.

35:7  LA: et quæ erat arida, erit in stagnum, et sitiens in fontes aquarum. In cubilibus, in quibus prius dracones habitabant, orietur viror calami et junci.
      EN: The parched ground will become a pool, and the thirsty land springs of water. In the lairs where dragons formerly dwelt, green reeds and rushes will grow.

35:8  LA: Et erit ibi semita et via, et via sancta vocabitur : non transibit per eam pollutus, et hæc erit vobis directa via, ita ut stulti non errent per eam.
      EN: There will be a path and a road there, and it will be called the Holy Way. No unclean person will pass along it; this will be a straight road for you, so that even fools will not stray along it.

35:9  LA: Non erit ibi leo, et mala bestia non ascendet per eam, nec invenietur ibi ; et ambulabunt qui liberati fuerint.
      EN: No lion will be there, nor will a harmful beast go up on it or be found there. Those who have been delivered will walk there.

35:10  LA: Et redempti a Domino convertentur, et venient in Sion cum laude, et lætitia sempiterna super caput eorum : gaudium et lætitiam obtinebunt, et fugiet dolor et gemitus.]
       EN: Those redeemed by the Lord will return and come to Zion with praise, with everlasting gladness upon their heads. They will obtain joy and gladness, and sorrow and sighing will flee away.

ISAIAH 36

36:1  LA: Et factum est in quartodecimo anno regis Ezechiæ, ascendit Sennacherib, rex Assyriorum, super omnes civitates Juda munitas, et cepit eas.
      EN: In the fourteenth year of King Hezekiah, Sennacherib king of the Assyrians advanced against all the fortified cities of Judah and captured them.

36:2  LA: Et misit rex Assyriorum Rabsacen de Lachis in Jerusalem, ad regem Ezechiam in manu gravi : et stetit in aquæductu piscinæ superioris in via Agri fullonis.
      EN: The king of the Assyrians sent the Rabshakeh from Lachish to Jerusalem, to King Hezekiah, with a great force. He stood at the aqueduct of the upper pool, on the road to the Fuller's Field.

36:3  LA: Et egressus est ad eum Eliacim, filius Helciæ, qui erat super domum, et Sobna scriba, et Joahe filius Asaph, a commentariis.
      EN: Eliakim son of Hilkiah, who was over the household, Shebna the scribe, and Joah son of Asaph, the recorder, went out to him.

36:4  LA: Et dixit ad eos Rabsaces : Dicite Ezechiæ : Hæc dicit rex magnus, rex Assyriorum : Quæ est ista fiducia qua confidis ?
      EN: The Rabshakeh said to them, “Tell Hezekiah: Thus says the great king, the king of the Assyrians: What is this confidence in which you trust?

36:5  LA: aut quo consilio vel fortitudine rebellare disponis ? super quem habes fiduciam, quia recessisti a me ?
      EN: With what strategy or strength do you intend to rebel? In whom do you trust, that you have withdrawn from me?

36:6  LA: Ecce confidis super baculum arundineum confractum istum, super Ægyptum ; cui si innixus fuerit homo, intrabit in manum ejus, et perforabit eam : sic Pharao, rex Ægypti, omnibus qui confidunt in eo.
      EN: Look, you trust in this broken reed of a staff, Egypt. If a man leans upon it, it will enter his hand and pierce it. Such is Pharaoh king of Egypt to all who trust in him.

36:7  LA: Quod si responderis mihi : In Domino Deo nostro confidimus ; nonne ipse est cujus abstulit Ezechias excelsa et altaria, et dixit Judæ et Jerusalem : Coram altari isto adorabitis ?
      EN: But if you answer me, ‘We trust in the Lord our God,’ is He not the one whose high places and altars Hezekiah removed, saying to Judah and Jerusalem, ‘You shall worship before this altar’?

36:8  LA: Et nunc trade te domino meo, regi Assyriorum, et dabo tibi duo millia equorum, nec poteris ex te præbere ascensores eorum :
      EN: Now surrender to my master, the king of the Assyrians, and I will give you two thousand horses, yet you will not be able to provide riders for them yourselves.

36:9  LA: et quomodo sustinebis faciem judicis unius loci ex servis domini mei minoribus ? Quod si confidis in Ægypto, in quadrigis et in equitibus,
      EN: How then will you withstand even a single governor of one place among the least of my master's servants? And if you trust in Egypt for chariots and horsemen,

36:10  LA: et nunc numquid sine Domino ascendi ad terram istam, ut disperderem eam ? Dominus dixit ad me : Ascende super terram istam, et disperde eam.
       EN: have I now advanced against this land to destroy it without the Lord? The Lord said to me, ‘Go up against this land and destroy it.’”

36:11  LA: Et dixit Eliacim, et Sobna, et Joahe, ad Rabsacen : Loquere ad servos tuos syra lingua ; intelligimus enim ; ne loquaris ad nos judaice in auribus populi qui est super murum.
       EN: Eliakim, Shebna, and Joah said to the Rabshakeh, “Speak to your servants in Aramaic, for we understand it. Do not speak to us in the language of Judah within the hearing of the people on the wall.”

36:12  LA: Et dixit ad eos Rabsaces : Numquid ad dominum tuum et ad te misit me dominus meus, ut loquerer omnia verba ista ? et non potius ad viros qui sedent in muro, ut comedant stercora sua, et bibant urinam pedum suorum vobiscum ?
       EN: The Rabshakeh said to them, “Did my master send me to your master and to you to speak all these words, and not rather to the men sitting on the wall, who will eat their own dung and drink the urine from between their feet along with you?”

36:13  LA: Et stetit Rabsaces, et clamavit voce magna judaice, et dixit : Audite verba regis magni, regis Assyriorum !
       EN: The Rabshakeh stood and cried out in a loud voice in the language of Judah, saying, “Hear the words of the great king, the king of the Assyrians!

36:14  LA: Hæc dicit rex : Non seducat vos Ezechias, quia non poterit eruere vos.
       EN: Thus says the king: Do not let Hezekiah deceive you, for he will not be able to rescue you.

36:15  LA: Et non vobis tribuat fiduciam Ezechias super Domino, dicens : Eruens liberabit nos Dominus : non dabitur civitas ista in manu regis Assyriorum.
       EN: Do not let Hezekiah give you confidence in the Lord by saying, ‘The Lord will surely deliver us; this city will not be given into the hand of the king of the Assyrians.’

36:16  LA: Nolite audire Ezechiam ; hæc enim dicit rex Assyriorum : Facite mecum benedictionem, et egredimini ad me, et comedite unusquisque vineam suam, et unusquisque ficum suam, et bibite unusquisque aquam cisternæ suæ,
       EN: Do not listen to Hezekiah, for thus says the king of the Assyrians: Come to terms with me and come out to me. Let each of you eat from his own vine and his own fig tree, and drink the water of his own cistern,

36:17  LA: donec veniam, et tollam vos ad terram quæ est ut terra vestra, terram frumenti et vini, terram panum et vinearum.
       EN: until I come and take you to a land like your own, a land of grain and wine, a land of bread and vineyards.

36:18  LA: Nec conturbet vos Ezechias, dicens : Dominus liberabit nos. Numquid liberaverunt dii gentium unusquisque terram suam de manu regis Assyriorum ?
       EN: Do not let Hezekiah unsettle you by saying, ‘The Lord will deliver us.’ Have the gods of the nations each delivered his own land from the hand of the king of the Assyrians?

36:19  LA: Ubi est deus Emath et Arphad ? ubi est deus Sepharvaim ? numquid liberaverunt Samariam de manu mea ?
       EN: Where is the god of Hamath and Arpad? Where is the god of Sepharvaim? Have they delivered Samaria from my hand?

36:20  LA: Quis est ex omnibus diis terrarum istarum qui eruerit terram suam de manu mea, ut eruat Dominus Jerusalem de manu mea ?
       EN: Which of all the gods of these lands has rescued his land from my hand, that the Lord should rescue Jerusalem from my hand?”

36:21  LA: Et siluerunt, et non responderunt ei verbum. Mandaverat enim rex, dicens : Ne respondeatis ei.
       EN: They remained silent and did not answer him a word, for the king had commanded, “Do not answer him.”

36:22  LA: Et ingressus est Eliacim, filius Helciæ, qui erat super domum, et Sobna scriba, et Joahe filius Asaph, a commentariis, ad Ezechiam, scissis vestibus, et nuntiaverunt ei verba Rabsacis.
       EN: Eliakim son of Hilkiah, who was over the household, Shebna the scribe, and Joah son of Asaph, the recorder, came to Hezekiah with their garments torn and reported the Rabshakeh's words to him.

ISAIAH 37

37:1  LA: Et factum est, cum audisset rex Ezechias, scidit vestimenta sua, et obvolutus est sacco, et intravit in domum Domini.
      EN: When King Hezekiah heard this, he tore his garments, wrapped himself in sackcloth, and entered the house of the Lord.

37:2  LA: Et misit Eliacim, qui erat super domum, et Sobnam scribam, et seniores de sacerdotibus, opertos saccis, ad Isaiam, filium Amos, prophetam,
      EN: He sent Eliakim, who was over the household, Shebna the scribe, and the elders of the priests, covered in sackcloth, to Isaiah the prophet, son of Amoz.

37:3  LA: et dixerunt ad eum : Hæc dicit Ezechias : Dies tribulationis, et correptionis, et blasphemiæ, dies hæc ; quia venerunt filii usque ad partum, et virtus non est pariendi.
      EN: They said to him, “Thus says Hezekiah: This day is a day of distress, rebuke, and blasphemy, for the children have come to the point of birth, but there is no strength to give birth.

37:4  LA: Si quomodo audiat Dominus Deus tuus verba Rabsacis, quem misit rex Assyriorum dominus suus ad blasphemandum Deum viventem et exprobrandum sermonibus quos audivit Dominus Deus tuus : leva ergo orationem pro reliquiis quæ repertæ sunt.
      EN: Perhaps the Lord your God will hear the words of the Rabshakeh, whom his master, the king of the Assyrians, sent to blaspheme the living God, and to taunt Him with words the Lord your God has heard. Therefore lift up a prayer for the remnant that is left.”

37:5  LA: Et venerunt servi regis Ezechiæ ad Isaiam.
      EN: King Hezekiah's servants came to Isaiah.

37:6  LA: Et dixit ad eos Isaias : Hæc dicetis domino vestro : Hæc dicit Dominus : Ne timeas a facie verborum quæ audisti, quibus blasphemaverunt pueri regis Assyriorum me.
      EN: Isaiah said to them, “You shall say to your master: Thus says the Lord: Do not be afraid of the words you have heard, with which the servants of the king of the Assyrians have blasphemed Me.

37:7  LA: Ecce ego dabo ei spiritum, et audiet nuntium, et revertetur ad terram suam, et corruere eum faciam gladio in terra sua.
      EN: Look, I will put a spirit within him, and he will hear a report and return to his own land, and I will make him fall by the sword in his own land.”

37:8  LA: Reversus est autem Rabsaces, et invenit regem Assyriorum præliantem adversus Lobnam : audierat enim quia profectus esset de Lachis.
      EN: The Rabshakeh returned and found the king of the Assyrians fighting against Libnah, for he had heard that he had departed from Lachish.

37:9  LA: Et audivit de Tharaca rege Æthiopiæ, dicentes : Egressus est ut pugnet contra te. Quod cum audisset, misit nuntios ad Ezechiam, dicens :
      EN: The king heard it said of Tirhakah king of Ethiopia, “He has come out to fight against you.” When he heard this, he sent messengers to Hezekiah, saying:

37:10  LA: Hæc dicetis Ezechiæ regi Judæ, loquentes : Non te decipiat Deus tuus in quo tu confidis, dicens : Non dabitur Jerusalem in manu regis Assyriorum.
       EN: “Say this to Hezekiah king of Judah: Do not let your God in whom you trust deceive you by saying, ‘Jerusalem will not be given into the hand of the king of the Assyrians.’

37:11  LA: Ecce tu audisti omnia quæ fecerunt reges Assyriorum omnibus terris, quas subverterunt : et tu poteris liberari ?
       EN: Look, you have heard all that the kings of the Assyrians have done to all the lands they overthrew. And will you be able to escape?

37:12  LA: Numquid eruerunt eos dii gentium quos subverterunt patres mei, Gozam, et Haram, et Reseph, et filios Eden qui erant in Thalassar ?
       EN: Did the gods of the nations that my fathers overthrew rescue them—Gozan, Haran, Rezeph, and the people of Eden who were in Telassar?

37:13  LA: Ubi est rex Emath, et rex Arphad, et rex urbis Sepharvaim, Ana, et Ava ?
       EN: Where is the king of Hamath, the king of Arpad, the king of the city of Sepharvaim, of Hena and Ivvah?”

37:14  LA: Et tulit Ezechias libros de manu nuntiorum, et legit eos, et ascendit in domum Domini, et expandit eos Ezechias coram Domino :
       EN: Hezekiah took the letters from the messengers' hands and read them. He went up to the house of the Lord and spread them out before the Lord.

37:15  LA: et oravit Ezechias ad Dominum, dicens :
       EN: Hezekiah prayed to the Lord, saying:

37:16  LA: Domine exercituum, Deus Israël, qui sedes super cherubim, tu es Deus solus omnium regnorum terræ : tu fecisti cælum et terram.
       EN: “Lord of hosts, God of Israel, who sit above the cherubim, You alone are God over all the kingdoms of the earth. You made heaven and earth.

37:17  LA: Inclina, Domine, aurem tuam, et audi ; aperi, Domine, oculos tuos, et vide : et audi omnia verba Sennacherib, quæ misit ad blasphemandum Deum viventem.
       EN: Incline Your ear, Lord, and hear; open Your eyes, Lord, and see. Hear all the words Sennacherib has sent to blaspheme the living God.

37:18  LA: Vere enim, Domine, desertas fecerunt reges Assyriorum terras, et regiones earum,
       EN: Truly, Lord, the kings of the Assyrians have laid waste the lands and their territories,

37:19  LA: et dederunt deos earum igni : non enim erant dii, sed opera manuum hominum, lignum et lapis, et comminuerunt eos.
       EN: and thrown their gods into the fire, for they were not gods, but the work of human hands, wood and stone; and they destroyed them.

37:20  LA: Et nunc, Domine Deus noster, salva nos de manu ejus, et cognoscant omnia regna terræ quia tu es Dominus solus.
       EN: Now, Lord our God, save us from his hand, so that all the kingdoms of the earth may know that You alone are the Lord.”

37:21  LA: Et misit Isaias, filius Amos, ad Ezechiam, dicens : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus Israël : Pro quibus rogasti me de Sennacherib, rege Assyriorum,
       EN: Isaiah son of Amoz sent word to Hezekiah, saying: Thus says the Lord, the God of Israel: Concerning what you asked Me about Sennacherib king of the Assyrians,

37:22  LA: hoc est verbum quod locutus est Dominus super eum : [Despexit te et subsannavit te, virgo filia Sion ; post te caput movit, filia Jerusalem.
       EN: this is the word the Lord has spoken against him: Virgin daughter Zion has despised and mocked you; daughter Jerusalem has shaken her head behind you.

37:23  LA: Cui exprobrasti ? et quem blasphemasti ? et super quem exaltasti vocem, et levasti altitudinem oculorum tuorum ? ad Sanctum Israël.
       EN: Whom have you taunted and blasphemed? Against whom have you raised your voice and lifted your eyes on high? Against the Holy One of Israel.

37:24  LA: In manu servorum tuorum exprobrasti Domino, et dixisti : In multitudine quadrigarum mearum ego ascendi altitudinem montium juga Libani ; et succidam excelsa cedrorum ejus, et electas abietes illius, et introibo altitudinem summitatis ejus, saltum Carmeli ejus.
       EN: Through your servants you have taunted the Lord and said, “With my many chariots I have climbed the mountain heights, the ridges of Lebanon. I will cut down its lofty cedars and choice firs, and enter its highest summit, the forest of its Carmel.

37:25  LA: Ego fodi, et bibi aquam, et exsiccavi vestigio pedis mei omnes rivos aggerum.
       EN: I have dug and drunk water, and with the sole of my foot dried up all the streams enclosed by embankments.”

37:26  LA: Numquid non audisti quæ olim fecerim ei ? Ex diebus antiquis ego plasmavi illud ; et nunc adduxi, et factum est in eradicationem collium compugnantium, et civitatum munitarum.
       EN: Have you not heard what I did to it long ago? From ancient days I formed it; now I have brought it about, and it has become the uprooting of battling hills and fortified cities.

37:27  LA: Habitatores earum breviata manu contremuerunt, et confusi sunt. Facti sunt sicut fœnum agri, et gramen pascuæ, et herba tectorum, quæ exaruit antequam maturesceret.
       EN: Their inhabitants, powerless, trembled and were put to shame. They became like grass in a field, green pasture, and grass on rooftops that withered before it ripened.

37:28  LA: Habitationem tuam, et egressum tuum, et introitum tuum cognovi, et insaniam tuam contra me.
       EN: I know your dwelling, your going out and your coming in, and your raging against Me.

37:29  LA: Cum fureres adversum me, superbia tua ascendit in aures meas. Ponam ergo circulum in naribus tuis, et frenum in labiis tuis, et reducam te in viam per quem venisti.]
       EN: When you raged against Me, your pride rose into My ears. Therefore I will put a ring in your nostrils and a bridle on your lips, and lead you back by the road on which you came.

37:30  LA: Tibi autem hoc erit signum : comede hoc anno quæ sponte nascuntur, et in anno secundo pomis vescere ; in anno autem tertio seminate et metite, et plantate vineas, et comedite fructum earum.
       EN: This will be the sign for you: This year eat what grows by itself, and in the second year feed on the fruit; but in the third year sow and reap, plant vineyards and eat their fruit.

37:31  LA: Et mittet id quod salvatum fuerit de domo Juda, et quod reliquum est, radicem deorsum, et faciet fructum sursum :
       EN: What is saved of the house of Judah, the remnant, will send roots downward and bear fruit upward.

37:32  LA: quia de Jerusalem exibunt reliquiæ, et salvatio de monte Sion : zelus Domini exercituum faciet istud.
       EN: For a remnant will go out from Jerusalem, and deliverance from Mount Zion. The zeal of the Lord of hosts will accomplish this.

37:33  LA: Propterea hæc dicit Dominus de rege Assyriorum : [Non intrabit civitatem hanc, et non jaciet ibi sagittam, et non occupabit eam clypeus, et non mittet in circuitu ejus aggerem.
       EN: Therefore thus says the Lord concerning the king of the Assyrians: He will not enter this city or shoot an arrow there; no shield will seize it, nor will he heap up a siege ramp around it.

37:34  LA: In via qua venit, per eam revertetur, et civitatem hanc non ingredietur, dicit Dominus.
       EN: By the road on which he came, by that road he will return, and he will not enter this city, says the Lord.

37:35  LA: Et protegam civitatem istam, ut salvem eam propter me, et propter David, servum meum.]
       EN: I will protect this city to save it for My own sake and for the sake of David My servant.

37:36  LA: Egressus est autem angelus Domini, et percussit in castris Assyriorum centum octoginta quinque millia. Et surrexerunt mane, et ecce omnes cadavera mortuorum.
       EN: The angel of the Lord went out and struck down one hundred eighty-five thousand in the Assyrian camp. When people rose in the morning, there they all were, dead bodies.

37:37  LA: Et egressus est, et abiit, et reversus est Sennacherib, rex Assyriorum, et habitavit in Ninive.
       EN: Sennacherib king of the Assyrians departed, went away, and returned to Nineveh, where he dwelt.

37:38  LA: Et factum est, cum adoraret in templo Nesroch deum suum, Adramelech et Sarasar, filii ejus, percusserunt eum gladio, fugeruntque in terram Ararat ; et regnavit Asarhaddon, filius ejus, pro eo.
       EN: While he was worshiping his god in the temple of Nisroch, his sons Adrammelech and Sharezer struck him down with the sword and fled to the land of Ararat. His son Esarhaddon reigned in his place.

ISAIAH 38

38:1  LA: In diebus illis ægrotavit Ezechias usque ad mortem ; et introivit ad eum Isaias, filius Amos, propheta, et dixit ei : Hæc dicit Dominus : Dispone domui tuæ, quia morieris tu, et non vives.
      EN: In those days Hezekiah became sick to the point of death. Isaiah the prophet, son of Amoz, came to him and said, “Thus says the Lord: Put your household in order, for you will die and not live.”

38:2  LA: Et convertit Ezechias faciem suam ad parietem, et oravit ad Dominum,
      EN: Hezekiah turned his face toward the wall and prayed to the Lord,

38:3  LA: et dixit : Obsecro, Domine, memento, quæso, quomodo ambulaverim coram te in veritate et in corde perfecto, et quod bonum est in oculis tuis fecerim. Et flevit Ezechias fletu magno.
      EN: saying, “I beg You, Lord, remember, I pray, how I have walked before You in truth and with a whole heart, and have done what is good in Your eyes.” And Hezekiah wept bitterly.

38:4  LA: Et factum est verbum Domini ad Isaiam, dicens :
      EN: The word of the Lord came to Isaiah, saying:

38:5  LA: Vade, et dic Ezechiæ : Hæc dicit Dominus Deus David patris tui : Audivi orationem tuam, et vidi lacrimas tuas ; ecce ego adjiciam super dies tuos quindecim annos,
      EN: “Go and tell Hezekiah: Thus says the Lord, the God of David your father: I have heard your prayer and seen your tears. Look, I will add fifteen years to your days,

38:6  LA: et de manu regis Assyriorum eruam te, et civitatem istam, et protegam eam.
      EN: and rescue you and this city from the hand of the king of the Assyrians, and I will protect it.

38:7  LA: Hoc autem tibi erit signum a Domino, quia faciet Dominus verbum hoc quod locutus est :
      EN: This will be your sign from the Lord that the Lord will accomplish this word He has spoken:

38:8  LA: ecce ego reverti faciam umbram linearum per quas descenderat in horologio Achaz in sole, retrorsum decem lineis. Et reversus est sol decem lineis per gradus quos descenderat.
      EN: Look, I will make the shadow of the marks, which has moved down on Ahaz's sundial in the sunlight, turn backward by ten marks.” And the sun moved back ten marks along the steps by which it had descended.

38:9  LA: Scriptura Ezechiæ, regis Juda, cum ægrotasset et convaluisset de infirmitate sua.
      EN: The writing of Hezekiah king of Judah when he had been sick and had recovered from his illness.

38:10  LA: [Ego dixi in dimidio dierum meorum : Vadam ad portas inferi ; quæsivi residuum annorum meorum.
       EN: I said in the midst of my days, “I shall go to the gates of the underworld.” I sought the remainder of my years.

38:11  LA: Dixi : Non videbo Dominum Deum in terra viventium ; non aspiciam hominem ultra, et habitatorem quietis.
       EN: I said, “I shall not see the Lord God in the land of the living; I shall no longer look upon human beings or the inhabitant of the land of rest.”

38:12  LA: Generatio mea ablata est, et convoluta est a me, quasi tabernaculum pastorum. Præcisa est velut a texente vita mea ; dum adhuc ordirer, succidit me : de mane usque ad vesperam finies me.
       EN: My lifetime has been taken away and rolled up from me like a shepherd's tent. My life has been cut off as by a weaver; while I was still weaving, He cut me down. From morning until evening You will make an end of me.

38:13  LA: Sperabam usque ad mane ; quasi leo, sic contrivit omnia ossa mea : de mane usque ad vesperam finies me.
       EN: I hoped until morning; like a lion He crushed all my bones. From morning until evening You will make an end of me.

38:14  LA: Sicut pullus hirundinis, sic clamabo ; meditabor ut columba. Attenuati sunt oculi mei, suspicientes in excelsum. Domine, vim patior : responde pro me.
       EN: Like a young swallow I will cry; like a dove I will moan. My eyes have grown weak from looking upward. Lord, I suffer violence; answer for me.

38:15  LA: Quid dicam, aut quid respondebit mihi, cum ipse fecerit ? Recogitabo tibi omnes annos meos in amaritudine animæ meæ.
       EN: What shall I say, or what will He answer me, since He Himself has done it? I will ponder before You all my years in the bitterness of my soul.

38:16  LA: Domine, si sic vivitur, et in talibus vita spiritus mei, corripies me, et vivificabis me.
       EN: Lord, if this is how one lives, and in such things is the life of my spirit, You will correct me and restore my life.

38:17  LA: Ecce in pace amaritudo mea amarissima. Tu autem eruisti animam meam ut non periret ; projecisti post tergum tuum omnia peccata mea.
       EN: Look, in peace my bitterness was most bitter. But You rescued my soul from perishing; You cast all my sins behind Your back.

38:18  LA: Quia non infernus confitebitur tibi, neque mors laudabit te : non exspectabunt qui descendunt in lacum veritatem tuam.
       EN: For the underworld will not give You thanks, nor will death praise You; those who go down into the pit will not wait for Your truth.

38:19  LA: Vivens, vivens ipse confitebitur tibi, sicut et ego hodie ; pater filiis notam faciet veritatem tuam.
       EN: The living, the living person will give You thanks, as I do today; a father will make Your truth known to his children.

38:20  LA: Domine, salvum me fac ! et psalmos nostros cantabimus cunctis diebus vitæ nostræ in domo Domini.]
       EN: Lord, save me! We will sing our psalms all the days of our life in the house of the Lord.

38:21  LA: Et jussit Isaias ut tollerent massam de ficis, et cataplasmarent super vulnus, et sanaretur.
       EN: Isaiah ordered them to take a lump of figs and apply it as a poultice to the wound, so that he might be healed.

38:22  LA: Et dixit Ezechias : Quod erit signum quia ascendam in domum Domini ?
       EN: Hezekiah said, “What will be the sign that I shall go up to the house of the Lord?”

ISAIAH 39

39:1  LA: In tempore illo misit Merodach Baladan, filius Baladan, rex Babylonis, libros et munera ad Ezechiam : audierat enim quod ægrotasset et convaluisset.
      EN: At that time Merodach-baladan son of Baladan, king of Babylon, sent letters and gifts to Hezekiah, for he had heard that he had been sick and had recovered.

39:2  LA: Lætatus est autem super eis Ezechias, et ostendit eis cellam aromatum, et argenti, et auri, et odoramentorum, et unguenti optimi, et omnes apothecas supellectilis suæ, et universa quæ inventa sunt in thesauris ejus. Non fuit verbum quod non ostenderet eis Ezechias in domo sua, et in omni potestate sua.
      EN: Hezekiah was pleased with them and showed them the storeroom of spices, silver, gold, perfumes, and the finest ointment, all the storehouses of his furnishings, and everything found in his treasuries. There was nothing in his house or in his whole dominion that Hezekiah did not show them.

39:3  LA: Introivit autem Isaias propheta ad Ezechiam regem, et dixit ei : Quid dixerunt viri isti, et unde venerunt ad te ? Et dixit Ezechias : De terra longinqua venerunt ad me, de Babylone.
      EN: Isaiah the prophet came to King Hezekiah and said to him, “What did these men say, and where did they come from to you?” Hezekiah said, “They came to me from a distant land, from Babylon.”

39:4  LA: Et dixit : Quid viderunt in domo tua ? Et dixit Ezechias : Omnia quæ in domo mea sunt viderunt ; non fuit res quam non ostenderim eis in thesauris meis.
      EN: He said, “What have they seen in your house?” Hezekiah said, “They have seen everything in my house. There was nothing in my treasuries that I did not show them.”

39:5  LA: Et dixit Isaias ad Ezechiam : Audi verbum Domini exercituum.
      EN: Isaiah said to Hezekiah, “Hear the word of the Lord of hosts:

39:6  LA: Ecce dies venient, et auferentur omnia quæ in domo tua sunt, et quæ thesaurizaverunt patres tui usque ad diem hanc, in Babylonem ; non relinquetur quidquam, dicit Dominus.
      EN: Look, days are coming when everything in your house, and everything your fathers have stored up to this day, will be carried away to Babylon. Nothing will be left, says the Lord.

39:7  LA: Et de filiis tuis, qui exibunt de te, quos genueris, tollent, et erunt eunuchi in palatio regis Babylonis.
      EN: Some of your sons who will come from you, whom you will father, will be taken away and become eunuchs in the palace of the king of Babylon.”

39:8  LA: Et dixit Ezechias ad Isaiam : Bonum verbum Domini, quod locutus est. Et dixit : Fiat tantum pax et veritas in diebus meis !
      EN: Hezekiah said to Isaiah, “The word of the Lord that He has spoken is good.” And he said, “Only let there be peace and truth in my days!”

ISAIAH 40

40:1  LA: Consolamini , consolamini, popule meus, dicit Deus vester.
      EN: Be comforted, be comforted, My people, says your God.

40:2  LA: Loquimini ad cor Jerusalem, et advocate eam, quoniam completa est malitia ejus, dimissa est iniquitas illius : suscepit de manu Domini duplicia pro omnibus peccatis suis.
      EN: Speak to the heart of Jerusalem and call to her, for her hardship is completed and her iniquity forgiven. She has received double from the Lord's hand for all her sins.

40:3  LA: Vox clamantis in deserto : Parate viam Domini, rectas facite in solitudine semitas Dei nostri.
      EN: The voice of one crying in the desert: Prepare the way of the Lord; make straight in the wilderness the paths of our God.

40:4  LA: Omnis vallis exaltabitur, et omnis mons et collis humiliabitur, et erunt prava in directa, et aspera in vias planas :
      EN: Every valley will be lifted up, and every mountain and hill brought low. Crooked places will become straight, and rough places level roads.

40:5  LA: et revelabitur gloria Domini, et videbit omnis caro pariter quod os Domini locutum est.
      EN: The Lord's glory will be revealed, and all flesh together will see what the mouth of the Lord has spoken.

40:6  LA: Vox dicentis : Clama. Et dixi : Quid clamabo ? Omnis caro fœnum, et omnis gloria ejus quasi flos agri.
      EN: A voice says, “Cry out.” And I said, “What shall I cry?” All flesh is grass, and all its glory like the flower of the field.

40:7  LA: Exsiccatum est fœnum, et cecidit flos, quia spiritus Domini sufflavit in eo. Vere fœnum est populus :
      EN: The grass has withered and the flower fallen, because the breath of the Lord has blown upon it. Truly the people are grass.

40:8  LA: exsiccatum est fœnum, et cecidit flos ; verbum autem Domini nostri manet in æternum.
      EN: The grass has withered and the flower fallen, but the word of our Lord remains forever.

40:9  LA: Super montem excelsum ascende, tu qui evangelizas Sion ; exalta in fortitudine vocem tuam, qui evangelizas Jerusalem : exalta, noli timere. Dic civitatibus Juda : Ecce Deus vester :
      EN: Go up upon a high mountain, you who bring good news to Zion. Lift up your voice with strength, you who bring good news to Jerusalem; lift it up, do not fear. Say to the cities of Judah, “Look, your God!

40:10  LA: ecce Dominus Deus in fortitudine veniet, et brachium ejus dominabitur : ecce merces ejus cum eo, et opus illius coram illo.
       EN: Look, the Lord God will come with strength, and His arm will rule. Look, His reward is with Him, and His work before Him.

40:11  LA: Sicut pastor gregem suum pascet, in brachio suo congregabit agnos, et in sinu suo levabit ; fœtas ipse portabit.
       EN: Like a shepherd He will feed His flock; He will gather the lambs in His arm and lift them into His bosom. He Himself will carry the ewes with young.”

40:12  LA: Quis mensus est pugillo aquas, et cælos palmo ponderavit ? quis appendit tribus digitis molem terræ, et libravit in pondere montes, et colles in statera ?
       EN: Who has measured the waters in his hollowed hand and weighed the heavens with a span? Who has held the mass of the earth with three fingers and weighed mountains on scales and hills in a balance?

40:13  LA: Quis adjuvit spiritum Domini ? aut quis consiliarius ejus fuit, et ostendit illi ?
       EN: Who has assisted the Spirit of the Lord, or been His counselor and instructed Him?

40:14  LA: cum quo iniit consilium, et instruxit eum, et docuit eum semitam justitiæ, et erudivit eum scientiam, et viam prudentiæ ostendit illi ?
       EN: With whom did He take counsel, and who instructed Him, taught Him the path of justice, imparted knowledge to Him, or showed Him the way of understanding?

40:15  LA: Ecce gentes quasi stilla situlæ, et quasi momentum stateræ reputatæ sunt ; ecce insulæ quasi pulvis exiguus.
       EN: Look, the nations are reckoned like a drop from a bucket and a small weight on a balance. Look, the islands are like a little dust.

40:16  LA: Et Libanus non sufficiet ad succendendum, et animalia ejus non sufficient ad holocaustum.
       EN: Lebanon will not suffice for fuel, nor will its animals suffice for a burnt offering.

40:17  LA: Omnes gentes quasi non sint, sic sunt coram eo, et quasi nihilum et inane reputatæ sunt ei.]
       EN: All nations are as though they did not exist before Him; He reckons them as nothing and emptiness.

40:18  LA: [Cui ergo similem fecisti Deum ? aut quam imaginem ponetis ei ?
       EN: To whom, then, have you likened God, or what likeness will you set up for Him?

40:19  LA: Numquid sculptile conflavit faber ? aut aurifex auro figuravit illud, et laminis argenteis argentarius ?
       EN: Has a craftsman cast a carved image? Has a goldsmith fashioned it in gold, and a silversmith in silver plates?

40:20  LA: Forte lignum et imputribile elegit ; artifex sapiens quærit quomodo statuat simulacrum, quod non moveatur.
       EN: He has chosen strong wood that will not rot; a skilled artisan seeks how to set up an idol that will not move.

40:21  LA: Numquid non scitis ? numquid non audistis ? numquid non annuntiatum est vobis ab initio ? numquid non intellexistis fundamenta terræ ?
       EN: Do you not know? Have you not heard? Has it not been announced to you from the beginning? Have you not understood the foundations of the earth?

40:22  LA: Qui sedet super gyrum terræ, et habitatores ejus sunt quasi locustæ ; qui extendit velut nihilum cælos, et expandit eos sicut tabernaculum ad inhabitandum ;
       EN: He sits above the circle of the earth, and its inhabitants are like locusts. He stretches out the heavens as though they were nothing and spreads them like a tent to dwell in.

40:23  LA: qui dat secretorum scrutatores quasi non sint, judices terræ velut inane fecit.
       EN: He makes searchers of secrets as though they did not exist; He has made the judges of the earth like emptiness.

40:24  LA: Et quidem neque plantatus, neque satus, neque radicatus in terra truncus eorum ; repente flavit in eos, et aruerunt, et turbo quasi stipulam auferet eos.
       EN: Indeed their trunk has been neither planted nor sown nor rooted in the earth; He suddenly blows upon them and they wither; a whirlwind will carry them away like stubble.

40:25  LA: Et cui assimilastis me, et adæquastis ? dicit Sanctus.
       EN: To whom have you likened Me and made Me equal? says the Holy One.

40:26  LA: Levate in excelsum oculos vestros, et videte quis creavit hæc : qui educit in numero militiam eorum, et omnes ex nomine vocat ; præ multitudine fortitudinis et roboris, virtutisque ejus, neque unum reliquum fuit.
       EN: Lift your eyes on high and see who created these things, who leads out their host by number and calls them all by name. Because of His abundant strength, might, and power, not one has been left behind.

40:27  LA: Quare dicis, Jacob, et loqueris, Israël : Abscondita est via mea a Domino, et a Deo meo judicium meum transivit ?
       EN: Why do you say, Jacob, and declare, Israel, “My way is hidden from the Lord, and my cause has passed unnoticed by my God”?

40:28  LA: Numquid nescis, aut non audisti ? Deus sempiternus Dominus, qui creavit terminos terræ : non deficiet, neque laborabit, nec est investigatio sapientiæ ejus.
       EN: Do you not know, or have you not heard? The Lord is the everlasting God, who created the ends of the earth. He will not faint or grow weary; His wisdom cannot be searched out.

40:29  LA: Qui dat lasso virtutem, et his qui non sunt, fortitudinem et robur multiplicat.
       EN: He gives strength to the weary and increases the might and power of those who are as nothing.

40:30  LA: Deficient pueri, et laborabunt, et juvenes in infirmitate cadent ;
       EN: Boys will faint and grow weary, and young men will fall in weakness,

40:31  LA: qui autem sperant in Domino mutabunt fortitudinem, assument pennas sicut aquilæ : current et non laborabunt, ambulabunt et non deficient.]
       EN: but those who hope in the Lord will renew their strength. They will take wings like eagles; they will run and not grow weary, walk and not faint.

ISAIAH 41

41:1  LA: Taceant ad me insulæ, et gentes mutent fortitudinem : accedant, et tunc loquantur ; simul ad judicium propinquemus.
      EN: Let the islands be silent before Me, and let the nations renew their strength. Let them approach, and then speak; let us draw near together for judgment.

41:2  LA: Quis suscitavit ab oriente Justum, vocavit eum ut sequeretur se ? Dabit in conspectu ejus gentes, et reges obtinebit : dabit quasi pulverem gladio ejus, sicut stipulam vento raptam arcui ejus.
      EN: Who has raised up the just one from the east and called him to follow Him? He will give nations into his presence, and he will possess kings. He will make them like dust before his sword, like windblown stubble before his bow.

41:3  LA: Persequetur eos, transibit in pace : semita in pedibus ejus non apparebit.
      EN: He will pursue them and pass through in peace; no path will appear beneath his feet.

41:4  LA: Quis hæc operatus est, et fecit, vocans generationes ab exordio ? Ego Dominus : primus et novissimus ego sum.
      EN: Who has accomplished and done these things, calling the generations from the beginning? I, the Lord: I am the first and the last.

41:5  LA: Viderunt insulæ, et timuerunt ; extrema terræ obstupuerunt : appropinquaverunt, et accesserunt.
      EN: The islands saw and feared; the ends of the earth were astonished. They drew near and came forward.

41:6  LA: Unusquisque proximo suo auxiliabitur, et fratri suo dicet : Confortare.
      EN: Each will help his neighbor and say to his brother, “Take courage.”

41:7  LA: Confortavit faber ærarius percutiens malleo eum, qui cudebat tunc temporis, dicens : Glutino bonum est ; et confortavit eum clavis, ut non moveretur.]
      EN: The bronze worker striking with a hammer encouraged the one who was then forging, saying, “It is good for joining.” He secured it with nails so that it would not move.

41:8  LA: [Et tu, Israël, serve meus, Jacob quem elegi, semen Abraham amici mei :
      EN: But you, Israel, My servant, Jacob whom I have chosen, offspring of Abraham My friend,

41:9  LA: in quo apprehendi te ab extremis terræ, et a longinquis ejus vocavi te, et dixi tibi : Servus meus es tu : elegi te, et non abjeci te.
      EN: in whom I took hold of you from the ends of the earth and called you from its distant places, and said to you, “You are My servant; I have chosen you and not cast you away,”

41:10  LA: Ne timeas, quia ego tecum sum ; ne declines, quia ego Deus tuus : confortavi te, et auxiliatus sum tibi, et suscepit te dextera Justi mei.
       EN: do not fear, for I am with you. Do not turn aside, for I am your God. I have strengthened and helped you, and the right hand of My Just One has upheld you.

41:11  LA: Ecce confundentur et erubescent omnes qui pugnant adversum te ; erunt quasi non sint, et peribunt viri qui contradicunt tibi.
       EN: Look, all who fight against you will be confounded and ashamed. The men who oppose you will become as though they did not exist and will perish.

41:12  LA: Quæres eos, et non invenies, viros rebelles tuos ; erunt quasi non sint, et veluti consumptio homines bellantes adversum te.
       EN: You will seek them and not find them, the men who rebel against you. Those who make war against you will be as though they did not exist, as something consumed.

41:13  LA: Quia ego Dominus Deus tuus, apprehendens manum tuam, dicensque tibi : Ne timeas : ego adjuvi te.
       EN: For I am the Lord your God, taking hold of your hand and saying to you, “Do not fear; I have helped you.”

41:14  LA: Noli timere, vermis Jacob, qui mortui estis ex Israël : ego auxiliatus sum tibi, dicit Dominus, et redemptor tuus Sanctus Israël.
       EN: Do not fear, worm Jacob, you dead ones of Israel. I have helped you, says the Lord, and your Redeemer is the Holy One of Israel.

41:15  LA: Ego posui te quasi plaustrum triturans novum, habens rostra serrantia ; triturabis montes, et comminues, et colles quasi pulverem pones.
       EN: I have made you like a new threshing cart with saw-like teeth. You will thresh mountains and crush them, and make the hills like dust.

41:16  LA: Ventilabis eos, et ventus tollet, et turbo disperget eos ; et tu exsultabis in Domino, in Sancto Israël lætaberis.
       EN: You will winnow them, and the wind will carry them away; the whirlwind will scatter them. But you will exult in the Lord and rejoice in the Holy One of Israel.

41:17  LA: Egeni et pauperes quærunt aquas, et non sunt ; lingua eorum siti aruit. Ego Dominus exaudiam eos, Deus Israël, non derelinquam eos.
       EN: The needy and poor seek water, and there is none; their tongue is parched with thirst. I, the Lord, will hear them; I, the God of Israel, will not forsake them.

41:18  LA: Aperiam in supinis collibus flumina, et in medio camporum fontes : ponam desertum in stagna aquarum, et terram inviam in rivos aquarum.
       EN: I will open rivers on sloping hills and springs in the midst of plains. I will make the desert pools of water and the trackless land streams of water.

41:19  LA: Dabo in solitudinem cedrum, et spinam, et myrtum, et lignum olivæ ; ponam in deserto abietem, ulmum, et buxum simul :
       EN: I will put cedar, thorn tree, myrtle, and olive tree in the wilderness; I will place fir, elm, and box tree together in the desert,

41:20  LA: ut videant, et sciant, et recogitent, et intelligant pariter, quia manus Domini fecit hoc, et Sanctus Israël creavit illud.
       EN: so that they may see and know, consider and understand together that the Lord's hand has done this and the Holy One of Israel has created it.

41:21  LA: Prope facite judicium vestrum, dicit Dominus ; Afferte, si quid forte habetis, dicit rex Jacob.
       EN: Bring your case forward, says the Lord; present anything you may have, says the King of Jacob.

41:22  LA: Accedant, et nuntient nobis quæcumque ventura sunt ; priora quæ fuerunt, nuntiate, et ponemus cor nostrum, et sciemus novissima eorum ; et quæ ventura sunt, indicate nobis.
       EN: Let them approach and announce to us whatever is to come. Announce the former things that happened, and we will take them to heart and know their outcome; tell us what is to come.

41:23  LA: Annuntiate quæ ventura sunt in futurum, et sciemus quia dii estis vos ; bene quoque aut male, si potestis, facite, et loquamur et videamus simul.
       EN: Announce the things to come in the future, and we will know that you are gods. Do good or harm, if you can, and let us speak and see together.

41:24  LA: Ecce vos estis ex nihilo, et opus vestrum ex eo quod non est : abominatio est qui elegit vos.
       EN: Look, you are from nothing, and your work from what does not exist. Whoever chooses you is an abomination.

41:25  LA: Suscitavi ab aquilone, et veniet ab ortu solis : vocabit nomen meum, et adducet magistratus quasi lutum, et velut plastes conculcans humum.
       EN: I have raised up one from the north, and he will come from the rising sun. He will call upon My name and bring rulers down like mud, like a potter treading clay.

41:26  LA: Quis annuntiavit ab exordio ut sciamus, et a principio ut dicamus : Justus es ? Non est neque annuntians, neque prædicens, neque audiens sermones vestros.
       EN: Who announced this from the beginning, that we might know, and from the outset, that we might say, “You are just”? There is no one announcing or foretelling, nor anyone hearing your words.

41:27  LA: Primus ad Sion dicet : Ecce adsunt, et Jerusalem evangelistam dabo.
       EN: The first will say to Zion, “Look, they are here,” and I will give Jerusalem a bearer of good news.

41:28  LA: Et vidi, et non erat neque ex istis quisquam qui iniret consilium, et interrogatus responderet verbum.
       EN: I looked, and there was no one among these to offer counsel or answer a word when questioned.

41:29  LA: Ecce omnes injusti, et vana opera eorum ; ventus et inane simulacra eorum.]
       EN: Look, they are all unjust, and their works are empty; their idols are wind and emptiness.

ISAIAH 42

42:1  LA: Ecce servus meus, suscipiam eum ; electus meus, complacuit sibi in illo anima mea : dedi spiritum meum super eum : judicium gentibus proferet.
      EN: Look, My servant, whom I will uphold; My chosen one, in whom My soul delights. I have put My Spirit upon him; he will bring forth judgment to the nations.

42:2  LA: Non clamabit, neque accipiet personam, nec audietur vox ejus foris.
      EN: He will not cry out or show partiality, nor will his voice be heard outside.

42:3  LA: Calamum quassatum non conteret, et linum fumigans non extinguet : in veritate educet judicium.
      EN: He will not break a bruised reed or extinguish a smoking wick; he will bring forth judgment in truth.

42:4  LA: Non erit tristis, neque turbulentus, donec ponat in terra judicium ; et legem ejus insulæ exspectabunt.
      EN: He will not be downcast or troubled until he establishes judgment on the earth, and the islands will await his law.

42:5  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus Deus, creans cælos, et extendens eos ; formans terram, et quæ germinant ex ea ; dans flatum populo qui est super eam, et spiritum calcantibus eam :
      EN: Thus says the Lord God, who creates the heavens and stretches them out, who forms the earth and what springs from it, who gives breath to the people upon it and spirit to those who walk upon it:

42:6  LA: Ego Dominus vocavi te in justitia, et apprehendi manum tuam, et servavi te : et dedi te in fœdus populi, in lucem gentium,
      EN: I, the Lord, have called you in justice, taken your hand, and kept you. I have given you as a covenant of the people, a light of the nations,

42:7  LA: ut aperires oculos cæcorum, et educeres de conclusione vinctum, de domo carceris sedentes in tenebris.
      EN: to open the eyes of the blind and bring the prisoner out of confinement, those sitting in darkness out of the prison house.

42:8  LA: Ego Dominus, hoc est nomen meum ; gloriam meam alteri non dabo, et laudem meam sculptilibus.
      EN: I am the Lord; that is My name. I will not give My glory to another or My praise to carved images.

42:9  LA: Quæ prima fuerunt, ecce venerunt ; nova quoque ego annuntio : antequam oriantur, audita vobis faciam.]
      EN: Look, the former things have come to pass. I also announce new things; before they arise I will make you hear of them.

42:10  LA: [Cantate Domino canticum novum, laus ejus ab extremis terræ, qui descenditis in mare, et plenitudo ejus ; insulæ, et habitatores earum.
       EN: Sing to the Lord a new song, His praise from the ends of the earth, you who go down to the sea and all that fills it, the islands and their inhabitants.

42:11  LA: Sublevetur desertum et civitates ejus. In domibus habitabit Cedar : laudate, habitatores petræ ; de vertice montium clamabunt.
       EN: Let the desert and its cities be lifted up. Kedar will dwell in houses. Sing praise, inhabitants of the rock; they will cry out from the mountain tops.

42:12  LA: Ponent Domino gloriam, et laudem ejus in insulis nuntiabunt.
       EN: They will give glory to the Lord and announce His praise in the islands.

42:13  LA: Dominus sicut fortis egredietur, sicut vir præliator suscitabit zelum ; vociferabitur, et clamabit : super inimicos suos confortabitur.]
       EN: The Lord will go out like a mighty man; like a warrior He will stir up zeal. He will shout and cry aloud; He will show His strength against His enemies.

42:14  LA: [Tacui semper, silui, patiens fui : sicut parturiens loquar ; dissipabo, et absorbebo simul.
       EN: I have long kept quiet; I have been silent and patient. I will speak like a woman in labor; I will scatter and swallow up at once.

42:15  LA: Desertos faciam montes et colles, et omne gramen eorum exsiccabo ; et ponam flumina in insulas, et stagna arefaciam.
       EN: I will lay waste mountains and hills and dry up all their grass. I will turn rivers into islands and dry up pools.

42:16  LA: Et ducam cæcos in viam quam nesciunt, et in semitis quas ignoraverunt ambulare eos faciam ; ponam tenebras coram eis in lucem, et prava in recta ; hæc verba feci eis, et non dereliqui eos.
       EN: I will lead the blind along a road they do not know and make them walk on paths unfamiliar to them. I will turn darkness before them into light and crooked places into straight ones. I have done these things for them and have not forsaken them.

42:17  LA: Conversi sunt retrorsum, confundantur confusione, qui confidunt in sculptili ; qui dicunt conflatili : Vos dii nostri.
       EN: Those who trust in a carved image have turned backward; let them be utterly confounded, those who say to a cast image, “You are our gods.”

42:18  LA: Surdi, audite, et cæci, intuemini ad videndum.
       EN: You deaf, hear; you blind, look and see.

42:19  LA: Quis cæcus, nisi servus meus ; et surdus, nisi ad quem nuntios meos misi ? quis cæcus, nisi qui venundatus est ? et quis cæcus, nisi servus Domini ?
       EN: Who is blind but My servant, or deaf but the one to whom I sent My messengers? Who is blind but the one who has been sold? And who is blind but the servant of the Lord?

42:20  LA: Qui vides multa, nonne custodies ? qui apertas habes aures, nonne audies ?
       EN: You who see many things, will you not observe them? You who have open ears, will you not hear?

42:21  LA: Et Dominus voluit ut sanctificaret eum, et magnificaret legem, et extolleret.
       EN: The Lord wished to sanctify him and to magnify and exalt the law.

42:22  LA: Ipse autem populus direptus, et vastatus ; laqueus juvenum omnes, et in domibus carcerum absconditi sunt ; facti sunt in rapinam, nec est qui eruat ; in direptionem, nec est qui dicat : Redde.
       EN: Yet he is a plundered and ravaged people; all are caught in the snare of young men and hidden in prison houses. They have become prey with no one to rescue them, plunder with no one to say, “Give it back.”

42:23  LA: Quis est in vobis qui audiat hoc, attendat, et auscultet futura ?
       EN: Who among you will hear this, pay attention, and listen to what is to come?

42:24  LA: Quis dedit in direptionem Jacob, et Israël vastantibus ? nonne Dominus ipse, cui peccavimus ? Et noluerunt in viis ejus ambulare, et non audierunt legem ejus.
       EN: Who gave Jacob up to plunder and Israel to ravagers? Was it not the Lord Himself, against whom we sinned? They refused to walk in His ways and did not hear His law.

42:25  LA: Et effudit super eum indignationem furoris sui, et forte bellum ; et combussit eum in circuitu, et non cognovit ; et succendit eum, et non intellexit.]
       EN: He poured out upon him His furious indignation and fierce war. It burned him all around, but he did not recognize it; it set him ablaze, but he did not understand.

ISAIAH 43

43:1  LA: Et nunc hæc dicit Dominus creans te, Jacob, et formans te, Israël : Noli timere, quia redemi te, et vocavi te nomine tuo : meus es tu.
      EN: But now thus says the Lord, who created you, Jacob, and formed you, Israel: Do not fear, for I have redeemed you and called you by your name; you are Mine.

43:2  LA: Cum transieris per aquas, tecum ero, et flumina non operient te ; cum ambulaveris in igne, non combureris, et flamma non ardebit in te.
      EN: When you pass through waters, I will be with you, and rivers will not overwhelm you. When you walk through fire, you will not be burned, and the flame will not blaze upon you.

43:3  LA: Quia ego Dominus Deus tuus, Sanctus Israël, salvator tuus, dedi propitiationem tuam Ægyptum, Æthopiam, et Saba, pro te.
      EN: For I am the Lord your God, the Holy One of Israel, your Savior. I gave Egypt as your ransom, Ethiopia and Seba in your place.

43:4  LA: Ex quo honorabilis factus es in oculis meis, et gloriosus, ego dilexi te, et dabo homines pro te, et populos pro anima tua.
      EN: Since you became honored and glorious in My eyes, I have loved you, and I will give human beings in your place and peoples for your life.

43:5  LA: Noli timere, quia ego tecum sum ; ab oriente adducam semen tuum, et ab occidente congregabo te.
      EN: Do not fear, for I am with you. I will bring your offspring from the east and gather you from the west.

43:6  LA: Dicam aquiloni : Da ; et austro : Noli prohibere : affer filios meos de longinquo, et filias meas ab extremis terræ.
      EN: I will say to the north, “Give them up,” and to the south, “Do not hold them back. Bring My sons from far away and My daughters from the ends of the earth.”

43:7  LA: Et omnem qui invocat nomen meum, in gloriam meam creavi eum, formavi eum, et feci eum.
