      EN: Everyone who calls upon My name I have created for My glory; I have formed and made him.

43:8  LA: Educ foras populum cæcum, et oculos habentem ; surdum, et aures ei sunt.
      EN: Bring out the people who are blind yet have eyes, who are deaf yet have ears.

43:9  LA: Omnes gentes congregatæ sunt simul, et collectæ sunt tribus. Quis in vobis annuntiet istud, et quæ prima sunt audire nos faciet ? Dent testes eorum, justificentur, et audiant, et dicant : Vere.
      EN: All the nations have gathered together, and the tribes have assembled. Who among you can announce this and make us hear the former things? Let them bring their witnesses and be vindicated; let them hear and say, “It is true.”

43:10  LA: Vos testes mei, dicit Dominus, et servus meus quem elegi : ut sciatis, et credatis mihi, et intelligatis quia ego ipse sum ; ante me non est formatus Deus, et post me non erit.
       EN: You are My witnesses, says the Lord, and My servant whom I have chosen, so that you may know and believe Me and understand that I am He. Before Me no God was formed, and after Me there will be none.

43:11  LA: Ego sum, ego sum Dominus, et non est absque me salvator.
       EN: I, I am the Lord, and apart from Me there is no savior.

43:12  LA: Ego annuntiavi, et salvavi ; auditum feci, et non fuit in vobis alienus : vos testes mei, dicit Dominus, et ego Deus.
       EN: I announced and saved; I made it heard, and there was no foreign god among you. You are My witnesses, says the Lord, and I am God.

43:13  LA: Et ab initio ego ipse, et non est qui de manu mea eruat. Operabor, et quis avertet illud ?]
       EN: From the beginning I am He, and no one can rescue from My hand. I will act, and who will turn it back?

43:14  LA: [Hæc dicit Dominus, redemptor vester, Sanctus Israël : Propter vos misi in Babylonem, et detraxi vectes universos, et Chaldæos in navibus suis gloriantes.
       EN: Thus says the Lord, your Redeemer, the Holy One of Israel: For your sake I sent to Babylon and pulled down all the bars, and the Chaldeans who gloried in their ships.

43:15  LA: Ego Dominus, Sanctus vester, creans Israël, rex vester.
       EN: I am the Lord, your Holy One, the Creator of Israel, your King.

43:16  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus, qui dedit in mari viam, et in aquis torrentibus semitam ;
       EN: Thus says the Lord, who made a way in the sea and a path through surging waters,

43:17  LA: qui eduxit quadrigam et equum, agmen et robustum : simul obdormierunt, nec resurgent ; contriti sunt quasi linum, et extincti sunt.
       EN: who brought out chariot and horse, army and warrior: together they fell asleep and will not rise; they were crushed like a wick and extinguished.

43:18  LA: Ne memineritis priorum, et antiqua ne intueamini.
       EN: Do not remember the former things or dwell upon things of old.

43:19  LA: Ecce ego facio nova, et nunc orientur, utique cognoscetis ea : ponam in deserto viam, et in invio flumina.
       EN: Look, I am doing new things, and now they will arise; surely you will know them. I will make a road in the desert and rivers in the trackless land.

43:20  LA: Glorificabit me bestia agri, dracones, et struthiones : quia dedi in deserto aquas, flumina in invio, ut darem potum populo meo, electo meo.
       EN: The beasts of the field, dragons and ostriches, will glorify Me, because I have given waters in the desert and rivers in the trackless land, to give drink to My people, My chosen one.

43:21  LA: Populum istum formavi mihi : laudem meam narrabit.]
       EN: I formed this people for Myself; it will recount My praise.

43:22  LA: [Non me invocasti, Jacob, nec laborasti in me, Israël.
       EN: You have not called upon Me, Jacob, nor have you labored for Me, Israel.

43:23  LA: Non obtulisti mihi arietem holocausti tui, et victimis tuis non glorificasti me ; non te servire feci in oblatione, nec laborem tibi præbui in thure.
       EN: You have not brought Me the ram of your burnt offering or glorified Me with your sacrifices. I did not make you serve Me with offerings or burden you with incense.

43:24  LA: Non emisti mihi argento calamum, et adipe victimarum tuarum non inebriasti me : verumtamen servire me fecisti in peccatis tuis ; præbuisti mihi laborem in iniquitatibus tuis.
       EN: You have not bought sweet cane for Me with silver or filled Me with the fat of your sacrifices. Yet you have made Me serve through your sins and burdened Me with your iniquities.

43:25  LA: Ego sum, ego sum ipse qui deleo iniquitates tuas propter me, et peccatorum tuorum non recordabor.
       EN: I, I am the one who blots out your iniquities for My own sake, and I will not remember your sins.

43:26  LA: Reduc me in memoriam, et judicemur simul : narra si quid habes ut justificeris.
       EN: Remind Me, and let us enter judgment together. Declare anything you have by which you may be justified.

43:27  LA: Pater tuus primus peccavit, et interpretes tui prævaricati sunt in me :
       EN: Your first father sinned, and your interpreters transgressed against Me.

43:28  LA: et contaminavi principes sanctos ; dedi ad internecionem Jacob, et Israël in blasphemiam.]
       EN: I profaned the holy princes; I gave Jacob over to slaughter and Israel to reproach.

ISAIAH 44

44:1  LA: Et nunc audi, Jacob, serve meus, et Israël, quem elegi.
      EN: But now hear, Jacob My servant, and Israel whom I have chosen.

44:2  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus faciens et formans te, ab utero auxiliator tuus : Noli timere, serve meus Jacob, et rectissime, quem elegi.
      EN: Thus says the Lord who made and formed you, your helper from the womb: Do not fear, My servant Jacob, you most upright one whom I have chosen.

44:3  LA: Effundam enim aquas super sitientem, et fluenta super aridam ; effundam spiritum meum super semen tuum, et benedictionem meam super stirpem tuam :
      EN: For I will pour water upon the thirsty and streams upon dry ground. I will pour My Spirit upon your offspring and My blessing upon your descendants.

44:4  LA: et germinabunt inter herbas, quasi salices juxta præterfluentes aquas.
      EN: They will spring up among the grass like willows beside flowing waters.

44:5  LA: Iste dicet : Domini ego sum ; et ille vocabit in nomine Jacob ; et hic scribet manu sua : Domino, et in nomine Israël assimilabitur.]
      EN: One will say, “I belong to the Lord”; another will call himself by Jacob's name. Another will write with his hand, “For the Lord,” and be identified by Israel's name.

44:6  LA: [Hæc dicit Dominus, rex Israël, et redemptor ejus, Dominus exercituum : Ego primus, et ego novissimus, et absque me non est deus.
      EN: Thus says the Lord, the King of Israel and its Redeemer, the Lord of hosts: I am the first and I am the last, and apart from Me there is no god.

44:7  LA: Quis similis mei ? vocet, et annuntiet : et ordinem exponat mihi, ex quo constitui populum antiquum ; ventura et quæ futura sunt annuntient eis.
      EN: Who is like Me? Let him call out and announce it and set it in order before Me, since I established the ancient people. Let them announce to them the things approaching and the things yet to come.

44:8  LA: Nolite timere, neque conturbemini : ex tunc audire te feci, et annuntiavi ; vos estis testes mei. Numquid est Deus absque me, et formator quem ego non noverim ?
      EN: Do not fear or be troubled. From that time I made you hear and announced it; you are My witnesses. Is there a God apart from Me, or a maker whom I do not know?

44:9  LA: Plastæ idoli omnes nihil sunt, et amantissima eorum non proderunt eis. Ipsi sunt testes eorum, quia non vident, neque intelligunt, ut confundantur.
      EN: All makers of idols are nothing, and their most cherished things will not profit them. They themselves are their witnesses, for they neither see nor understand, and so they will be put to shame.

44:10  LA: Quis formavit deum, et sculptile conflavit ad nihil utile ?
       EN: Who has formed a god and cast an image useful for nothing?

44:11  LA: Ecce omnes participes ejus confundentur, fabri enim sunt ex hominibus ; convenient omnes, stabunt et pavebunt, et confundentur simul.
       EN: Look, all who share in it will be confounded, for its craftsmen are only human beings. They will all gather and stand; they will be afraid and confounded together.

44:12  LA: Faber ferrarius lima operatus est, in prunis et in malleis formavit illud, et operatus est in brachio fortitudinis suæ ; esuriet et deficiet, non bibet aquam et lassescet.
       EN: The ironsmith has worked with a file, formed it in coals and with hammers, and worked with his strong arm. He will hunger and lose strength; he will not drink water and will grow weary.

44:13  LA: Artifex lignarius extendit normam, formavit illud in runcina, fecit illud in angularibus, et in circino tornavit illud, et fecit imaginem viri quasi speciosum hominem habitantem in domo ;
       EN: The woodworker stretches a rule, shapes it with a plane, makes it with angles, and turns it with a compass. He makes the image of a man, like a handsome human being dwelling in a house.

44:14  LA: succidit cedros, tulit ilicem, et quercum, quæ steterat inter ligna saltus ; plantavit pinum, quam pluvia nutrivit :
       EN: He cuts down cedars and takes holm oak and oak that stood among the forest trees; he plants a pine that the rain nourishes.

44:15  LA: et facta est hominibus in focum ; sumpsit ex eis, et calefactus est ; et succendit et coxit panes ; de reliquo autem operatus est deum et adoravit ; fecit sculptile, et curvatus est ante illud.
       EN: It becomes fuel for people. He takes some of it and warms himself; he kindles it and bakes bread. But from the rest he makes a god and worships it; he makes a carved image and bows before it.

44:16  LA: Medium ejus combussit igni, et de medio ejus carnes comedit ; coxit pulmentum, et saturatus est, et calefactus est, et dixit : Vah ! calefactus sum, vidi focum ;
       EN: He burns half of it in the fire, and with that half cooks meat to eat; he cooks a dish and is satisfied, warms himself, and says, “Ah! I am warm; I have seen the fire.”

44:17  LA: reliquum autem ejus deum fecit et sculptile sibi ; curvatur ante illud, et adorat illud, et obsecrat, dicens : Libera me, quia deus meus es tu !
       EN: From the rest he makes himself a god and a carved image. He bows before it, worships it, and prays to it, saying, “Deliver me, for you are my god!”

44:18  LA: Nescierunt, neque intellexerunt ; obliti enim sunt ne videant oculi eorum, et ne intelligant corde suo.
       EN: They have not known or understood, for they have been blinded, so that their eyes do not see and their hearts do not understand.

44:19  LA: Non recogitant in mente sua, neque cognoscunt, neque sentiunt, ut dicant : Medietatem ejus combussi igni, et coxi super carbones ejus panes ; coxi carnes et comedi, et de reliquo ejus idolum faciam ? ante truncum ligni procidam ?
       EN: They do not reflect in their minds or know or perceive enough to say, “I burned half of it in the fire and baked bread over its coals; I cooked meat and ate it. Shall I make an idol from the rest and fall before a block of wood?”

44:20  LA: Pars ejus cinis est ; cor insipiens adoravit illud, et non liberabit animam suam : neque dicet : Forte mendacium est in dextera mea.
       EN: Part of it is ashes; his foolish heart has worshiped it, and he will not deliver his soul or say, “Perhaps there is a lie in my right hand.”

44:21  LA: Memento horum Jacob, et Israël, quoniam servus meus es tu. Formavi te ; servus meus es tu, Israël, ne obliviscaris mei.
       EN: Remember these things, Jacob and Israel, for you are My servant. I formed you; you are My servant, Israel. Do not forget Me.

44:22  LA: Delevi ut nubem iniquitates tuas, et quasi nebulam peccata tua : revertere ad me, quoniam redemi te.
       EN: I have wiped away your iniquities like a cloud and your sins like mist. Return to Me, for I have redeemed you.

44:23  LA: Laudate, cæli, quoniam misericordiam fecit Dominus ; jubilate, extrema terræ ; resonate, montes, laudationem, saltus et omne lignum ejus, quoniam redemit Dominus Jacob, et Israël gloriabitur.]
       EN: Sing praise, heavens, for the Lord has shown mercy. Shout for joy, ends of the earth; mountains, ring out with praise, forest and all its trees, for the Lord has redeemed Jacob, and Israel will glory.

44:24  LA: [Hæc dicit Dominus, redemptor tuus, et formator tuus ex utero : Ego sum Dominus, faciens omnia, extendens cælos solus, stabiliens terram, et nullus mecum ;
       EN: Thus says the Lord, your Redeemer and the one who formed you from the womb: I am the Lord, who makes all things, who alone stretches out the heavens and establishes the earth, with no one beside Me;

44:25  LA: irrita faciens signa divinorum, et ariolos in furorem vertens ; convertens sapientes retrorsum, et scientiam eorum stultam faciens ;
       EN: who makes diviners' signs fail and turns soothsayers mad, who turns the wise backward and makes their knowledge foolish;

44:26  LA: suscitans verbum servi sui, et consilium nuntiorum suorum complens ; qui dico Jerusalem : Habitaberis, et civitatibus Juda : Ædificabimini, et deserta ejus suscitabo ;
       EN: who establishes the word of His servant and fulfills the counsel of His messengers; who says to Jerusalem, “You will be inhabited,” and to Judah's cities, “You will be rebuilt, and I will raise up her ruins”;

44:27  LA: qui dico profundo : Desolare, et flumina tua arefaciam ;
       EN: who says to the deep, “Be desolate; I will dry up your rivers”;

44:28  LA: qui dico Cyro : Pastor meus es, et omnem voluntatem meam complebis ; qui dico Jerusalem : Ædificaberis, et templo : Fundaberis.]
       EN: who says to Cyrus, “You are My shepherd and will fulfill all My will”; who says to Jerusalem, “You will be rebuilt,” and to the temple, “Your foundations will be laid.”

ISAIAH 45

45:1  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus christo meo Cyro, cujus apprehendi dexteram, ut subjiciam ante faciem ejus gentes, et dorsa regum vertam, et aperiam coram eo januas, et portæ non claudentur :
      EN: Thus says the Lord to My anointed, Cyrus, whose right hand I have grasped to subdue nations before him, turn the backs of kings, and open doors before him, so that gates will not be shut:

45:2  LA: Ego ante te ibo, et gloriosos terræ humiliabo ; portas æreas conteram, et vectes ferreos confringam :
      EN: I will go before you and humble the glorious ones of the earth. I will break bronze gates and shatter iron bars.

45:3  LA: et dabo tibi thesauros absconditos, et arcana secretorum, ut scias quia ego Dominus, qui voco nomen tuum, Deus Israël,
      EN: I will give you hidden treasures and riches concealed in secret places, so that you may know that I am the Lord, who calls you by name, the God of Israel,

45:4  LA: propter servum meum Jacob, et Israël, electum meum ; et vocavi te nomine tuo : assimilavi te, et non cognovisti me.
      EN: for the sake of My servant Jacob and Israel My chosen one. I have called you by your name; I have designated you, though you have not known Me.

45:5  LA: Ego Dominus, et non est amplius ; extra me non est deus ; accinxi te, et non cognovisti me :
      EN: I am the Lord, and there is no other; apart from Me there is no god. I have girded you, though you have not known Me,

45:6  LA: ut sciant hi qui ab ortu solis et qui ab occidente, quoniam absque me non est : ego Dominus, et non est alter :
      EN: so that those from the rising sun and those from the west may know that there is none apart from Me. I am the Lord, and there is no other,

45:7  LA: formans lucem et creans tenebras, faciens pacem et creans malum : ego Dominus faciens omnia hæc.
      EN: forming light and creating darkness, making peace and creating calamity. I am the Lord, doing all these things.

45:8  LA: Rorate, cæli, desuper, et nubes pluant justum ; aperiatur terra, et germinet Salvatorem, et justitia oriatur simul : ego Dominus creavi eum.]
      EN: Drop dew from above, heavens, and let the clouds rain down the just one. Let the earth open and bring forth a savior, and let justice spring up together. I, the Lord, have created him.

45:9  LA: [Væ qui contradicit fictori suo, testa de samiis terræ ! Numquid dicet lutum figulo suo : Quid facis, et opus tuum absque manibus est ?
      EN: Woe to the one who contradicts his maker, a potsherd among the earth's earthenware! Will clay say to its potter, “What are you doing? Your work has no hands”?

45:10  LA: Væ qui dicit patri : Quid generas ? et mulieri : Quid parturis ?
       EN: Woe to the one who says to a father, “What are you fathering?” and to a woman, “What are you bringing forth?”

45:11  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus, Sanctus Israël, plastes ejus : Ventura interrogate me ; super filios meos et super opus manuum mearum mandate mihi.
       EN: Thus says the Lord, the Holy One of Israel, its maker: Ask Me about things to come; give Me commands concerning My children and the work of My hands.

45:12  LA: Ego feci terram, et hominem super eam creavi ego : manus meæ tetenderunt cælos, et omni militiæ eorum mandavi.
       EN: I made the earth, and I created humanity upon it. My hands stretched out the heavens, and I commanded all their host.

45:13  LA: Ego suscitavi eum ad justitiam, et omnes vias ejus dirigam ; ipse ædificabit civitatem meam, et captivitatem meam dimittet, non in pretio neque in muneribus, dicit Dominus Deus exercituum.]
       EN: I have raised him up for justice and will make all his ways straight. He will build My city and release My captives, without payment or gifts, says the Lord God of hosts.

45:14  LA: [Hæc dicit Dominus : Labor Ægypti, et negotiatio Æthiopiæ, et Sabaim viri sublimes ad te transibunt, et tui erunt ; post te ambulabunt, vincti manicis pergent, et te adorabunt, teque deprecabuntur. Tantum in te est Deus, et non est absque te deus.
       EN: Thus says the Lord: The labor of Egypt, the trade of Ethiopia, and the tall Sabean men will pass over to you and be yours. They will walk behind you and come bound in chains; they will bow to you and entreat you: “God is only in you, and apart from you there is no god.”

45:15  LA: Vere tu es Deus absconditus, Deus Israël, salvator.
       EN: Truly You are a hidden God, the God of Israel, the Savior.

45:16  LA: Confusi sunt, et erubuerunt omnes : simul abierunt in confusionem fabricatores errorum.
       EN: They are all confounded and ashamed; the makers of delusions have gone away together in confusion.

45:17  LA: Israël salvatus est in Domino salute æterna ; non confundemini, et non erubescetis usque in sæculum sæculi.
       EN: Israel has been saved in the Lord with everlasting salvation. You will not be confounded or ashamed forever and ever.

45:18  LA: Quia hæc dicit Dominus creans cælos, ipse Deus formans terram et faciens eam, ipse plastes ejus ; non in vanum creavit eam : ut habitaretur formavit eam : Ego Dominus, et non est alius.
       EN: For thus says the Lord, who creates the heavens, God Himself who forms and makes the earth, its very maker: He did not create it in vain; He formed it to be inhabited. I am the Lord, and there is no other.

45:19  LA: Non in abscondito locutus sum, in loco terræ tenebroso ; non dixi semini Jacob frustra : Quærite me : ego Dominus loquens justitiam, annuntians recta.
       EN: I have not spoken in secret, in a dark place of the earth. I did not say in vain to Jacob's offspring, “Seek Me.” I am the Lord, speaking justice and announcing what is right.

45:20  LA: Congregamini, et venite, et accedite simul qui salvati estis ex gentibus : nescierunt qui levant lignum sculpturæ suæ, et rogant deum non salvantem.
       EN: Gather and come; draw near together, you who have escaped from the nations. Those who carry the wood of their carved image and pray to a god who cannot save have no knowledge.

45:21  LA: Annuntiate, et venite, et consiliamini simul. Quis auditum fecit hoc ab initio, ex tunc prædixit illud ? numquid non ego Dominus, et non est ultra deus absque me ? Deus justus, et salvans non est præter me.
       EN: Announce it, come, and take counsel together. Who made this heard from the beginning and foretold it long ago? Was it not I, the Lord? There is no other god apart from Me; there is no just God and Savior besides Me.

45:22  LA: Convertimini ad me, et salvi eritis, omnes fines terræ, quia ego Deus, et non est alius.
       EN: Turn to Me and you will be saved, all the ends of the earth, for I am God and there is no other.

45:23  LA: In memetipso juravi ; egredietur de ore meo justitiæ verbum, et non revertetur : quia mihi curvabitur omne genu, et jurabit omnis lingua.
       EN: I have sworn by Myself: a word of justice will go forth from My mouth and will not return. Every knee will bend to Me, and every tongue will swear.

45:24  LA: Ergo in Domino, dicet, meæ sunt justitiæ et imperium ; ad eum venient, et confundentur omnes qui repugnant ei.
       EN: “Therefore in the Lord,” one will say, “are my righteousness and power.” They will come to Him, and all who oppose Him will be confounded.

45:25  LA: In Domino justificabitur, et laudabitur omne semen Israël.]
       EN: In the Lord all the offspring of Israel will be justified and praised.

ISAIAH 46

46:1  LA: Confractus est Bel, contritus est Nabo ; facta sunt simulacra eorum bestiis et jumentis, onera vestra gravi pondere usque ad lassitudinem.
      EN: Bel is broken, Nebo crushed. Their idols have become loads for beasts and pack animals, your burdens weighing heavily to the point of exhaustion.

46:2  LA: Contabuerunt, et contrita sunt simul ; non potuerunt salvare portantem, et anima eorum in captivitatem ibit.
      EN: They have wasted away and been crushed together. They could not save the one who carried them, and they themselves will go into captivity.

46:3  LA: Audite me, domus Jacob, et omne residuum domus Israël ; qui portamini a meo utero, qui gestamini a mea vulva.
      EN: Listen to Me, house of Jacob and all the remnant of the house of Israel, you whom I have carried from My womb and borne from My body.

46:4  LA: Usque ad senectam ego ipse, et usque ad canos ego portabo ; ego feci, et ego feram ; ego portabo, et salvabo.
      EN: Even to old age I am the same, and to gray hairs I will carry you. I made you, and I will bear you; I will carry and save you.

46:5  LA: Cui assimilastis me, et adæquastis, et comparastis me, et fecistis similem ?
      EN: To whom have you likened Me, made Me equal, compared Me, and made Me similar?

46:6  LA: Qui confertis aurum de sacculo, et argentum statera ponderatis, conducentes aurificem ut faciat deum, et procidunt, et adorant.
      EN: You pour out gold from a bag and weigh silver in a balance, hiring a goldsmith to make a god; then they fall down and worship.

46:7  LA: Portant illum in humeris gestantes, et ponentes in loco suo, et stabit, ac de loco suo non movebitur : sed et cum clamaverint ad eum, non audiet ; de tribulatione non salvabit eos.
      EN: They carry it upon their shoulders, bear it along, and set it in its place. It will stand and not move from its place. Even when they cry to it, it will not hear; it will not save them from distress.

46:8  LA: Mementote istud, et confundamini ; redite, prævaricatores, ad cor.
      EN: Remember this and be ashamed; return to your senses, transgressors.

46:9  LA: Recordamini prioris sæculi, quoniam ego sum Deus, et non est ultra deus, nec est similis mei.
      EN: Remember the former age, for I am God, and there is no other god and none like Me,

46:10  LA: Annuntians ab exordio novissimum, et ab initio quæ necdum facta sunt, dicens : Consilium meum stabit, et omnis voluntas mea fiet.
       EN: announcing the end from the beginning and, from the outset, things not yet done. I say, “My counsel will stand, and all that I will shall come to pass.”

46:11  LA: Vocans ab oriente avem, et de terra longinqua virum voluntatis meæ : et locutus sum, et adducam illud ; creavi et faciam illud.
       EN: I call a bird from the east, and from a distant land the man of My purpose. I have spoken and will bring it about; I have formed it and will do it.

46:12  LA: Audite me, duro corde, qui longe estis a justitia.
       EN: Hear Me, you hardhearted, who are far from justice.

46:13  LA: Prope feci justitiam meam, non elongabitur, et salus mea non morabitur. Dabo in Sion salutem, et in Israël gloriam meam.]
       EN: I have brought My justice near; it will not be far away, and My salvation will not delay. I will give salvation in Zion and My glory in Israel.

ISAIAH 47

47:1  LA: Descende , sede in pulvere, virgo filia Babylon : sede in terra ; non est solium filiæ Chaldæorum, quia ultra non vocaberis mollis et tenera.
      EN: Come down and sit in the dust, virgin daughter Babylon. Sit on the ground; there is no throne for daughter Chaldea, for you will no longer be called delicate and tender.

47:2  LA: Tolle molam, et mole farinam ; denuda turpitudinem tuam ; discooperi humerum, revela crura, transi flumina.
      EN: Take the millstone and grind flour. Expose your shame; uncover your shoulder, bare your legs, and cross rivers.

47:3  LA: Revelabitur ignominia tua, et videbitur opprobrium tuum ; ultionem capiam, et non resistet mihi homo.
      EN: Your disgrace will be uncovered, and your shame will be seen. I will take vengeance, and no human being will resist Me.

47:4  LA: Redemptor noster, Dominus exercituum nomen illius, Sanctus Israël.
      EN: Our Redeemer—the Lord of hosts is His name, the Holy One of Israel.

47:5  LA: Sede tacens, et intra in tenebras, filia Chaldæorum, quia non vocaberis ultra domina regnorum.
      EN: Sit in silence and enter darkness, daughter Chaldea, for you will no longer be called the mistress of kingdoms.

47:6  LA: Iratus sum super populum meum : contaminavi hæreditatem meam, et dedi eos in manu tua : non posuisti eis misericordias ; super senem aggravasti jugum tuum valde.
      EN: I was angry with My people; I profaned My inheritance and gave them into your hand. You showed them no mercy; you made your yoke exceedingly heavy upon the old.

47:7  LA: Et dixisti : In sempiternum ero domina. Non posuisti hæc super cor tuum, neque recordata es novissimi tui.
      EN: You said, “I will be mistress forever.” You did not take these things to heart or remember your end.

47:8  LA: Et nunc audi hæc delicata, et habitans confidenter, quæ dicis in corde tuo : Ego sum, et non est præter me amplius ; non sedebo vidua, et ignorabo sterilitatem.
      EN: Now hear these things, pampered one who dwells securely, who says in her heart, “I am, and there is no other besides me. I will not sit as a widow or know childlessness.”

47:9  LA: Venient tibi duo hæc subito in die una, sterilitas et viduitas : universa venerunt super te, propter multitudinem maleficiorum tuorum, et propter duritiam incantatorum tuorum vehementem.
      EN: These two things will come upon you suddenly in one day: childlessness and widowhood. They have come upon you in full because of your many sorceries and the exceedingly stubborn persistence of your enchanters.

47:10  LA: Et fiduciam habuisti in malitia tua, et dixisti : Non est qui videat me. Sapientia tua et scientia tua, hæc decepit te. Et dixisti in corde tuo : Ego sum, et præter me non est altera.
       EN: You trusted in your wickedness and said, “There is no one who sees me.” Your wisdom and your knowledge have deceived you. You said in your heart, “I am, and there is no other besides me.”

47:11  LA: Veniet super te malum, et nescies ortum ejus ; et irruet super te calamitas quam non poteris expiare ; veniet super te repente miseria quam nescies.
       EN: Evil will come upon you, and you will not know its origin. Calamity will rush upon you that you cannot avert; misery you do not know will suddenly come upon you.

47:12  LA: Sta cum incantatoribus tuis et cum multitudine maleficiorum tuorum, in quibus laborasti ab adolescentia tua, si forte quod prosit tibi, aut si possis fieri fortior.
       EN: Stand with your enchanters and with your many sorceries, in which you have labored from your youth, to see whether they may profit you or whether you can become stronger.

47:13  LA: Defecisti in multitudine consiliorum tuorum. Stent, et salvent te augures cæli, qui contemplabantur sidera, et supputabant menses, ut ex eis annuntiarent ventura tibi.
       EN: You have grown weary amid your many counsels. Let the diviners of heaven stand up and save you, those who observed the stars and calculated the months to announce from them what would come upon you.

47:14  LA: Ecce facti sunt quasi stipula, ignis combussit eos ; non liberabunt animam suam de manu flammæ ; non sunt prunæ quibus calefiant, nec focus ut sedeant ad eum.
       EN: Look, they have become like stubble; fire has burned them. They will not deliver themselves from the power of the flame. There are no coals to warm them, no hearth to sit beside.

47:15  LA: Sic facta sunt tibi in quibuscumque laboraveras : negotiatores tui ab adolescentia tua, unusquisque in via sua erraverunt ; non est qui salvet te.]
       EN: Such have become for you all the things in which you labored. Your merchants from your youth have wandered, each on his own way; there is no one to save you.

ISAIAH 48

48:1  LA: Audite hæc, domus Jacob, qui vocamini nomine Israël, et de aquis Juda existis ; qui juratis in nomine Domini, et Dei Israël recordamini non in veritate neque in justitia.
      EN: Hear these things, house of Jacob, you who are called by Israel's name and have come forth from Judah's waters, who swear by the Lord's name and remember the God of Israel, but not in truth or justice.

48:2  LA: De civitate enim sancta vocati sunt, et super Deum Israël constabiliti sunt : Dominus exercituum nomen ejus.
      EN: For they are called after the holy city and are established upon the God of Israel; the Lord of hosts is His name.

48:3  LA: Priora ex tunc annuntiavi, et ex ore meo exierunt, et audita feci ea : repente operatus sum, et venerunt.
      EN: I announced the former things long ago; they went forth from My mouth, and I made them heard. Suddenly I acted, and they came to pass.

48:4  LA: Scivi enim quia durus es tu, et nervus ferreus cervix tua, et frons tua ærea.
      EN: For I knew that you are stubborn, your neck an iron sinew and your forehead bronze.

48:5  LA: Prædixi tibi ex tunc ; antequam venirent, indicavi tibi, ne forte diceres : Idola mea fecerunt hæc, et sculptilia mea et conflatilia mandaverunt ista.
      EN: I foretold it to you long ago; before these things came, I told you, lest you say, “My idols did these things, and my carved and cast images commanded them.”

48:6  LA: Quæ audisti, vide omnia ; vos autem, num annuntiastis ? Audita feci tibi nova ex tunc, et conservata sunt quæ nescis.
      EN: Look at all that you have heard. But have you announced it? From that time I have made new things heard by you, things kept in reserve that you do not know.

48:7  LA: Nunc creata sunt et non ex tunc ; et ante diem, et non audisti ea, ne forte dicas : Ecce ego cognovi ea.
      EN: They are created now and not long ago; before today you had not heard them, lest you say, “Look, I already knew them.”

48:8  LA: Neque audisti, neque cognovisti, neque ex tunc aperta est auris tua : scio enim quia prævaricans prævaricaberis, et transgressorem ex utero vocavi te.
      EN: You neither heard nor knew, nor was your ear opened long ago. For I know that you will surely act treacherously, and I have called you a transgressor from the womb.

48:9  LA: Propter nomen meum longe faciam furorem meum, et laude mea infrenabo te, ne intereas.
      EN: For My name's sake I will put My fury far away, and for My praise I will restrain you, so that you do not perish.

48:10  LA: Ecce excoxi te, sed non quasi argentum ; elegi te in camino paupertatis.
       EN: Look, I have refined you, but not like silver; I have chosen you in the furnace of poverty.

48:11  LA: Propter me, propter me faciam, ut non blasphemer ; et gloriam meam alteri non dabo.
       EN: For My own sake, for My own sake I will act, so that I am not blasphemed; I will not give My glory to another.

48:12  LA: Audi me, Jacob, et Israël, quem ego voco : ego ipse, ego primus, et ego novissimus.
       EN: Hear Me, Jacob, and Israel whom I call: I am He; I am the first, and I am the last.

48:13  LA: Manus quoque mea fundavit terram, et dextera mea mensa est cælos ; ego vocabo eos, et stabunt simul.
       EN: My hand laid the earth's foundations, and My right hand measured the heavens. I will call them, and they will stand together.

48:14  LA: Congregamini, omnes vos, et audite : quis de eis annuntiavit hæc ? Dominus dilexit eum, faciet voluntatem suam in Babylone, et brachium suum in Chaldæis.
       EN: Gather, all of you, and hear. Who among them announced these things? The Lord has loved him; he will carry out His will in Babylon and exercise His arm against the Chaldeans.

48:15  LA: Ego, ego locutus sum, et vocavi eum ; adduxi eum, et directa est via ejus.
       EN: I, I have spoken and called him; I have brought him, and his way has been made straight.

48:16  LA: Accedite ad me et audite hoc : non a principio in abscondito locutus sum : ex tempore antequam fieret, ibi eram : et nunc Dominus Deus misit me, et spiritus ejus.
       EN: Draw near to me and hear this: From the beginning I have not spoken in secret. From the time before it came to be, I was there. And now the Lord God and His Spirit have sent me.

48:17  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus, redemptor tuus, Sanctus Israël : Ego Dominus Deus tuus, docens te utilia, gubernans te in via qua ambulas.
       EN: Thus says the Lord, your Redeemer, the Holy One of Israel: I am the Lord your God, teaching you what is profitable and guiding you along the road on which you walk.

48:18  LA: Utinam attendisses mandata mea : facta fuisset sicut flumen pax tua, et justitia tua sicut gurgites maris :
       EN: If only you had attended to My commands! Your peace would have been like a river, and your justice like the waves of the sea.

48:19  LA: et fuisset quasi arena semen tuum, et stirps uteri tui ut lapilli ejus ; non interisset et non fuisset attritum nomen ejus a facie mea.
       EN: Your offspring would have been like sand, and the descendants of your womb like its pebbles. Their name would not have perished or been effaced from My presence.

48:20  LA: Egredimini de Babylone, fugite a Chaldæis, in voce exsultationis annuntiate : auditum facite hoc, et efferte illud usque ad extrema terræ. Dicite : Redemit Dominus servum suum Jacob.
       EN: Go out from Babylon; flee from the Chaldeans. Announce it with a voice of exultation; make this heard and carry it to the ends of the earth. Say, “The Lord has redeemed His servant Jacob.”

48:21  LA: Non sitierunt in deserto, cum educeret eos : aquam de petra produxit eis, et scidit petram, et fluxerunt aquæ.
       EN: They did not thirst in the desert when He led them out. He brought water out of the rock for them; He split the rock, and waters flowed.

48:22  LA: Non est pax impiis, dicit Dominus.]
       EN: There is no peace for the wicked, says the Lord.

ISAIAH 49

49:1  LA: Audite , insulæ, et attendite, populi de longe : Dominus ab utero vocavit me ; de ventre matris meæ recordatus est nominis mei.
      EN: Hear, islands, and pay attention, peoples far away. The Lord called me from the womb; from my mother's body He remembered my name.

49:2  LA: Et posuit os meum quasi gladium acutum, in umbra manus suæ protexit me, et posuit me sicut sagittam electam : in pharetra sua abscondit me.
      EN: He made my mouth like a sharp sword and protected me in the shadow of His hand. He made me like a choice arrow and hid me in His quiver.

49:3  LA: Et dixit mihi : Servus meus es tu Israël, quia in te gloriabor.
      EN: He said to me, “You are My servant, Israel, for in you I will glory.”

49:4  LA: Et ego dixi : In vacuum laboravi ; sine causa et vane fortitudinem meam consumpsi : ergo judicium meum cum Domino, et opus meum cum Deo meo.
      EN: But I said, “I have labored for nothing; without cause and in vain I have spent my strength. Therefore my judgment is with the Lord, and my work with my God.”

49:5  LA: Et nunc dicit Dominus, formans me ex utero servum sibi, ut reducam Jacob ad eum, et Israël non congregabitur ; et glorificatus sum in oculis Domini, et Deus meus factus est fortitudo mea.
      EN: And now the Lord speaks, who formed me from the womb as His servant to bring Jacob back to Him, though Israel will not be gathered. I have been glorified in the Lord's eyes, and my God has become my strength.

49:6  LA: Et dixit : Parum est ut sis mihi servus ad suscitandas tribus Jacob, et fæces Israël convertendas : ecce dedi te in lucem gentium, ut sis salus mea usque ad extremum terræ.
      EN: He said, “It is too little for you to be My servant to raise up Jacob's tribes and bring back the dregs of Israel. Look, I have given you as a light of the nations, so that you may be My salvation to the ends of the earth.”

49:7  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus, redemptor Israël, Sanctus ejus, ad contemptibilem animam, ad abominatam gentem, ad servum dominorum : Reges videbunt, et consurgent principes, et adorabunt propter Dominum, quia fidelis est, et Sanctum Israël qui elegit te.]
      EN: Thus says the Lord, the Redeemer of Israel, its Holy One, to the despised soul, to the abhorred nation, to the servant of rulers: Kings will see, and princes will rise and bow down because of the Lord, who is faithful, and the Holy One of Israel, who has chosen you.

49:8  LA: [Hæc dicit Dominus : In tempore placito exaudivi te, et in die salutis auxiliatus sum tui : et servavi te, et dedi te in fœdus populi, ut suscitares terram, et possideres hæreditates dissipatas ;
      EN: Thus says the Lord: In an acceptable time I heard you, and on the day of salvation I helped you. I kept you and gave you as a covenant of the people, to restore the land and take possession of the devastated inheritances,

49:9  LA: ut diceres his qui vincti sunt : Exite, et his qui in tenebris : Revelamini. Super vias pascentur, et in omnibus planis pascua eorum.
      EN: to say to those who are bound, “Come out,” and to those in darkness, “Show yourselves.” They will feed along the roads, and their pastures will be on all the level places.

49:10  LA: Non esurient neque sitient, et non percutiet eos æstus et sol, quia miserator eorum reget eos, et ad fontes aquarum potabit eos.
       EN: They will neither hunger nor thirst, and heat and sun will not strike them, for He who has mercy on them will guide them and give them drink at springs of water.

49:11  LA: Et ponam omnes montes meos in viam, et semitæ meæ exaltabuntur.
       EN: I will make all My mountains a road, and My paths will be raised up.

49:12  LA: Ecce isti de longe venient, et ecce illi ab aquilone et mari, et isti de terra australi.
       EN: Look, these will come from far away, those from the north and the sea, and these from the southern land.

49:13  LA: Laudate, cæli, et exsulta, terra ; jubilate, montes, laudem, quia consolatus est Dominus populum suum, et pauperum suorum miserebitur.]
       EN: Sing praise, heavens, and exult, earth; mountains, shout forth praise, for the Lord has comforted His people and will have mercy on His poor.

49:14  LA: [Et dixit Sion : Dereliquit me Dominus, et Dominus oblitus est mei.
       EN: Zion said, “The Lord has forsaken me; the Lord has forgotten me.”

49:15  LA: Numquid oblivisci potest mulier infantem suum, ut non misereatur filio uteri sui ? Etsi illa oblita fuerit, ego tamen non obliviscar tui.
       EN: Can a woman forget her infant, so that she has no mercy on the child of her womb? Even if she forgets, I will not forget you.

49:16  LA: Ecce in manibus meis descripsi te ; muri tui coram oculis meis semper.
       EN: Look, I have inscribed you upon My hands; your walls are always before My eyes.

49:17  LA: Venerunt structores tui ; destruentes te et dissipantes a te exibunt.
       EN: Your builders have come; those destroying and ravaging you will depart from you.

49:18  LA: Leva in circuitu oculos tuos, et vide : omnes isti congregati sunt, venerunt tibi. Vivo ego, dicit Dominus, quia omnibus his velut ornamento vestieris, et circumdabis tibi eos quasi sponsa ;
       EN: Lift your eyes all around and see: all these have gathered and come to you. As I live, says the Lord, you will clothe yourself with all of them like an ornament and bind them about you like a bride.

49:19  LA: quia deserta tua, et solitudines tuæ, et terra ruinæ tuæ, nunc angusta erunt præ habitatoribus ; et longe fugabuntur qui absorbebant te.
       EN: For your deserts, your lonely places, and your ruined land will now be too narrow for their inhabitants, and those who devoured you will be driven far away.

49:20  LA: Adhuc dicent in auribus tuis filii sterilitatis tuæ : Angustus est mihi locus ; fac spatium mihi ut habitem.
       EN: The children of your childlessness will yet say in your hearing, “The place is too narrow for me; make room for me to dwell.”

49:21  LA: Et dices in corde tuo : Quis genuit mihi istos ? ego sterilis et non pariens, transmigrata, et captiva ; et istos quis enutrivit ? ego destituta et sola ; et isti ubi erant ?
       EN: You will say in your heart, “Who has borne these for me? I was barren and unable to bear, exiled and captive. Who reared these? I was forsaken and alone; where were they?”

49:22  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus Deus : Ecce levabo ad gentes manum meam, et ad populos exaltabo signum meum : et afferent filios tuos in ulnis, et filias tuas super humeros portabunt.
       EN: Thus says the Lord God: Look, I will lift My hand to the nations and raise My standard to the peoples. They will bring your sons in their arms and carry your daughters on their shoulders.

49:23  LA: Et erunt reges nutritii tui, et reginæ nutrices tuæ ; vultu in terram demisso adorabunt te, et pulverem pedum tuorum lingent. Et scies quia ego Dominus, super quo non confundentur qui exspectant eum.
       EN: Kings will be your foster fathers and queens your nursing mothers. With their faces bowed to the earth they will pay homage to you and lick the dust of your feet. You will know that I am the Lord; those who wait for Him will not be put to shame.

49:24  LA: Numquid tolletur a forti præda ? aut quod captum fuerit a robusto, salvum esse poterit ?
       EN: Can prey be taken from the mighty, or can what has been captured by the strong be saved?

49:25  LA: Quia hæc dicit Dominus : Equidem, et captivitas a forti tolletur, et quod ablatum fuerit a robusto, salvabitur. Eos vero qui judicaverunt te, ego judicabo, et filios tuos ego salvabo.
       EN: For thus says the Lord: Indeed, captives will be taken from the mighty, and what has been seized by the strong will be saved. I will judge those who judged you, and I will save your children.

49:26  LA: Et cibabo hostes tuos carnibus suis, et quasi musto, sanguine suo inebriabuntur, et sciet omnis caro quia ego Dominus salvans te, et redemptor tuus fortis Jacob.]
       EN: I will feed your enemies with their own flesh, and they will become drunk on their own blood as on new wine. All flesh will know that I am the Lord who saves you, your Redeemer, the Mighty One of Jacob.

ISAIAH 50

50:1  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus : Quis est hic liber repudii matris vestræ, quo dimisi eam ? aut quis est creditor meus, cui vendidi vos ? Ecce in iniquitatibus vestris venditi estis, et in sceleribus vestris dimisi matrem vestram.
      EN: Thus says the Lord: What is this certificate by which I divorced your mother? Or who is My creditor to whom I sold you? Look, you were sold because of your iniquities, and because of your crimes I sent your mother away.

50:2  LA: Quia veni, et non erat vir ; vocavi, et non erat qui audiret. Numquid abbreviata et parvula facta est manus mea, ut non possim redimere ? aut non est in me virtus ad liberandum ? Ecce in increpatione mea desertum faciam mare, ponam flumina in siccum ; computrescent pisces sine aqua, et morientur in siti.
      EN: For I came, and there was no one; I called, and there was no one to hear. Has My hand been shortened and made small, so that I cannot redeem? Or is there no strength in Me to deliver? Look, by My rebuke I will make the sea a desert and turn rivers dry. Their fish will rot without water and die of thirst.

50:3  LA: Induam cælos tenebris, et saccum ponam operimentum eorum.]
      EN: I will clothe the heavens in darkness and make sackcloth their covering.

50:4  LA: [Dominus dedit mihi linguam eruditam, ut sciam sustentare eum qui lassus est verbo. Erigit mane, mane erigit mihi aurem, ut audiam quasi magistrum.
      EN: The Lord has given me a learned tongue, so that I may know how to sustain the weary with a word. Morning after morning He awakens my ear, so that I may listen as to a teacher.

50:5  LA: Dominus Deus aperuit mihi aurem, ego autem non contradico : retrorsum non abii.
      EN: The Lord God has opened my ear, and I do not resist; I have not turned backward.

50:6  LA: Corpus meum dedi percutientibus, et genas meas vellentibus ; faciem meam non averti ab increpantibus et conspuentibus in me.
      EN: I gave my body to those who struck me and my cheeks to those who plucked them. I did not turn my face away from those who rebuked me and spat upon me.

50:7  LA: Dominus Deus auxiliator meus, ideo non sum confusus ; ideo posui faciem meam ut petram durissimam, et scio quoniam non confundar.
      EN: The Lord God is my helper; therefore I have not been confounded. Therefore I have set my face like the hardest rock, and I know that I will not be put to shame.

50:8  LA: Juxta est qui justificat me ; quis contradicet mihi ? Stemus simul ; quis est adversarius meus ? accedat ad me.
      EN: He who justifies me is near. Who will oppose me? Let us stand together. Who is my adversary? Let him approach me.

50:9  LA: Ecce Dominus Deus auxiliator meus ; quis est qui condemnet me ? Ecce omnes quasi vestimentum conterentur ; tinea comedet eos.
      EN: Look, the Lord God is my helper; who will condemn me? Look, all of them will wear out like a garment; the moth will eat them.

50:10  LA: Quis ex vobis timens Dominum, audiens vocem servi sui ? Qui ambulavit in tenebris, et non est lumen ei, speret in nomine Domini, et innitatur super Deum suum.
       EN: Who among you fears the Lord and hears the voice of His servant? Let the one who has walked in darkness and has no light hope in the Lord's name and lean upon his God.

50:11  LA: Ecce vos omnes accendentes ignem, accincti flammis : ambulate in lumine ignis vestri, et in flammis quas succendistis ; de manu mea factum est hoc vobis : in doloribus dormietis.]
       EN: Look, all you who kindle fire, girded with flames: walk in the light of your fire and in the flames you have kindled. This has come to you from My hand: you will lie down in sorrows.

ISAIAH 51

51:1  LA: Audite me, qui sequimini quod justum est, et quæritis Dominum ; attendite ad petram unde excisi estis, et ad cavernam laci de qua præcisi estis.
      EN: Listen to Me, you who pursue what is just and seek the Lord. Look to the rock from which you were cut and the hollow of the pit from which you were quarried.

51:2  LA: Attendite ad Abraham, patrem vestrum, et ad Saram, quæ peperit vos : quia unum vocavi eum, et benedixi ei, et multiplicavi eum.
      EN: Look to Abraham your father and to Sarah who bore you, for I called him when he was one, and blessed and multiplied him.

51:3  LA: Consolabitur ergo Dominus Sion, et consolabitur omnes ruinas ejus : et ponet desertum ejus quasi delicias, et solitudinem ejus quasi hortum Domini. Gaudium et lætitia invenietur in ea, gratiarum actio et vox laudis.
      EN: Therefore the Lord will comfort Zion and all her ruins. He will make her desert like a place of delight and her wilderness like the garden of the Lord. Joy and gladness will be found in her, thanksgiving and the sound of praise.

51:4  LA: Attendite ad me, popule meus, et tribus mea, me audite : quia lex a me exiet, et judicium meum in lucem populorum requiescet.
      EN: Pay attention to Me, My people; hear Me, My tribe. For a law will go out from Me, and My judgment will rest as a light for the peoples.

51:5  LA: Prope est justus meus, egressus est salvator meus, et brachia mea populos judicabunt ; me insulæ exspectabunt, et brachium meum sustinebunt.
      EN: My just one is near; My savior has gone forth, and My arms will judge the peoples. The islands will wait for Me and look to My arm.

51:6  LA: Levate in cælum oculos vestros, et videte sub terra deorsum : quia cæli sicut fumus liquescent, et terra sicut vestimentum atteretur, et habitatores ejus sicut hæc interibunt : salus autem mea in sempiternum erit, et justitia mea non deficiet.
      EN: Lift your eyes to heaven and look beneath the earth below. For the heavens will dissolve like smoke, and the earth will wear out like a garment; its inhabitants will perish like these. But My salvation will be forever, and My justice will not fail.

51:7  LA: Audite me, qui scitis justum, populus meus, lex mea in corde eorum : nolite timere opprobrium hominum, et blasphemias eorum ne metuatis :
      EN: Hear Me, you who know what is just, My people with My law in their hearts. Do not fear human reproach or dread their insults,

51:8  LA: sicut enim vestimentum, sic comedet eos vermis, et sicut lanam, sic devorabit eos tinea : salus autem mea in sempiternum erit, et justitia mea in generationes generationum.
      EN: for the worm will eat them like a garment, and the moth will devour them like wool. But My salvation will be forever, and My justice through generation after generation.

51:9  LA: Consurge, consurge, induere fortitudinem, brachium Domini ! consurge sicut in diebus antiquis, in generationibus sæculorum. Numquid non tu percussisti superbum, vulnerasti draconem ?
      EN: Arise, arise, clothe yourself with strength, arm of the Lord! Arise as in ancient days, in generations long past. Was it not You who struck the proud one and wounded the dragon?

51:10  LA: numquid non tu siccasti mare, aquam abyssi vehementis ; qui posuisti profundum maris viam, ut transirent liberati ?
       EN: Was it not You who dried up the sea, the waters of the mighty deep, who made the depths of the sea a road for the delivered to cross?

51:11  LA: Et nunc qui redempti sunt a Domino, revertentur, et venient in Sion laudantes, et lætitia sempiterna super capita eorum : gaudium et lætitiam tenebunt ; fugiet dolor et gemitus.
       EN: Now those redeemed by the Lord will return and come to Zion with praise, with everlasting gladness upon their heads. They will hold joy and gladness, and sorrow and sighing will flee away.

51:12  LA: Ego, ego ipse consolabor vos. Quis tu, ut timeres ab homine mortali, et a filio hominis qui quasi fœnum ita arescet ?
       EN: I, I Myself will comfort you. Who are you, that you should fear a mortal man, a son of man who will wither like grass?

51:13  LA: Et oblitus es Domini, factoris tui, qui tetendit cælos et fundavit terram ; et formidasti jugiter tota die a facie furoris ejus qui te tribulabat, et paraverat ad perdendum. Ubi nunc est furor tribulantis ?
       EN: You have forgotten the Lord your Maker, who stretched out the heavens and founded the earth. You have constantly feared all day before the fury of the one who afflicted you and prepared to destroy you. Where now is the fury of your oppressor?

51:14  LA: Cito veniet gradiens ad aperiendum ; et non interficiet usque ad internecionem, nec deficiet panis ejus.
       EN: The one who walks forth to open will come quickly; he will not kill to the point of destruction, nor will his bread fail.

51:15  LA: Ego autem sum Dominus Deus tuus, qui conturbo mare, et intumescunt fluctus ejus : Dominus exercituum nomen meum.
       EN: But I am the Lord your God, who stirs the sea so that its waves swell. The Lord of hosts is My name.

51:16  LA: Posui verba mea in ore tuo, et in umbra manus meæ protexi te, ut plantes cælos, et fundes terram, et dicas ad Sion : Populus meus es tu.]
       EN: I have put My words in your mouth and protected you in the shadow of My hand, so that you may plant the heavens, found the earth, and say to Zion, “You are My people.”

51:17  LA: [Elevare, elevare, consurge, Jerusalem, quæ bibisti de manu Domini calicem iræ ejus ; usque ad fundum calicis soporis bibisti, et potasti usque ad fæces.
       EN: Rise up, rise up, stand, Jerusalem, you who have drunk the cup of His wrath from the Lord's hand. You have drunk the cup of stupor to its bottom and drained it to the dregs.

51:18  LA: Non est qui sustentet eam, ex omnibus filiis quos genuit ; et non est qui apprehendat manum ejus, ex omnibus filiis quos enutrivit.
       EN: There is no one to support her among all the sons she bore, and no one to take her hand among all the sons she reared.

51:19  LA: Duo sunt quæ occurrerunt tibi ; quis contristabitur super te ? Vastitas, et contritio, et fames, et gladius ; quis consolabitur te ?
       EN: Two things have befallen you—who will grieve over you? Devastation and destruction, famine and sword—who will comfort you?

51:20  LA: Filii tui projecti sunt, dormierunt in capite omnium viarum sicut oryx illaqueatus, pleni indignatione Domini, increpatione Dei tui.
       EN: Your sons have been cast down and lie at the head of every street like a trapped oryx, filled with the Lord's indignation, with your God's rebuke.

51:21  LA: Idcirco audi hoc, paupercula, et ebria non a vino.
       EN: Therefore hear this, poor afflicted one, drunk but not with wine.

51:22  LA: Hæc dicit dominator tuus Dominus, et Deus tuus, qui pugnabit pro populo suo : Ecce tuli de manu tua calicem soporis, fundum calicis indignationis meæ : non adjicies ut bibas illum ultra.
       EN: Thus says your Master, the Lord, your God who will fight for His people: Look, I have taken from your hand the cup of stupor, the bottom of the cup of My indignation. You will drink it no more.

51:23  LA: Et ponam illum in manu eorum qui te humiliaverunt, et dixerunt animæ tuæ : Incurvare, ut transeamus ; et posuisti ut terram corpus tuum, et quasi viam transeuntibus.]
       EN: I will put it into the hands of those who humiliated you and said to you, “Bend down so that we may pass over.” You made your body like the ground, like a road for passersby.

ISAIAH 52

52:1  LA: Consurge , consurge, induere fortitudine tua, Sion ! induere vestimentis gloriæ tuæ, Jerusalem, civitas Sancti, quia non adjiciet ultra ut pertranseat per te incircumcisus et immundus.
      EN: Arise, arise, clothe yourself with your strength, Zion! Put on your glorious garments, Jerusalem, city of the Holy One, for the uncircumcised and unclean will no longer pass through you.

52:2  LA: Excutere de pulvere, consurge ; sede, Jerusalem ! solve vincula colli tui, captiva filia Sion.
      EN: Shake off the dust; arise and take your seat, Jerusalem! Loosen the bonds from your neck, captive daughter Zion.

52:3  LA: Quia hæc dicit Dominus : Gratis venundati estis, et sine argento redimemini.
      EN: For thus says the Lord: You were sold for nothing, and without silver you will be redeemed.

52:4  LA: Quia hæc dicit Dominus Deus : In Ægyptum descendit populus meus in principio, ut colonus esset ibi, et Assur absque ulla causa calumniatus est eum.
      EN: For thus says the Lord God: My people went down to Egypt at first to live there as settlers, and Assyria oppressed them without any cause.

52:5  LA: Et nunc quid mihi est hic, dicit Dominus, quoniam ablatus est populus meus gratis ? Dominatores ejus inique agunt, dicit Dominus, et jugiter tota die nomen meum blasphematur.
      EN: Now what have I here, says the Lord, since My people have been taken away for nothing? Their rulers act unjustly, says the Lord, and continually all day My name is blasphemed.

52:6  LA: Propter hoc sciet populus meus nomen meum in die illa : quia ego ipse qui loquebar, ecce adsum.]
      EN: Therefore My people will know My name on that day, for I am the one who was speaking. Look, I am here.

52:7  LA: [Quam pulchri super montes pedes annuntiantis et prædicantis pacem ; annuntiantis bonum, prædicantis salutem, dicentis Sion : Regnabit Deus tuus !
      EN: How beautiful upon the mountains are the feet of the one announcing and proclaiming peace, announcing good and proclaiming salvation, saying to Zion, “Your God will reign!”

52:8  LA: Vox speculatorum tuorum : levaverunt vocem, simul laudabunt, quia oculo ad oculum videbunt cum converterit Dominus Sion.
      EN: The voice of your watchmen! They have lifted their voice and will praise together, for they will see eye to eye when the Lord restores Zion.

52:9  LA: Gaudete, et laudate simul, deserta Jerusalem, quia consolatus est Dominus populum suum ; redemit Jerusalem.
      EN: Rejoice and praise together, ruins of Jerusalem, for the Lord has comforted His people; He has redeemed Jerusalem.

52:10  LA: Paravit Dominus brachium sanctum suum in oculis omnium gentium ; et videbunt omnes fines terræ salutare Dei nostri.
       EN: The Lord has made ready His holy arm before the eyes of all nations, and all the ends of the earth will see the salvation of our God.

52:11  LA: Recedite, recedite ; exite inde, pollutum nolite tangere ; exite de medio ejus ; mundamini, qui fertis vasa Domini.
       EN: Depart, depart; come out from there. Touch nothing unclean. Come out from her midst; purify yourselves, you who carry the Lord's vessels.

52:12  LA: Quoniam non in tumultu exibitis, nec in fuga properabitis ; præcedet enim vos Dominus, et congregabit vos Deus Israël.]
       EN: For you will not go out in tumult or hurry away in flight. The Lord will go before you, and the God of Israel will gather you.

52:13  LA: [Ecce intelliget servus meus, exaltabitur et elevabitur, et sublimis erit valde.
       EN: Look, My servant will act with understanding; he will be exalted and lifted up and will be exceedingly high.

52:14  LA: Sicut obstupuerunt super te multi, sic inglorius erit inter viros aspectus ejus, et forma ejus inter filios hominum.
       EN: Just as many were astonished at you, so his appearance will be without glory among men, and his form among the children of humanity.

52:15  LA: Iste asperget gentes multas ; super ipsum continebunt reges os suum : quia quibus non est narratum de eo viderunt, et qui non audierunt contemplati sunt.]
       EN: He will sprinkle many nations; kings will shut their mouths because of him. For those to whom nothing was told about him have seen, and those who have not heard have beheld.

ISAIAH 53

53:1  LA: Quis credidit auditui nostro ? et brachium Domini cui revelatum est ?
      EN: Who has believed our report, and to whom has the arm of the Lord been revealed?

53:2  LA: Et ascendet sicut virgultum coram eo, et sicut radix de terra sitienti. Non est species ei, neque decor, et vidimus eum, et non erat aspectus, et desideravimus eum :
      EN: He will rise like a young shoot before Him and like a root from thirsty ground. He has no beauty or splendor. We saw him, and there was no attractiveness, yet we desired him:

53:3  LA: despectum, et novissimum virorum, virum dolorum, et scientem infirmitatem, et quasi absconditus vultus ejus et despectus, unde nec reputavimus eum.
      EN: despised and the lowest of men, a man of sorrows and acquainted with infirmity, his face as though hidden and despised; therefore we held him in no regard.

53:4  LA: Vere languores nostros ipse tulit, et dolores nostros ipse portavit ; et nos putavimus eum quasi leprosum, et percussum a Deo, et humiliatum.
      EN: Truly he himself bore our infirmities and carried our sorrows. Yet we thought him like a leper, struck by God and humbled.

53:5  LA: Ipse autem vulneratus est propter iniquitates nostras ; attritus est propter scelera nostra : disciplina pacis nostræ super eum, et livore ejus sanati sumus.
      EN: But he was wounded for our iniquities, crushed for our crimes. The discipline of our peace was upon him, and by his bruising we were healed.

53:6  LA: Omnes nos quasi oves erravimus, unusquisque in viam suam declinavit : et posuit Dominus in eo iniquitatem omnium nostrum.
      EN: All of us have strayed like sheep; each has turned to his own way, and the Lord laid upon him the iniquity of us all.

53:7  LA: Oblatus est quia ipse voluit, et non aperuit os suum ; sicut ovis ad occisionem ducetur, et quasi agnus coram tondente se obmutescet, et non aperiet os suum.
      EN: He was offered because he himself willed it, and he did not open his mouth. Like a sheep he will be led to slaughter, and like a lamb before its shearer he will be silent and will not open his mouth.

53:8  LA: De angustia, et de judicio sublatus est. Generationem ejus quis enarrabit ? quia abscissus est de terra viventium : propter scelus populi mei percussi eum.
      EN: He was taken away from anguish and judgment. Who will recount his generation? For he was cut off from the land of the living; for the crime of My people I struck him.

53:9  LA: Et dabit impios pro sepultura, et divitem pro morte sua, eo quod iniquitatem non fecerit, neque dolus fuerit in ore ejus.
      EN: He will give the wicked for his burial and a rich man for his death, because he had done no iniquity and no deceit had been in his mouth.

53:10  LA: Et Dominus voluit conterere eum in infirmitate. Si posuerit pro peccato animam suam, videbit semen longævum, et voluntas Domini in manu ejus dirigetur.
       EN: The Lord willed to crush him in weakness. If he gives his life for sin, he will see long-lived offspring, and the Lord’s purpose will prosper in his hand.

53:11  LA: Pro eo quod laboravit anima ejus, videbit et saturabitur. In scientia sua justificabit ipse justus servus meus multos, et iniquitates eorum ipse portabit.
       EN: Because his soul has labored, he will see and be satisfied. By his knowledge My just servant will himself justify many, and he himself will bear their iniquities.

53:12  LA: Ideo dispertiam ei plurimos, et fortium dividet spolia, pro eo quod tradidit in mortem animam suam, et cum sceleratis reputatus est, et ipse peccata multorum tulit, et pro transgressoribus rogavit.]
       EN: Therefore I will allot very many to him, and he will divide the spoils of the mighty, because he handed over his life to death and was counted among criminals. He himself bore the sins of many and prayed for transgressors.

ISAIAH 54

54:1  LA: Lauda , sterilis, quæ non paris ; decanta laudem, et hinni, quæ non pariebas : quoniam multi filii desertæ magis quam ejus quæ habet virum, dicit Dominus.
      EN: Sing praise, barren one who does not bear. Break into praise and cry aloud, you who were not in labor, for the deserted woman's children are more numerous than those of her who has a husband, says the Lord.

54:2  LA: Dilata locum tentorii tui, et pelles tabernaculorum tuorum extende : ne parcas : longos fac funiculos tuos, et clavos tuos consolida.
      EN: Enlarge the place of your tent and stretch out the skins of your dwellings. Do not hold back; lengthen your cords and strengthen your stakes.

54:3  LA: Ad dexteram enim et ad lævam penetrabis, et semen tuum gentes hæreditabit, et civitates desertas inhabitabit.
      EN: For you will spread to the right and left, and your offspring will inherit nations and inhabit deserted cities.

54:4  LA: Noli timere, quia non confunderis, neque erubesces ; non enim te pudebit, quia confusionis adolescentiæ tuæ oblivisceris, et opprobrii viduitatis tuæ non recordaberis amplius.
      EN: Do not fear, for you will not be confounded or blush. You will not be ashamed, for you will forget the shame of your youth and remember the reproach of your widowhood no more.

54:5  LA: Quia dominabitur tui qui fecit te, Dominus exercituum nomen ejus, et redemptor tuus, Sanctus Israël : Deus omnis terræ vocabitur.
      EN: For your Maker will rule over you; the Lord of hosts is His name, and your Redeemer is the Holy One of Israel. He will be called the God of all the earth.

54:6  LA: Quia ut mulierem derelictam et mœrentem spiritu vocavit te Dominus, et uxorem ab adolescentia abjectam, dixit Deus tuus.
      EN: For the Lord has called you like a woman forsaken and grieving in spirit, like a wife from youth who has been cast away, says your God.

54:7  LA: Ad punctum in modico dereliqui te, et in miserationibus magnis congregabo te.
      EN: For a brief moment I forsook you, but with great compassion I will gather you.

54:8  LA: In momento indignationis abscondi faciem meam parumper a te ; et in misericordia sempiterna misertus sum tui, dixit redemptor tuus, Dominus.
      EN: In a moment of indignation I hid My face from you for a little while, but with everlasting mercy I have had compassion on you, says your Redeemer, the Lord.

54:9  LA: Sicut in diebus Noë istud mihi est, cui juravi ne inducerem aquas Noë ultra super terram ; sic juravi ut non irascar tibi, et non increpem te.
      EN: This is to Me as in the days of Noah, to whom I swore that I would no longer bring Noah's waters upon the earth. So I have sworn not to be angry with you or rebuke you.

54:10  LA: Montes enim commovebuntur, et colles contremiscent ; misericordia autem mea non recedet a te, et fœdus pacis meæ non movebitur, dixit miserator tuus Dominus.
       EN: For mountains will be moved and hills will tremble, but My mercy will not depart from you, nor will My covenant of peace be shaken, says the Lord who has compassion on you.

54:11  LA: Paupercula, tempestate convulsa absque ulla consolatione, ecce ego sternam per ordinem lapides tuos, et fundabo te in sapphiris :
       EN: Poor afflicted one, storm-tossed and without any comfort, look, I will lay your stones in order and found you upon sapphires.

54:12  LA: et ponam jaspidem propugnacula tua, et portas tuas in lapides sculptos, et omnes terminos tuos in lapides desiderabiles ;
       EN: I will make your battlements of jasper, your gates of carved stones, and all your borders of precious stones,

54:13  LA: universos filios tuos doctos a Domino, et multitudinem pacis filiis tuis.
       EN: all your children taught by the Lord, and abundant peace for your children.

54:14  LA: Et in justitia fundaberis : recede procul a calumnia, quia non timebis, et a pavore, quia non appropinquabit tibi.
       EN: You will be founded in justice. Stay far from oppression, for you will not fear, and from terror, for it will not approach you.

54:15  LA: Ecce accola veniet qui non erat mecum, advena quondam tuus adjungetur tibi.
       EN: Look, a settler will come who was not with Me; one formerly a stranger to you will join himself to you.

54:16  LA: Ecce ego creavi fabrum sufflantem in igne prunas, et proferentem vas in opus suum ; et ego creavi interfectorem ad disperdendum.
       EN: Look, I created the craftsman who blows upon coals in the fire and brings forth an instrument for his work; I also created the destroyer to lay waste.

54:17  LA: Omne vas quod fictum est contra te, non dirigetur, et omnem linguam resistentem tibi in judicio, judicabis. Hæc est hæreditas servorum Domini, et justitia eorum apud me, dicit Dominus.]
       EN: No instrument fashioned against you will succeed, and you will condemn every tongue that opposes you in judgment. This is the inheritance of the Lord's servants, and their justice is with Me, says the Lord.

ISAIAH 55

55:1  LA: Omnes sitientes, venite ad aquas, et qui non habetis argentum, properate, emite, et comedite : venite, emite absque argento et absque ulla commutatione vinum et lac.
      EN: All you who thirst, come to the waters. You who have no silver, hurry, buy, and eat. Come, buy wine and milk without silver and without any exchange.

55:2  LA: Quare appenditis argentum non in panibus, et laborem vestrum non in saturitate ? Audite, audientes me, et comedite bonum, et delectabitur in crassitudine anima vestra.
      EN: Why do you weigh out silver for what is not bread, and your labor for what does not satisfy? Listen carefully to Me and eat what is good, and your soul will delight in rich food.

55:3  LA: Inclinate aurem vestram, et venite ad me ; audite, et vivet anima vestra, et feriam vobiscum pactum sempiternum, misericordias David fideles.
      EN: Incline your ear and come to Me; hear, and your soul will live. I will make an everlasting covenant with you, the faithful mercies of David.

55:4  LA: Ecce testem populis dedi eum, ducem ac præceptorem gentibus.
      EN: Look, I have given him as a witness to the peoples, a leader and commander of nations.

55:5  LA: Ecce gentem quam nesciebas vocabis, et gentes quæ te non cognoverunt ad te current, propter Dominum Deum tuum, et Sanctum Israël, quia glorificavit te.
      EN: Look, you will call a nation you did not know, and nations that did not know you will run to you because of the Lord your God, the Holy One of Israel, for He has glorified you.

55:6  LA: Quærite Dominum dum inveniri potest ; invocate eum dum prope est.
      EN: Seek the Lord while He can be found; call upon Him while He is near.

55:7  LA: Derelinquat impius viam suam, et vir iniquus cogitationes suas, et revertatur ad Dominum, et miserebitur ejus ; et ad Deum nostrum, quoniam multus est ad ignoscendum.
      EN: Let the wicked forsake his way and the unjust man his thoughts. Let him return to the Lord, and He will have mercy on him, and to our God, for He is abundant in forgiveness.

55:8  LA: Non enim cogitationes meæ cogitationes vestræ, neque viæ vestræ viæ meæ, dicit Dominus.
      EN: For My thoughts are not your thoughts, nor are your ways My ways, says the Lord.

55:9  LA: Quia sicut exaltantur cæli a terra, sic exaltatæ sunt viæ meæ a viis vestris, et cogitationes meæ a cogitationibus vestris.
      EN: For as the heavens are raised above the earth, so My ways are raised above your ways and My thoughts above your thoughts.

55:10  LA: Et quomodo descendit imber et nix de cælo, et illuc ultra non revertitur, sed inebriat terram, et infundit eam, et germinare eam facit, et dat semen serenti, et panem comedenti :
       EN: As rain and snow come down from heaven and do not return there, but drench the earth, water it, make it sprout, and give seed to the sower and bread to the eater,

55:11  LA: sic erit verbum meum quod egredietur de ore meo ; non revertetur ad me vacuum, sed faciet quæcumque volui, et prosperabitur in his ad quæ misi illud.
       EN: so will My word be that goes forth from My mouth. It will not return to Me empty, but will accomplish whatever I have willed and prosper in the things for which I sent it.

55:12  LA: Quia in lætitia egrediemini, et in pace deducemini ; montes et colles cantabunt coram vobis laudem, et omnia ligna regionis plaudent manu.
       EN: For you will go out in gladness and be led in peace. Mountains and hills will sing praise before you, and all the trees of the countryside will clap their hands.

55:13  LA: Pro saliunca ascendet abies, et pro urtica crescet myrtus ; et erit Dominus nominatus in signum æternum quod non auferetur.]
       EN: Where the low shrub grew, the fir will rise. Where nettles grew, the myrtle will grow. The Lord will be renowned as an everlasting sign that will never be taken away.

ISAIAH 56

56:1  LA: Hæc dicit Dominus : Custodite judicium, et facite justitiam, quia juxta est salus mea ut veniat, et justitia mea ut reveletur.
      EN: Thus says the Lord: Keep judgment and practice justice, for My salvation is near to coming and My justice to being revealed.

56:2  LA: Beatus vir qui facit hoc, et filius hominis qui apprehendet istud, custodiens sabbatum ne polluat illud, custodiens manus suas ne faciat omne malum.
      EN: Blessed is the man who does this, the son of man who holds fast to it, keeping the Sabbath from being profaned and guarding his hands from doing any evil.

56:3  LA: Et non dicat filius advenæ qui adhæret Domino, dicens : Separatione dividet me Dominus a populo suo ; et non dicat eunuchus : Ecce ego lignum aridum.
      EN: Let not the foreigner's son who clings to the Lord say, “The Lord will surely separate me from His people.” And let not the eunuch say, “Look, I am a dry tree.”

56:4  LA: Quia hæc dicit Dominus eunuchis : Qui custodierint sabbata mea, et elegerint quæ ego volui, et tenuerint fœdus meum,
      EN: For thus says the Lord to the eunuchs: Those who keep My Sabbaths, choose what I desire, and hold fast to My covenant,

56:5  LA: dabo eis in domo mea et in muris meis locum, et nomen melius a filiis et filiabus : nomen sempiternum dabo eis, quod non peribit.
      EN: I will give within My house and My walls a place and a name better than sons and daughters. I will give them an everlasting name that will not perish.

56:6  LA: Et filios advenæ, qui adhærent Domino, ut colant eum, et diligant nomen ejus, ut sint ei in servos ; omnem custodientem sabbatum ne polluat illud, et tenentem fœdus meum ;
      EN: And the foreigners' sons who cling to the Lord to serve Him and love His name, to be His servants, everyone who keeps the Sabbath from being profaned and holds fast to My covenant,

56:7  LA: adducam eos in montem sanctum meum, et lætificabo eos in domo orationis meæ ; holocausta eorum et victimæ eorum placebunt mihi super altari meo, quia domus mea domus orationis vocabitur cunctis populis.
      EN: I will bring them to My holy mountain and gladden them in My house of prayer. Their burnt offerings and sacrifices will please Me upon My altar, for My house will be called a house of prayer for all peoples.

56:8  LA: Ait Dominus Deus, qui congregat dispersos Israël : Adhuc congregabo ad eum congregatos ejus.]
      EN: The Lord God, who gathers the dispersed of Israel, says: I will yet gather his people to him.

56:9  LA: [Omnes bestiæ agri, venite ad devorandum, universæ bestiæ saltus.
      EN: All you beasts of the field, come to devour, all you beasts of the forest.

56:10  LA: Speculatores ejus cæci omnes ; nescierunt universi : canes muti non valentes latrare, videntes vana, dormientes, et amantes somnia.
       EN: His watchmen are all blind; none has knowledge. They are mute dogs unable to bark, seeing empty visions, sleeping, and loving dreams.

56:11  LA: Et canes imprudentissimi nescierunt saturitatem ; ipsi pastores ignoraverunt intelligentiam : omnes in viam suam declinaverunt ; unusquisque ad avaritiam suam, a summo usque ad novissimum.
       EN: These utterly senseless dogs have never known satisfaction. The shepherds themselves have lacked understanding; all have turned to their own way, each to his greed, from the highest to the lowest.

ISAIAH 57

57:1  LA: Justus perit, et non est qui recogitet in corde suo ; et viri misericordiæ colliguntur, quia non est qui intelligat : a facie enim malitiæ collectus est justus.
      EN: The just person perishes, and no one takes it to heart. Merciful people are gathered away, for no one understands; the just person has been gathered away from the presence of evil.

57:2  LA: Veniat pax ; requiescat in cubili suo qui ambulavit in directione sua.
      EN: Let peace come; let the one who walked uprightly rest upon his bed.

57:3  LA: Vos autem accedite huc, filii auguratricis, semen adulteri et fornicariæ.
      EN: But you, come here, children of a sorceress, offspring of an adulterer and a prostitute.

57:4  LA: Super quem lusistis ? super quem dilatastis os, et ejecistis linguam ? Numquid non vos filii scelesti, semen mendax,
      EN: At whom have you mocked? Against whom have you opened your mouth wide and stuck out your tongue? Are you not wicked children, a lying offspring,

57:5  LA: qui consolamini in diis subter omne lignum frondosum, immolantes parvulos in torrentibus, subter eminentes petras ?
      EN: who take comfort in gods beneath every leafy tree, sacrificing little children in the streams beneath overhanging rocks?

57:6  LA: In partibus torrentis pars tua ; hæc est sors tua : et ipsis effudisti libamen, obtulisti sacrificium. Numquid super his non indignabor ?
      EN: Your portion is among the portions of the stream; these are your lot. To them you have poured a libation and offered sacrifice. Shall I not be indignant at these things?

57:7  LA: Super montem excelsum et sublimem posuisti cubile tuum, et illuc ascendisti ut immolares hostias.
      EN: Upon a high and lofty mountain you have set your bed, and there you went up to offer sacrifices.

57:8  LA: Et post ostium, et retro postem, posuisti memoriale tuum. Quia juxta me discooperuisti, et suscepisti adulterum, dilatasti cubile tuum, et pepigisti cum eis fœdus ; dilexisti stratum eorum manu aperta.
      EN: Behind the door and behind the doorpost you have placed your memorial. For beside Me you uncovered yourself and received an adulterer; you widened your bed and made a covenant with them. You loved their bed with an open hand.

57:9  LA: Et ornasti te regi unguento, et multiplicasti pigmenta tua. Misisti legatos tuos procul, et humiliata es usque ad inferos.
      EN: You adorned yourself for the king with ointment and multiplied your perfumes. You sent your envoys far away and humbled yourself as far as the underworld.

57:10  LA: In multitudine viæ tuæ laborasti ; non dixisti : Quiescam. Vitam manus tuæ invenisti ; propterea non rogasti.
       EN: You grew weary with your many journeys, but did not say, “I will rest.” You found life in your own hand; therefore you did not ask for help.

57:11  LA: Pro quo sollicita timuisti, quia mentita es, et mei non es recordata, neque cogitasti in corde tuo ? Quia ego tacens et quasi non videns, et mei oblita es.
       EN: Whom did you anxiously fear, that you lied and did not remember Me or take Me to heart? Because I was silent and as though not seeing, you forgot Me.

57:12  LA: Ego annuntiabo justitiam tuam, et opera tua non proderunt tibi.
       EN: I will declare your righteousness, and your works will not profit you.

57:13  LA: Cum clamaveris, liberent te congregati tui, et omnes eos auferet ventus, tollet aura.] [Qui autem fiduciam habet mei, hæreditabit terram, et possidebit montem sanctum meum.
       EN: When you cry out, let those you have gathered deliver you. The wind will carry them all away; a breeze will lift them off. But the one who trusts in Me will inherit the land and possess My holy mountain.

57:14  LA: Et dicam : Viam facite, præbete iter ; declinate de semita, auferte offendicula de via populi mei.
       EN: I will say, “Make a road, provide a way. Turn aside from the path; remove stumbling blocks from My people's road.”

57:15  LA: Quia hæc dicit Excelsus, et Sublimis, habitans æternitatem, et sanctum nomen ejus : in excelso et in sancto habitans, et cum contrito et humili spiritu : ut vivificet spiritum humilium, et vivificet cor contritorum.
       EN: For thus says the High and Exalted One, who inhabits eternity and whose name is holy, who dwells in a high and holy place, and with the contrite and humble in spirit, to give life to the spirit of the humble and the heart of the contrite:

57:16  LA: Non enim in sempiternum litigabo, neque usque ad finem irascar, quia spiritus a facie mea egredietur, et flatus ego faciam.
       EN: I will not contend forever or be angry to the end, for spirit will go forth from My presence, and I will make the breath of life.

57:17  LA: Propter iniquitatem avaritiæ ejus iratus sum, et percussi eum. Abscondi a te faciem meam, et indignatus sum ; et abiit vagus in via cordis sui.
